## I / I

### Beginning of the Wild-Goose Chase / 野鹅追踪之始

===Original===
It all started one afternoon, early in May, when I came out of the House of Commons with Tommy Deloraine. I had got in by an accident at a by-election, when I was supposed to be fighting a forlorn hope, and as I was just beginning to be busy at the Bar I found my hands pretty full. It was before Tommy succeeded, in the days when he sat for the family seat in Yorkshire, and that afternoon he was in a powerful bad temper. Out of doors it was jolly spring weather, there was greenery in Parliament Square and bits of gay colour, and a light wind was blowing up from the river. Inside a dull debate was winding on, and an advertising member had been trying to get up a row with the Speaker. The contrast between the frowsy place and the cheerful world outside would have impressed even the soul of a Government Whip.

===Chinese===
这一切始自五月上旬的一个下午，那时我和汤米·德洛兰（Tommy Deloraine）一同走出下议院。我是靠一次补选中的意外才得以进身的——本是被派去打一场毫无希望的硬仗——而由于我在律师界（the Bar）的事务才刚刚繁忙起来，我发觉自己实在分身乏术。那时汤米尚未发迹，他还坐着自家在约克郡（Yorkshire）的世袭议席，那天下午他正一肚子邪火。门外是明媚的春光，议会广场（Parliament Square）里绿意盎然，点缀着斑驳的鲜色，一阵轻风从河面上吹来。门内却是一场沉闷乏味的辩论没完没了，还有个爱出风头的议员刚跟议长大闹了一场。这霉气熏天的所在与门外那欢快世界之间的反差，纵然是一个政府督导（Government Whip）的灵魂见了，也要为之一动。

===Original===
Tommy sniffed the spring breeze like a supercilious stag.

“This about finishes me,” he groaned. “What a juggins I am to be mouldering here! Joggleberry is the celestial limit, what they call in happier lands the pink penultimate. And the frowst on those back benches! Was there ever such a moth-eaten old museum?”

“It is the Mother of Parliaments,” I observed.

“Damned monkey-house,” said Tommy. “I must get off for a bit, or I’ll bonnet Joggleberry or get up and propose a national monument to Guy Fawkes, or something silly.”

===Chinese===
汤米吸着那阵春风，活像一头神气高傲的牡鹿。

“这下我可算是完了，”他呻吟道。“我居然赖在这儿发霉，真是个大傻瓜！乔格尔贝里（Joggleberry）简直到了登峰造极的地步，换在好些的国度里，这号人就叫倒数第二的极品。还有后排长椅上那股子闷人的馊味儿！天底下还有这么个老掉牙、霉得掉渣的博物馆么？”

“这儿可是议会之母（Mother of Parliaments），”我评论道。

“该死的猴笼子，”汤米说。“我非得出去透透气不可，要不然我非得去把乔格尔贝里捉弄一番，要不就站起来提议给盖伊·福克斯（Guy Fawkes）立一座国家纪念碑，或者干出点什么蠢事来。”

===Original===
I did not see him for a day or two, and then one morning he rang me up and peremptorily summoned me to dine with him. I went, knowing very well what I should find. Tommy was off next day to shoot lions on the Equator, or something equally unconscientious. He was a bad acquaintance for a placid sedentary soul like me, for though he could work like a Trojan when the fit took him, he was never at the same job very long. In the same week he would harass an Under Secretary about horses for the Army, write voluminously to the press about a gun he had invented for potting aeroplanes, give a fancy-dress ball which he forgot to attend, and get into the semifinal of the racquets championship. I waited daily to see him start a new religion.

===Chinese===
有一两天我没见到他，后来某天早晨他打来电话，不容分说地召我去与他共进晚餐。我去了，心里很清楚等着我的会是什么。汤米第二天就要动身去赤道上猎狮，或者干点什么同样不务正业的勾当。他这种人，对我这般安分守己、伏案度日的人来说可不是个好搭子；因为虽说兴致一来他能像特洛伊人那般拼命干活，可同一桩事他从来干不长。同在一周之内，他会为了骑兵用的马匹去纠缠一位副国务大臣（Under Secretary），又会就他发明的一尊用来打飞机的炮给报界洋洋洒洒写上一通，再办一场化装舞会却忘了自己出席，还能一路打进墙球锦标赛的半决赛。我每天都在等着看他创立一种新宗教。

===Original===
That night, I recollect, he had an odd assortment of guests. A Cabinet Minister was there, a gentle being for whom Tommy professed public scorn and private affection; a sailor; an Indian cavalry fellow; Chapman, the Labour member, whom Tommy called Chipmunk; myself, and old Milson of the Treasury. Our host was in tremendous form, chaffing everybody, and sending Chipmunk into great rolling gusts of merriment. The two lived adjacent in Yorkshire, and on platforms abused each other like pickpockets.

===Chinese===
我记得，那天晚上他那一桌客人格外杂。到场的有一位内阁大臣——汤米当众对他颇为不齿、私下却满心喜爱的一位温文君子；一位海军军官；一个印度骑兵团的小伙子；工党议员查普曼（Chapman），汤米管他叫“花栗鼠”；还有我，以及财政部的老米尔森（Milson）。主人兴致高极了，拿众人一一打趣，把“花栗鼠”逗得前仰后合、笑声一阵接一阵。这俩人在约克郡比邻而居，到了讲台上却把对方骂得如同扒手一般。

===Original===
Tommy enlarged on the misfits of civilised life. He maintained that none of us, except perhaps the sailor and the cavalryman, were at our proper job. He would have had Wytham﻿—that was the Minister﻿—a cardinal of the Roman Church, and he said that Milson should have been the Warden of a college full of port and prejudice. Me he was kind enough to allocate to some reconstructed Imperial General Staff, merely because I had a craze for military history. Tommy’s perception did not go very deep. He told Chapman he should have been a lumberman in California. “You’d have made an uncommon good logger, Chipmunk, and you know you’re a dashed bad politician.”

===Chinese===
汤米大发议论，历数文明人生活里种种错位之处。他坚持说，在座的除了那海军军官和骑兵小伙，没一个是干着自己本分差事的。他恨不得把怀瑟姆（Wytham）——也就是那位大臣——送去当罗马教会的红衣主教，又说米尔森本该去做一所学院的院长，在葡萄酒与偏见之中终老。至于我，他倒是厚爱，把我安插到某个重新组建的帝国总参谋部（Imperial General Staff）里去，理由不过是我对军事史有几分痴迷。汤米的洞察力其实并不怎么深刻。他告诉查普曼，查普曼本该去加利福尼亚当个伐木工。“你会是个呱呱叫的好伐木工，花栗鼠，你自己也知道，你干政治可是糟透了。”

===Original===
When questioned about himself he became reticent, as the newspapers say. “I doubt if I’m much good at any job,” he confessed, “except to ginger up my friends. Anyhow, I’m getting out of this hole. Paired for the rest of the session with a chap who has lockjaw. I’m off to stretch my legs and get back my sense of proportion.”

===Chinese===
待到问及他自己，他便像报上说的那样“讳莫如深”起来。“我看哪桩差事我也干不大好，”他坦白道，“除了给朋友们打打气。不管怎样，我总算要钻出这个窟窿了。剩下这会期我跟一个患了破伤风的伙计配成对子（彼此代为请假缺席）。我要出去伸伸腿脚，把分寸感找回来。”

===Original===
Someone asked him where he was going, and was told “Venezuela, to buy Government bonds and look for birds’ nests.”

Nobody took Tommy seriously, so his guests did not trouble to bid him the kind of farewell a prolonged journey would demand. But when the others had gone, and we were sitting in the little back smoking-room on the first floor, he became solemn. Portentously solemn, for he wrinkled up his brows and dropped his jaw in the way he had when he fancied he was in earnest.

===Chinese===
有人问他上哪儿去，得到的回答是：“委内瑞拉，去买政府债券，顺便找找鸟窝。”

没人把汤米的话当真，所以客人们也懒得像送他出远门那样郑重道别。可等旁人都走了，只剩我们俩坐在二楼那间小小的后吸烟室里时，他忽然郑重起来。郑重得煞有介事——他皱起眉头、耷拉着下巴，那是他自以为一本正经时特有的模样。

===Original===
“I’ve taken on a queer job, Leithen,” he said, “and I want you to hear about it. None of my family know, and I would like to leave someone behind me who could get on to my tracks if things got troublesome.”

===Chinese===
“我揽了桩古怪的差事，利思滕，”他说，“我想让你听听是怎么回事。我家里谁都不知道，我想身后好歹留个人，万一事情闹得棘手了，能顺着我的踪迹找上来。”

===Original===
I braced myself for some preposterous confidence, for I was experienced in Tommy’s vagaries. But I own to being surprised when he asked me if I remembered Pitt-Heron.

I remembered Pitt-Heron very well. He had been at Oxford with me, but he was no great friend of mine, though for about two years Tommy and he had been inseparable. He had had a prodigious reputation for cleverness with everybody but the college authorities, and used to spend his vacations doing mad things in the Alps and the Balkans and writing about them in the halfpenny press. He was enormously rich﻿—cotton mills and Liverpool ground rents﻿—and, being without a father, did pretty much what his fantastic taste dictated. He was rather a hero for a bit after he came down, for he had made some wild journey in the neighbourhood of Afghanistan and written an exciting book about it.

===Chinese===
我打起精神，准备听他吐露什么荒唐的秘密，因为对汤米那些异想天开的做派我早已见怪不怪。可当他问我是否还记得皮特-赫伦（Pitt-Heron）时，我得承认，我还是吃了一惊。

皮特-赫伦我记得很清楚。他跟我是牛津（Oxford）的同学，不过跟我交情不深，倒是有大约两年的光景，他和汤米形影不离。除了学院当局之外，人人都惊叹他绝顶聪明；每逢假期，他便去阿尔卑斯山（the Alps）和巴尔干半岛（the Balkans）干些疯疯癫癫的事，再给那些半便士一份的小报写文章记述一番。他富得流油——名下有棉纺厂和利物浦（Liverpool）的地租——加之早年丧父，便由着性子，怎么离奇怎么来。他毕业之后那一阵子倒颇有几分英雄名声，因为他曾到阿富汗（Afghanistan）一带做过一次荒唐的远行，还就此写了一本引人入胜的书。

===Original===
Then he married a pretty cousin of Tommy’s, who happened to be the only person that ever captured my stony heart, and settled down in London. I did not go to their house, and soon I found that very few of his friends saw much of him, either. His travels and magazine articles suddenly stopped, and I put it down to the common course of successful domesticity. Apparently I was wrong.

===Chinese===
后来他娶了汤米一位漂亮的表妹——偏巧她也是有史以来唯一俘获过我那颗冥顽之心的人——随后便在伦敦安顿下来。我没上他们家走动，不久我又发觉，他的朋友里极少有人还能常见到他。他的游记和杂志文章戛然而止，我把这归之于美满家庭生活的寻常轨迹。如今看来，我显然想错了。

===Original===
“Charles Pitt-Heron,” said Tommy, “is blowing up for a most thundering mess.”

I asked what kind of mess, and Tommy said he didn’t know. “That’s the mischief of it. You remember the wild beggar he used to be, always off on the spree to the Mountains of the Moon, or somewhere. Well, he has been damping down his fires lately and trying to behave like a respectable citizen, but God knows what he has been thinking! I go a good deal to Portman Square, and all last year he has been getting queerer.”

===Chinese===
“查尔斯·皮特-赫伦，”汤米说，“正酿着一场惊天大祸。”

我问他什么大祸，汤米说他也不知道。“糟就糟在这儿。你总记得他从前那副荒唐德行，动不动就跑到月亮山脉（Mountains of the Moon），要不就别的什么鬼地方去撒野。近来他倒是一直压着火，想做个本分的体面人，可天知道他心里都在盘算些什么！我常去波特曼广场（Portman Square），整整一年下来，他这人越来越古怪了。”

===Original===
Questions as to the nature of the queerness only elicited the fact that Pitt-Heron had taken to science with some enthusiasm.

“He has got a laboratory at the back of the house﻿—used to be the billiard-room﻿—where he works away half the night. And Lord! The crew you meet there! Every kind of heathen﻿—Chinese and Turks, and long-haired chaps from Russia, and fat Germans. I’ve several times blundered into the push. They’ve all got an odd secretive air about them, and Charlie is becoming like them. He won’t answer a plain question or look you straight in the face. Ethel sees it, too, and she has often talked to me about it.”

===Chinese===
我再追问那古怪究竟是怎样的古怪，问来问去只问出一件事：皮特-赫伦迷上了科学，而且热衷得很。

“他在宅子后头弄了个实验室——原本是台球房——在那儿一干就是大半夜。老天！你在那儿遇见的那帮人！形形色色的异教徒——中国人、土耳其人、俄国来的长头发家伙，还有胖胖的德国人。我有好几回一头撞进那伙人堆里。他们个个身上都透着股说不出的诡秘劲儿，查尔斯也变得跟他们一个样了。你问他句正经话，他不答；也不肯正眼瞧你。埃塞尔（Ethel）也看出来了，她常跟我念叨这事。”

===Original===
I said I saw no harm in such a hobby.

“I do,” said Tommy grimly. “Anyhow, the fellow has bolted.”

“What on earth﻿—” I began, but was cut short.

===Chinese===
我说我看不出这种嗜好有什么坏处。

“我倒看出来了，”汤米阴沉地说。“不管怎样，那家伙溜了。”

“到底怎么——”我才开口，就被他截断了。

===Original===
“Bolted without a word to a mortal soul. He told Ethel he would be home for luncheon yesterday, and never came. His man knew nothing about him, hadn’t packed for him, or anything; but he found he had stuffed some things into a kit-bag and gone out by the back through the mews. Ethel was in terrible straits, and sent for me, and I ranged all yesterday afternoon like a wolf on the scent. I found he had drawn a biggish sum in gold from the bank, but I couldn’t find any trace of where he had gone.

===Chinese===
“一声不响地溜了，没跟任何一个活人透半句口风。他昨天告诉埃塞尔午饭前回家，却再没露面。他的贴身男仆对他去向一无所知，也没替他收拾行李，什么都没有；后来才发现他胡乱往一只旅行包里塞了些东西，从后门穿过马厩巷溜了出去。埃塞尔急得走投无路，把我叫了去，我整个昨天下午像头寻着气味的狼似的满处乱转。我查出他从银行提走了一笔数目不小的金币，可他到底上哪儿去了，我连半点踪迹也找不到。

===Original===
“I was just setting out for Scotland Yard this morning, when Tomlin, the valet, rang me up and said he had found a card in the waistcoat of the dress clothes that Charles had worn the night before he left. It had a name on it like Konalevsky, and it struck me that they might know something about the business at the Russian Embassy. Well, I went round there, and the long and short of it was that I found there was a fellow of that name among the clerks. I saw him, and he said he had gone to see Mr. Pitt-Heron two days before with a letter from some Embassy chap. Unfortunately, the man in question had gone off to New York next day, but Konalevsky told me one thing which helped to clear up matters. It seemed that the letter had been one of those passports that Embassies give to their friends﻿—a higher-powered sort than the ordinary make﻿—and Konalevsky gathered from something he had heard that Charles was aiming for Moscow.”

===Chinese===
“今天早上我正要动身去苏格兰场（Scotland Yard），那贴身男仆汤姆林（Tomlin）却打来电话，说他在查尔斯出走前一夜所穿礼服的马甲里翻出了一张名片。上面的名字像是科纳列夫斯基（Konalevsky），我便琢磨着，俄罗斯大使馆那边说不定知道点内情。于是我便跑了一趟，长话短说，我在那儿的职员里头果然找到一个同名同姓的家伙。我见了他，他说两天前他曾带着某位使馆同僚的一封信去拜访皮特-赫伦先生。不巧的是，那位托信的先生第二天就动身去了纽约（New York）；不过科纳列夫斯基倒是告诉我一桩事，帮着把事情理出了头绪。原来那封信是使馆发给自家友人的一种护照——比寻常的护照权力更大、通行更广——科纳列夫斯基从风闻里听出，查尔斯此行的目的地是莫斯科（Moscow）。”

===Original===
Tommy paused to let his news sink in.

“Well, that was good enough for me. I’m off tomorrow to run him to ground.”

===Chinese===
汤米顿了顿，好让这消息在我心里沉淀下来。

“行，这对我已经够了。我明天就动身，非把他逮回来不可。”

===Original===
“But why shouldn’t a man go to Moscow if he wants?” I said feebly.

“You don’t understand,” said the sage Tommy. “You don’t know old Charles as I know him. He’s got into a queer set, and there’s no knowing what mischief he’s up to. He’s perfectly capable of starting a revolution in Armenia or somewhere merely to see how it feels like to be a revolutionary. That’s the damned thing about the artistic temperament. Anyhow, he’s got to chuck it. I won’t have Ethel scared to death by his whims. I am going to hale him back from Moscow, even if I have to pretend he’s an escaped lunatic. He’s probably like enough one by this time if he has taken no clothes.”

===Chinese===
“可一个人想上莫斯科，凭什么去不得呢？”我有气无力地说。

“你不懂，”汤米一副先知先觉的口吻。“你不像我那样了解老查尔斯。他陷进了一帮古怪人里头，天晓得他在捣什么鬼。他这种人，完全有可能跑到亚美尼亚（Armenia）或别的什么地方去发动一场革命，纯粹是为了尝尝当革命党是什么滋味。这就是艺术家气质那要命的地方。反正，他非罢手不可。我绝不容许埃塞尔被他这些怪念头活活吓死。我要把他从莫斯科硬拖回来，哪怕我得假称他是个逃出来的疯子。再说了，要是他当真一件换洗衣物也没带，这会儿他说不定真跟疯子差不离了。”

===Original===
I have forgotten what I said, but it was some plea for caution. I could not see the reason for these heroics. Pitt-Heron did not interest me greatly, and the notion of Tommy as a defender of the hearth amused me. I thought that he was working on very slight evidence and would probably make a fool of himself.

“It’s only another of the man’s fads,” I said. “He never could do things like an ordinary mortal. What possible trouble could there be? Money?”

===Chinese===
我忘了自己当时说了些什么，总之是劝他谨慎些。我看不出有什么必要摆出这般壮烈的架势。皮特-赫伦并不怎么勾起我的兴趣，而汤米充任家庭守护者这念头倒让我觉得好笑。我心想他所凭的不过是一星半点的蛛丝马迹，多半要闹个自讨没趣。

“这无非又是那人的一时兴之所至罢了，”我说。“他向来不能像个正经人那样做事。能出什么乱子呢？钱财？”

===Original===
“Rich as Croesus,” said Tommy.

“A woman?”

“Blind as a bat to female beauty.”

“The wrong side of the law?”

“Don’t think so. He could settle any ordinary scrape with a cheque.”

“Then I give it up. Whatever it is it looks as if Pitt-Heron would have a companion in misfortune before you are done with the business. I’m all for your taking a holiday, for at present you are a nuisance to your friends and a disgrace to your country’s legislature. But for goodness’ sake curb your passion for romance. They don’t like it in Russia.”

===Chinese===
“富得跟克罗伊斯（Croesus）似的，”汤米说。

“女人？”

“对女人的美貌，他瞎得跟蝙蝠一样。”

“犯了法？”

“不像。寻常的麻烦，他开张支票就能摆平。”

“那我可就猜不着了。不管是什么，照这情形看，等你这趟差事收场之前，皮特-赫伦怕是要多一个同病相怜的伴儿了。你去度个假我是再赞成不过，因为眼下的你，对朋友是个麻烦，对你国家的立法机构是个耻辱。可看在老天的份上，把你那股子浪漫的热情收一收吧。在俄国，人家可不吃这一套。”

===Original===
Next morning Tommy turned up to see me in Chambers. The prospect of travel always went to his head like wine. He was in wild spirits, and had forgotten his anger at the defaulting Pitt-Heron in gratitude for his provision of an occupation. He talked of carrying him off to the Caucasus when he had found him, to investigate the habits of the Caucasian stag.

I remember the scene as if it were yesterday. It was a hot May morning, and the sun which came through the dusty window in Fountain Court lit up the dust and squalor of my working chambers. I was pretty busy at the time, and my table was well-nourished with briefs. Tommy picked up one and began to read it. It was about a new drainage scheme in West Ham. He tossed it down and looked at me pityingly.

“Poor old beggar!” he said. “To spend your days on such work when the world is chockfull of amusing things. Life goes roaring by and you only hear the echo in your stuffy rooms. You can hardly see the sun for the cobwebs on these windows of yours. Charles is a fool, but I’m blessed if he isn’t wiser than you. Don’t you wish you were coming with me?”

===Chinese===
第二天早上，汤米跑到我的事务所来看我。一想到出门远行，他总是像喝了酒似的精神上头。他快活得要命，因为皮特-赫伦这桩“开小差”的勾当反倒给了他一桩正经事做，他连那一腔火气都忘了。他说等找到了人，要把他掳到高加索（the Caucasus）去，考察一番高加索牡鹿的习性。

那番情景我至今记得，恍如昨日。那是个炎热的五月早晨，从喷泉庭院（Fountain Court）那扇蒙尘的窗子透进来的阳光，把我这间事务所里的尘垢与寒碜照得一清二楚。我那时事务正忙，案头堆满了讼案摘要。汤米顺手拿起一份翻看起来。那桩案子讲的是西汉姆（West Ham）一项新的排水工程。他把它一扔，满眼怜悯地望着我。

“可怜的老家伙！”他说。“外头大千世界里有趣的事多得塞不下，你倒好，把日子全耗在这种活计上。生活在身边轰隆隆地飞驰而过，你却只在你那几间闷屋子里听见点回声。你这些窗子结满了蛛网，连太阳都快瞧不见了。查尔斯是个傻瓜，可他要是没比你聪明，那才见鬼了。你就不想跟我一块儿去？”

===Original===
The queer thing was that I did. I remember the occasion, as I have said, for it was one of the few on which I have had a pang of dissatisfaction with the calling I had chosen. As Tommy’s footsteps grew faint on the stairs I suddenly felt as if I were missing something, as if somehow I were out of it. It is an unpleasant feeling, even when you know that the thing you are out of is foolishness.

===Chinese===
奇怪的是，我还当真想。我说过，我记得那个时刻——那是我少有的几回，对自家选定的营生涌起一阵不满足的隐痛。汤米的脚步声在楼梯上渐渐远去，我忽然觉得自己仿佛错过了什么，仿佛某种意义上，我把自己隔绝在了局外。这是一种叫人不大痛快的感觉，哪怕你明知你所错过的那桩事不过是桩蠢事。

===Original===
Tommy went off at 11 from Victoria, and my work was pretty well ruined for the day. I felt oddly restless, and the cause was not merely Tommy’s departure. My thoughts kept turning to the Pitt-Herons﻿—chiefly to Ethel, that adorable child unequally yoked to a perverse egoist, but a good deal to the egoist himself. I have never suffered much from whimsies, but I suddenly began to feel a curious interest in the business, an unwilling interest, for I found it in my heart to regret my robust scepticism of the night before. And it was more than interest. I had a sort of presentiment that I was going to be mixed up in the affair more than I wanted. I told myself angrily that the life of an industrious common-law barrister could have little to do with the wanderings of two maniacs in Muscovy. But, try as I might, I could not get rid of the obsession. That night it followed me into my dreams, and I saw myself with a knout coercing Tommy and Pitt-Heron in a Russian fortress which faded away into the Carlton Hotel.

===Chinese===
汤米十一点从维多利亚车站（Victoria）动身走了，我这一天的活计也基本算是毁了。我心头有一种说不出的烦躁，其因由并不单单是汤米的离去。我的念头一个劲儿地往皮特-赫伦一家身上转——主要是埃塞尔，那个委身于一个任性利己主义者、实在不甚般配的可爱人儿，可也大半落在那个利己主义者本人身上。我向来不大为各种奇想所扰，可我竟忽然对这件事生出一种古怪的兴趣来——一种身不由己的兴趣，因为我发觉自己心里竟开始懊悔昨夜那副坚定的怀疑腔调了。而且这还不仅仅是兴趣。我隐隐有种预感：我在这桩事里要卷得比我情愿的更深。我恼怒地对自己说，一个勤勉的普通法出庭律师的日子，跟两个疯子在莫斯科公国（Muscovy）那边的游荡能有什么相干。可无论我怎么挣扎，都甩不掉这桩心事。那天夜里它一路跟进了我的梦乡——我梦见自己手持皮鞭，在一座俄罗斯要塞里驱遣着汤米和皮特-赫伦，而那座要塞却渐渐化作了卡尔顿饭店（Carlton Hotel）。

===Original===
Next afternoon I found my steps wending in the direction of Portman Square. I lived at the time in Down Street, and I told myself I would be none the worse of a walk in the Park before dinner. I had a fancy to see Mrs. Pitt-Heron, for, though I had only met her twice since her marriage, there had been a day when we were the closest of friends.

I found her alone, a perplexed and saddened lady with imploring eyes. Those eyes questioned me as to how much I knew. I told her presently that I had seen Tommy and was aware of his errand. I was moved to add that she might count on me if there were anything she wished done on this side of the Channel.

She was very little changed. There was still the old exquisite slimness, the old shy courtesy. But she told me nothing. Charles was full of business and becoming very forgetful. She was sure the Russian journey was all a stupid mistake. He probably thought he had told her of his departure. He would write; she expected a letter by every post.

===Chinese===
第二天下午，我发觉自己的脚步正不自觉地往波特曼广场那边去。那时我住在唐街（Down Street），我便告诉自己，晚饭前到公园里散散步于我也无妨。我很想见见皮特-赫伦太太，因为虽说自她出嫁之后我只见过她两面，可曾经有那么一段日子，我们是最要好的朋友。

我见到她独自一人——一位满面困惑与愁苦的夫人，眼中带着恳求。那双眼睛在盘问我究竟知道多少。过了一会儿，我告诉她我见过汤米，也知道他此行的使命。我一时动情，又补了一句：海峡这一边若有什么她想办的事，她尽可以指望我。

她几乎没怎么变。还是从前那分动人的纤瘦，还是从前那份羞怯的温婉。可她什么也没告诉我。她说查尔斯事务繁忙，记性也大不如前。她确信这趟俄国之行全是一场愚蠢的误会。他大概以为自己已经把动身的事告诉她了。他会写信的；她每一班邮差都盼着一封信。

===Original===
But her haggard eyes belied her optimism. I could see that there had been odd happenings of late in the Pitt-Heron household. She either knew or feared something﻿—the latter, I thought, for her air was more of apprehension than of painful enlightenment.

I did not stay long, and, as I walked home, I had an awkward feeling that I had intruded. Also I was increasingly certain that there was trouble brewing, and that Tommy had more warrant for his journey than I had given him credit for. I cast my mind back to gather recollections of Pitt-Heron, but all I could find was an impression of a brilliant uncomfortable being, who had been too fond of the byways of life for my sober tastes. There was nothing crooked in him in the wrong sense, but there might be a good deal that was perverse. I remember consoling myself with the thought that, though he might shatter his wife’s nerves by his vagaries, he would scarcely break her heart.

To be watchful, I decided, was my business. And I could not get rid of the feeling that I might soon have cause for all my vigilance.

===Chinese===
可她那双憔悴的眼睛却戳穿了她那番乐观。我看得出来，近来皮特-赫伦家中定是出了些蹊跷的事。她要么知道些什么，要么在害怕什么——我看是后者，因为她那神情更像忧惧，而非出于痛苦的知情。

我没久坐。回家的路上，我心里有种不自在的感觉，觉得自己是不请自来的闯入者。同时我也越来越确信，麻烦正在酝酿，汤米这趟远行比我先前以为的更有道理。我回溯记忆，想拼凑出关于皮特-赫伦的印象，可我能找到的无非是这样一笔：一个才华横溢却叫人不自在的人物，太爱生活里那些冷僻的旁门左道，不合我这般持重的口味。他身上并没有什么真正邪门歪道的地方，但乖张之处可能不少。我记得我还这样宽慰自己：他那些胡作非为纵然会震碎妻子的神经，到底还不至于伤透她的心。

我认定，自己的本分便是留心守望。而我怎么也甩不掉那种感觉：也许用不了多久，我这番警觉便要派上用场了。

## II / II

### I First Hear of Mr. Andrew Lumley / 我初闻安德鲁·拉姆利先生

===Original===
A fortnight later—to be accurate, on the 21st of May—I did a thing I rarely do, and went down to South London on a County Court case. It was an ordinary taxicab accident, and, as the solicitors for the company were good clients of mine, and the regular county-court junior was ill in bed, I took the case to oblige them. There was the usual dull conflict of evidence. An empty taxicab, proceeding slowly on the right side of the road and hooting decorously at the corners, had been run into by a private motorcar, which had darted down a side street. The taxi had been swung round and its bonnet considerably damaged, while its driver had suffered a dislocated shoulder. The bad feature in the case was that the motorcar had not halted to investigate the damage, but had proceeded unconscientiously on its way, and the assistance of the London police had been called in to trace it. It turned out to be the property of a Mr. Julius Pavia, a retired East India merchant, who lived in a large villa in the neighbourhood of Blackheath, and at the time of the accident it had been occupied by his butler. The company brought an action for damages against its owner.

===Chinese===
两星期之后——确切地说是五月二十一日——我做了一件我极少做的事：南下伦敦南区，去办一桩郡法院（County Court）的案子。那是一起寻常的出租汽车事故。由于该公司的代理律师是我的老主顾，而那位固定的郡法院初级律师又卧病在床，我便接下这桩案子，算是帮他们一个忙。案情不出所料，是那种乏味的各执一词。一辆空载的出租汽车在马路右侧缓缓行驶，拐角处规规矩矩地按着喇叭，却被一辆从小巷里窜出来的私人汽车撞了个正着。出租车被撞得打了个旋儿，车头损毁得不轻，司机也肩关节脱了臼。案中最恶劣的一节是：那辆私人汽车肇事后竟没有停车查看损情，反倒不管不顾地扬长而去，于是只好请伦敦警方协助追缉。后来查明，车主是一位朱利厄斯·帕维亚（Julius Pavia）先生，退休的东印度商人，住在布莱克希思（Blackheath）附近的一幢大宅子里；出事时车是由他的管家驾驶的。出租汽车公司遂向车主提起了损害赔偿之诉。

===Original===
The butler, Tuke, by name, was the only witness for the defence. He was a tall man, with a very long, thin face, and a jaw the two parts of which seemed scarcely to fit. He was profuse in his apologies on behalf of his master, who was abroad. It seemed that on the morning in question—it was the 8th of May—he had received instructions from Mr. Pavia to convey a message to a passenger by the Continental express from Victoria, and had been hot on this errand when he met the taxi. He was not aware that there had been any damage, thought it only a slight grazing of the two cars, and on his master’s behalf consented to the judgment of the court.

===Chinese===
被告方唯一的证人，便是那位管家，名叫图克（Tuke）。他是个高个子，脸庞又长又瘦，下巴的两半仿佛不大合得拢。他替身在国外的主人连声致歉。据他说，出事那天早晨——也就是五月八日——他奉帕维亚先生之命，去给一位搭乘维多利亚车站（Victoria）欧洲大陆快车的旅客传句话，正火急火燎地赶着这趟差使，便撞上了那辆出租车。他压根儿不知道撞出了什么损伤，以为不过是两车轻微擦蹭，便代表主人接受法庭的判决。

===Original===
It was a commonplace business, but Tuke was by no means a commonplace witness. He was very unlike the conventional butler, much liker one of those successful financiers whose portraits you see in the picture papers. His little eyes were quick with intelligence, and there were lines of ruthlessness around his mouth, like those of a man often called to decisive action. His story was simplicity itself, and he answered my questions with an air of serious candour. The train he had to meet was the 11 a.m. from Victoria, the train by which Tommy had travelled. The passenger he had to see was an American gentleman, Mr. Wright Davies. His master, Mr. Pavia, was in Italy, but would shortly be home again.

The case was over in twenty minutes, but it was something unique in my professional experience. For I took a most intense and unreasoning dislike to that bland butler. I cross-examined with some rudeness, was answered with steady courtesy, and hopelessly snubbed. The upshot was that I lost my temper, to the surprise of the County Court judge. All the way back I was both angry and ashamed of myself. Halfway home I realised that the accident had happened on the very day that Tommy left London. The coincidence merely flickered across my mind, for there could be no earthly connection between the two events.

===Chinese===
这本是一桩司空见惯的案子，可图克其人却绝非一个司空见惯的证人。他与人们印象中那种千篇一律的管家全然不同，倒更像你在画报上见过的那种功成名就的金融家。他那双小眼睛机敏地滴溜溜转，嘴角四周刻着几道冷酷无情的纹路，像是那种常常需要当机立断的人物。他的说辞简单至极，回答我的盘问时也带着一种郑重其事的坦率。他要去接的车，是上午十一点从维多利亚车站开出的那一班——正是汤米搭乘的那一趟。他要去见的旅客，是一位美国先生，赖特·戴维斯（Wright Davies）。他的主人帕维亚先生此刻身在意大利，不过不久便会回国。

案子二十分钟便审结了，但在我执业的经历中，这却是独一无二的一遭。因为我竟对那位和颜悦色的管家生出了一种极其强烈、毫无道理的反感。我盘问他时多少带了几分粗暴，得到的却是始终如一的客气应答，到头来结结实实地碰了一鼻子灰。结果我发了脾气，连那位郡法院的法官都颇为吃惊。回去的一路上，我又是恼怒，又是替自己惭愧。归途过半，我才猛然想起：那桩事故发生的日子，恰是汤米离开伦敦的那一天。这巧合在我脑中不过一闪而过罢了，因为这两件事之间，断不可能有任何干系。

===Original===
That afternoon I wasted some time in looking up Pavia in the directory. He was there sure enough, as the occupier of a suburban mansion called the White Lodge. He had no city address, so it was clear that he was out of business. My irritation with the man had made me inquisitive about the master. It was a curious name he bore, possibly Italian, possibly Goanese. I wondered how he got on with his highly competent butler. If Tuke had been my servant I would have wrung his neck or bolted before a week was out.

===Chinese===
那天下午，我浪费了些工夫在行名簿上查帕维亚。他果然赫然在列，是城郊一幢名叫“白屋”（the White Lodge）的宅邸的住户。他没有城里的办公地址，可见早已不在商界做事了。我对那管家的一腔恼火，倒勾起了我对他主人的好奇。他这个名字颇为古怪，像是意大利人，又像是果阿人。我纳闷他跟那位精明强干的管家相处得如何。换了图克是我的仆人，不出一个礼拜，不是拧断他的脖子，就是我自己拔腿跑路。

===Original===
Have you ever noticed that, when you hear a name that strikes you, you seem to be constantly hearing it for a bit. Once I had a case in which one of the parties was called Jubber, a name I had never met before, but I ran across two other Jubbers before the case was over. Anyhow, the day after the Blackheath visit I was briefed in a big Stock Exchange case, which turned on the true ownership of certain bearer bonds. It was a complicated business which I need not trouble you with, and it involved a number of consultations with my lay clients, a famous firm of brokers. They produced their books and my chambers were filled with glossy gentlemen talking a strange jargon.

===Chinese===
你可曾留意过：一旦某个名字引起了你的注意，此后一阵子你便似乎到处都能听见它。从前我经手过一桩案子，其中一方姓贾伯（Jubber），这姓我此前从未见过，可案子还没了结，我又接连碰上了另外两个贾伯。话说回来，去布莱克希思的第二天，我接下了一桩证券交易所（the Stock Exchange）的大案，案情关乎若干不记名债券（bearer bonds）的真正归属。这事错综复杂，不必在此赘述烦扰诸位，只是其中牵涉与我的当事人——一家赫赫有名的经纪行——多次磋商。他们搬来了账册，我的事务所里一时间挤满了衣着光鲜的先生们，满口说着一种古怪的行话。

===Original===
I had to examine my clients closely on their practice in treating a certain class of bearer security, and they were very frank in expounding their business. I was not surprised to hear that Pitt-Heron was one of the most valued names on their lists. With his wealth he was bound to be a good deal in the city. Now I had no desire to pry into Pitt-Heron’s private affairs, especially his financial arrangements, but his name was in my thoughts at the time, and I could not help looking curiously at what was put before me. He seemed to have been buying these bonds on a big scale. I had the indiscretion to ask if Mr. Pitt-Heron had long followed this course, and was told that he had begun to purchase some six months before.

===Chinese===
我不得不就他们处理某类不记名证券的惯例，细细盘问我的当事人，而他们阐述起自家的生意来也十分坦率。听说皮特-赫伦是他们名单上最受看重的客户之一，我并不觉得意外。以他的财力，在城里大量出入本是理所当然。我本无意窥探皮特-赫伦的私事，尤其不想打听他的财务安排，无奈他的名字那时正萦绕在我心头，我不由得怀着好奇审视摆在我面前的那些材料。看样子他一直在大规模购入这类债券。我一时多嘴，冒昧问了句皮特-赫伦先生做这桩买卖有多久了，得到的回答是：大约半年前开始购入的。

===Original===
“Mr. Pitt-Heron,” volunteered the stockbroker, “is very closely connected in his financial operations with another esteemed client of ours, Mr. Julius Pavia. They are both attracted by this class of security.”

At the moment I scarcely noted the name, but after dinner that night I began to speculate about the connection. I had found out the name of one of Charles’s mysterious new friends.

===Chinese===
“皮特-赫伦先生，”那位股票经纪人主动说道，“在他的财务运作上，与我们的另一位贵客过从甚密——朱利厄斯·帕维亚先生。他们二位都对这类证券情有独钟。”

当时我几乎没有留意这个名字，可那天晚上吃过晚饭，我便开始琢磨起这层关系来。我总算又查出了查尔斯那帮神秘新朋友中一人的姓名。

===Original===
It was not a very promising discovery. A retired East India merchant did not suggest anything wildly speculative, but I began to wonder if Charles’s preoccupation, to which Tommy had been witness, might not be connected with financial worries. I could not believe that the huge Pitt-Heron fortune had been seriously affected, or that his flight was that of a defaulter, but he might have got entangled in some shady city business which preyed on his sensitive soul. Somehow or other I could not believe that Mr. Pavia was a wholly innocent old gentleman; his butler looked too formidable. It was possible that he was blackmailing Pitt-Heron, and that the latter had departed to get out of his clutches.

But on what ground? I had no notion as to the blackmailable thing that might lurk in Charles’s past, and the guesses which flitted through my brain were too fantastic to consider seriously. After all, I had only the flimsiest basis for conjecture. Pavia and Pitt-Heron were friends; Tommy had gone off in quest of Pitt-Heron; Pavia’s butler had broken the law of the land in order, for some reason or other, to see the departure of the train by which Tommy had travelled. I remember laughing at myself for my suspicions, and reflecting that, if Tommy could see into my head, he would turn a deaf ear in the future to my complaints of his lack of balance.

===Chinese===
这个发现算不上多有指望。一个退休的东印度商人，听不出什么穷凶极恶的投机味道；但我转念又想，汤米所目睹的查尔斯那种魂不守舍的心不在焉，莫非与财务上的烦恼有关？我无法相信皮特-赫伦那庞大的家产会元气大伤，也不信他的出走是一个亏空公款者的潜逃，但他说不定卷进了城里某桩见不得光的勾当，正啃噬着他那颗敏感的心。不知怎的，我总觉得帕维亚先生不会是个全然清白无辜的老绅士——他那管家看起来实在太厉害了。说不定他在敲诈皮特-赫伦，而后者出走便是为了挣脱他的魔掌。

可凭什么敲诈呢？查尔斯的过去可能藏着什么足以授人以柄的把柄，我毫无头绪，而那些掠过我脑海的猜测又太过荒诞，不值得认真对待。说到底，我据以推测的根据薄得可怜：帕维亚和皮特-赫伦是朋友；汤米为寻皮特-赫伦而出走；帕维亚的管家为了某种缘故，不惜触犯国法，也要去目送汤米所乘那趟列车的驶离。我记得我曾在心里嘲笑自己的疑神疑鬼，心想汤米若能看穿我这脑中的念头，日后我再抱怨他行事失衡，他大可以充耳不闻了。

===Original===
But the thing stuck in my mind, and I called again that week on Mrs. Pitt-Heron. She had had no word from her husband, and only a bare line from Tommy, giving his Moscow address. Poor child, it was a wretched business for her. She had to keep a smiling face to the world, invent credible tales to account for her husband’s absence, and all the while anxiety and dread were gnawing at her heart. I asked her if she had ever met a Mr. Pavia, but the name was unknown to her. She knew nothing of Charles’s business dealings, but at my request she interviewed his bankers, and I heard from her next day that his affairs were in perfect order. It was no financial crisis which had precipitated him abroad.

===Chinese===
可这桩心事怎么也挥之不去，那一周我便又去拜访了皮特-赫伦太太。她没有收到丈夫半个字的消息，汤米也只寄来寥寥一行，给了个莫斯科（Moscow）的地址。可怜的人儿，这于她实在是一桩折磨人的事。她得在外人面前维持一张笑脸，编出些说得过去的说辞来解释丈夫的缺席，而忧惧与惶恐无时无刻不在啃噬她的心。我问她可曾见过一位帕维亚先生，她对这个名字全然陌生。她对查尔斯的生意往来一无所知，但应我的请求，她去见了他的银行家。第二天她告诉我，他的账目井井有条。把他逼出国门的，绝非一场财务危机。

===Original===
A few days later I stumbled by the merest accident upon what sailors call a “cross-bearing.” At the time I used to “devil” a little for the Solicitor-General, and “note” cases sent to him from the different Government offices. It was thankless work, but it was supposed to be good for an ambitious lawyer. By this prosaic channel I received the first hint of another of Charles’s friends.

===Chinese===
几天之后，我纯粹出于偶然，撞上了水手们所说的“交叉方位”（cross-bearing）——两条方位线一交叉，位置便定了下来。那时我时常替副检察长（Solicitor-General）打打下手，替他“做摘记”，处理各政府部门转来的案子。这差事吃力不讨好，可据说于一个有野心的律师不无裨益。偏偏就是经由这条枯燥乏味的渠道，我第一次得到了关于查尔斯另一位朋友的线索。

===Original===
I had sent me one day the papers dealing with the arrest of a German spy at Plymouth, for at the time there was a sort of epidemic of roving Teutons who got themselves into compromising situations, and gravely troubled the souls of the Admiralty and the War-Office. This case was distinguished from the common ruck by the higher social standing of the accused. Generally the spy is a photographer or bagman who attempts to win the bibulous confidence of minor officials. But this specimen was no less than a professor of a famous German University, a man of excellent manners, wide culture, and attractive presence, who had dined with Port officers and danced with Admirals’ daughters.

===Chinese===
有一天，有人转来了一宗在普利茅斯（Plymouth）逮捕德国间谍的案卷，因为那段时日里，四下流窜的条顿人犹如瘟疫一般，屡屡使自己陷入形迹可疑的境地，令海军部（the Admiralty）和陆军部（the War Office）的大人们大为头疼。这一桩案子之所以从那一干庸常之辈中脱颖而出，全在于被告社会地位之高。寻常的间谍，不是摄影师便是旅行推销员，千方百计地去套那些小职员酒酣耳热时的口风。可这一位却是堂堂一所著名德国大学的教授，举止优雅、学识渊博、风度翩翩，与港务军官们同席进餐，与海军上将的千金们翩翩起舞。

===Original===
I have forgotten the evidence or what was the legal point submitted for the Law Officers’ opinion; in any case it matters little, for he was acquitted. What interested me at the time was the testimonials as to character which he carried with him. He had many letters of introduction. One was from Pitt-Heron to his wife’s sailor uncle; and when he was arrested one Englishman went so far as to wire that he took upon himself the whole costs of the defence. This gentleman was a Mr. Andrew Lumley, stated in the papers sent me to be a rich bachelor, a member of the Athenaeum and Carlton Clubs, and a dweller in the Albany.

===Chinese===
证据是什么，或者呈交法律长官们裁定的法律争点何在，我已经忘了；反正也无甚要紧，因为他最终被判无罪。当时引起我兴味的，是他随身携带的那些品行推荐信。他握有许多介绍信。其中一封出自皮特-赫伦之手，是写给皮特-赫伦太太那位当海军的叔父的；而他被捕之后，有一位英国人竟然拍来电报，声明愿独自承担全部辩护费用。这位先生便是安德鲁·拉姆利（Andrew Lumley）先生——卷宗里注明他是一位富有的单身汉，雅典娜神庙俱乐部（the Athenaeum）和卡尔顿俱乐部（Carlton Club）的会员，寓所设在奥尔巴尼（the Albany）。

===Original===
Remember, that till a few weeks before I had known nothing of Pitt-Heron’s circle, and here were three bits of information dropping in on me unsolicited, just when my interest had been awakened. I began to get really keen, for every man at the bottom of his heart believes that he is a born detective. I was on the lookout for Charles’s infrequent friends, and I argued that if he knew the spy and the spy knew Mr. Lumley, the odds were that Pitt-Heron and Lumley were acquaintances. I hunted up the latter in the Red Book. Sure enough, he lived in the Albany, belonged to half a dozen clubs, and had a country house in Hampshire.

===Chinese===
须知，直到几星期之前，我对皮特-赫伦的交际圈子还一无所知，而如今，恰逢我的兴味被勾起，竟有三条消息不请自来地落到我头上。我开始真正上了心——毕竟每个男人骨子里都暗自相信自己天生是个侦探。我正留心查访查尔斯那寥寥无几的朋友，于是推想：既然他认识那间谍，而那间谍又认识拉姆利先生，那么皮特-赫伦与拉姆利相识的可能性便极大。我在红皮书（the Red Book）里查了查后者的底细。果不其然，他住在奥尔巴尼，加入了半打俱乐部，在汉普郡（Hampshire）还有一所乡间宅邸。

===Original===
I tucked the name away in a pigeonhole of my memory, and for some days asked everyone I met if he knew the philanthropist of the Albany. I had no luck till the Saturday, when, lunching at the club, I ran against Jenkinson, the art critic.

===Chinese===
我把这个名字收进了记忆的某个分格中，此后好几天，逢人便问是否认识那位住在奥尔巴尼的“慈善家”。一直毫无所获，直到那个星期六，我在俱乐部吃午饭时撞见了艺术评论家詹金森（Jenkinson）。

===Original===
I forget if you know that I have always been a bit of a connoisseur in a mild way. I used to dabble in prints and miniatures, but at that time my interest lay chiefly in Old Wedgwood, of which I had collected some good pieces. Old Wedgwood is a thing which few people collect seriously, but the few who do are apt to be monomaniacs. Whenever a big collection comes into the market it fetches high prices, but it generally finds its way into not more than half a dozen hands. Wedgwoodites all know each other, and they are less cutthroat in their methods than most collectors. Of all I have ever met Jenkinson was the keenest, and he would discourse for hours on the “feel” of good jasper and the respective merits of blue and sage-green grounds.

===Chinese===
我忘了你是否知道，我一向算得上是个浅尝辄止的鉴赏家。从前我也曾在版画和袖珍画像上略事涉猎，可那阵子我的兴致主要落在老韦奇伍德瓷器（Old Wedgwood）上，其中也搜罗了几件精品。老韦奇伍德是样鲜有人认真收藏的东西，可一旦认真收藏起来，那少数几人往往入了魔一般。每逢一大宗藏品流入市场，总能卖出高价，但到头来一般也不过落入寥寥五六人之手。韦奇伍德藏家彼此都相识，搜罗手段也不像多数藏家那般你死我活。在我所见过的同好之中，詹金森是痴迷最深的一个，他能就上好碧玉瓷的“手感”、蓝色底子与鼠尾草绿底子孰优孰劣，滔滔不绝地议论上好几个钟头。

===Original===
That day he was full of excitement. He babbled through luncheon about the Wentworth sale, which he had attended the week before. There had been a pair of magnificent plaques, with a unique Flaxman design, which had roused his enthusiasm. Urns and medallions and whatnot had gone to this or that connoisseur, and Jenkinson could quote their prices, but the plaques dominated his fancy, and he was furious that the nation had not acquired them. It seemed that he had been to South Kensington and the British Museum and all sorts of dignitaries, and he thought he might yet persuade the authorities to offer for them if the purchaser would resell. They had been bought by Lutrin for a well-known private collector, by name Andrew Lumley.

===Chinese===
那天他兴奋得不得了。整顿午饭他都在喋喋不休地谈论温特沃斯拍卖会（the Wentworth sale），那是他前一个星期去凑过的热闹。会上有一对气派非凡的饰板，绘着独一无二的弗拉克斯曼（Flaxman）图样，激起了他满腔的热忱。各式各样的瓮瓶、徽章以及诸如此类的物件，分头落入了这位那位藏家之手，詹金森能把它们的成交价一一报出来；可那对饰板始终盘踞着他的心思，他愤愤不平的是国家竟没有将它们收归公有。看样子他已跑遍了肯辛顿南区（South Kensington）和大英博物馆（British Museum），游说了一干要人，他觉得自己也许还能说动当局出价洽购——假如买主肯转手的话。那对饰板已被卢特兰（Lutrin）替一位赫赫有名的私人藏家买走了，此人名叫安德鲁·拉姆利。

===Original===
I pricked up my ears and asked about Mr. Lumley.

Jenkinson said he was a rich old buffer who locked up his things in cupboards and never let the public get a look at them. He suspected that a lot of the best things at recent sales had found their way to him, and that meant that they were put in cold storage for good.

I asked if he knew him.

===Chinese===
我竖起了耳朵，向他打听拉姆利先生其人。

詹金森说，他是个有钱的老家伙，把自己的宝贝全锁在柜子里，从不让公众瞧上一眼。他怀疑近来几次拍卖会上许多顶好的物件都落到了他手里，而那就意味着这些东西被永久打入了冷宫。

我又问他认不认识此人。

===Original===
No, he told me, but he had once or twice been allowed to look at his things for books he had been writing. He had never seen the man, for he always bought through agents, but he had heard of people who knew him. “It is the old silly game,” he said. “He will fill half a dozen houses with priceless treasures, and then die, and the whole show will be sold at auction and the best things carried off to America. It’s enough to make a patriot swear.”

===Chinese===
不认识，他告诉我，不过有一两回，对方曾准许他观看藏品，供他写书之用。他从未见过此人本人，因为对方买东西向来只通过代理人；但他听说过一些认识此人的人。“都是那套老掉牙的蠢把戏，”他说。“他会用无价之宝塞满五六幢房子，然后两腿一蹬死了，整批货色被拉去拍卖，顶好的东西全给运到美国去了。这事儿够让任何一个爱国者骂娘的。”

===Original===
There was balm in Gilead, however. Mr. Lumley apparently might be willing to resell the Wedgwood plaques if he got a fair offer. So Jenkinson had been informed by Lutrin, and that very afternoon he was going to look at them. He asked me to come with him, and, having nothing to do, I accepted.

===Chinese===
不过，总算还有一线宽慰：倘若出价公道，拉姆利先生看来是肯将那对韦奇伍德饰板转手的。詹金森是从卢特兰处得到这个消息的，而且就在那天下午，他就要去看那对饰板。他邀我同行，我反正无事可做，便答应了。

===Original===
Jenkinson’s car was waiting for us at the club door. It was closed, for the afternoon was wet. I did not hear his directions to the chauffeur, and we had been on the road ten minutes or so before I discovered that we had crossed the river and were traversing South London. I had expected to find the things in Lutrin’s shop, but to my delight I was told that Lumley had taken delivery of them at once.

“He keeps very few of his things in the Albany except his books,” I was told. “But he has a house at Blackheath which is stuffed from cellar to garret.”

“What is the name of it?” I asked with a sudden suspicion.

“The White Lodge,” said Jenkinson.

“But that belongs to a man called Pavia,” I said.

“I can’t help that. The things in it belong to old Lumley, all right. I know, for I’ve been three times there with his permission.”

===Chinese===
詹金森的车已在俱乐部门口候着了。车篷是合着的，因为那天下午下着雨。我没听见他吩咐司机什么，等上了路十来分钟，我才发现我们早已过了河，正在伦敦南区穿行。我本以为那些东西会在卢特兰的铺子里，不料有人告诉我，拉姆利早已当场提走了货，这倒正中我下怀。

“除了他的藏书，他在奥尔巴尼几乎不放什么东西，”我被告知，“可他在布莱克希思有一幢房子，从地窖到阁楼都塞得满满当当。”

“那房子叫什么名字？”我心头忽然掠过一丝疑云，问道。

“白屋，”詹金森说。

“可那是一个叫帕维亚的人的房子呀，”我说。

“我管不着那些。里面的东西，千真万确是老拉姆利的。我知道，因为我拿着他的许可去过那儿三回。”

===Original===
Jenkinson got little out of me for the rest of the ride. Here was excellent corroborative evidence of what I had allowed myself to suspect. Pavia was a friend of Pitt-Heron, Lumley was a friend of Pitt-Heron; Lumley was obviously a friend of Pavia, and he might be Pavia himself, for the retired East India merchant, as I figured him, would not be above an innocent impersonation. Anyhow, if I could find one or the other, I might learn something about Charles’s recent doings. I sincerely hoped that the owner might be at home that afternoon when we inspected his treasures, for so far I had found no one who could procure me an introduction to that mysterious old bachelor of artistic and philo-Teutonic tastes.

===Chinese===
剩下那段路程，詹金森没能从我嘴里套出多少话来。这可是绝好的旁证，印证了我此前大胆萌生的疑心。帕维亚是皮特-赫伦的朋友，拉姆利也是皮特-赫伦的朋友；拉姆利显然又是帕维亚的朋友——他甚至可能就是帕维亚本人，因为照我的揣想，一个退休的东印度商人，搞一出无伤大雅的冒名顶替也不无可能。无论如何，只要我能找到这两人中的任何一个，说不定就能打听到查尔斯近来的一些行径。我由衷地盼望那天下午我们鉴赏那些珍藏时，主人正好在家，因为至今我还没找到任何人能替我引见那位神秘的、趣味高雅且亲德的老单身汉。

===Original===
We reached the White Lodge about half-past three. It was one of those small, square, late-Georgian mansions which you see all around London—once a country-house among fields, now only a villa in a pretentious garden. I looked to see my super-butler Tuke, but the door was opened by a female servant, who inspected Jenkinson’s card of admission, and somewhat unwillingly allowed us to enter.

===Chinese===
我们大约三点半抵达了白屋。那是伦敦四郊随处可见的一种小巧方正的乔治王朝晚期宅邸——从前是田野间的一幢乡间别业，如今不过是坐落在一片浮华花园里的一栋别墅罢了。我巴望着能见着我那位超级管家图克，可开门的却是个女仆，她查验了詹金森的准入凭证，颇有些不情愿地放我们进去。

===Original===
My companion had not exaggerated when he described the place as full of treasures. It was far more like the shop of a Bond Street art-dealer than a civilised dwelling. The hall was crowded with Japanese armour and lacquer cabinets. One room was lined from floor to ceiling with good pictures, mostly seventeenth-century Dutch, and had enough Chippendale chairs to accommodate a public meeting. Jenkinson would fain have prowled around, but we were moved on by the inexorable servant to the little back room where lay the objects of our visit. The plaques had been only half-unpacked, and in a moment Jenkinson was busy on them with a magnifying glass, purring to himself like a contented cat.

===Chinese===
我的同伴形容此处满是珍藏，倒并没有夸大其词。这地方与其说是一处文明的居所，不如说更像邦德街（Bond Street）上某家艺术品经销商的铺子。门厅里挤满了日式盔甲和漆器橱柜。有一间屋子从地板到天花板挂满了上佳的画作，多是十七世纪的荷兰画派，屋里的齐彭代尔（Chippendale）椅子多得够容纳一场公众集会。詹金森巴不得四下转悠一番，可那名铁面无私的女仆催着我们往前走，把我们领到了后面那间小屋里——我们此行要看的东西就搁在那儿。那对饰板只拆了一半的包装，转眼间詹金森便拿着放大镜忙活上了，活像一只心满意足的猫，喉咙里呼噜呼噜地直哼哼。

===Original===
The housekeeper stood on guard by the door, Jenkinson was absorbed, and after the first inspection of the treasures I had leisure to look about me. It was an untidy little room, full of fine Chinese porcelain in dusty glass cabinets, and in a corner stood piles of old Persian rugs.

Pavia, I reflected, must be an easygoing soul, entirely oblivious of comfort, if he allowed his friend to turn his dwelling into such a pantechnicon. Less and less did I believe in the existence of the retired East Indian merchant. The house was Lumley’s, who chose to pass under another name during his occasional visits. His motive might be innocent enough, but somehow I did not think so. His butler had looked too infernally intelligent.

===Chinese===
女管家守在门口，詹金森全神贯注，我在头一回饱览那些珍宝之后，便有了闲工夫四下打量。这是一间凌乱的小屋，落满灰尘的玻璃柜里满是精美的中国瓷器，角落里还堆着一摞摞旧波斯地毯。

我暗想，帕维亚若当真容许朋友把自己的宅邸变成这么一座大货栈，那他必定是个随和得可以、全然不把舒适放在心上的人。我越发不信那个退休的东印度商人当真存在了。这房子是拉姆利的，他不过是在偶尔造访时换个化名罢了。他的动机也许足够清白，可不知怎的，我总觉得不然。他那管家看上去实在精明得叫人发毛。

===Original===
With my foot I turned over the lid of one of the packing-cases that had held the Wedgwoods. It was covered with a litter of cotton-wool and shavings, and below it lay a crumpled piece of paper. I looked again, and saw that it was a telegraph form. Clearly somebody, with the telegram in his hand, had opened the cases, and had left it on the top of one, whence it had dropped to the floor and been covered by the lid when it was flung off.

===Chinese===
我用脚翻开了其中一只装过韦奇伍德瓷器的板条箱盖子。上面是一堆乱七八糟的棉絮和刨花，下面压着一张皱巴巴的纸片。我再看了一眼，发现那是一张电报纸。分明是有人手持电报、打开过这些箱子，随手将它搁在一只箱子上头，后来它滑落到地上，又被甩开的箱盖盖住了。

===Original===
I hope and believe that I am as scrupulous as other people, but then and there came on me the conviction that I must read that telegram. I felt the gimlet eye of the housekeeper on me, so I had recourse to craft. I took out my cigarette case as if to smoke, and clumsily upset its contents amongst the shavings. Then on my knees I began to pick them up, turning over the litter till the telegram was exposed.

===Chinese===
我但愿也相信，自己与旁人一般规规矩矩，可就在那时那地，一种确信攫住了我：我非读那份电报不可。我感觉到女管家那锥子般锐利的目光正盯着我，于是只好施个巧计。我掏出烟盒，装作要抽烟的样子，笨手笨脚地把里头的烟全撒在了刨花堆里。然后我跪下身来，一支一支地捡，一面翻动着那堆杂物，直到那份电报暴露出来。

===Original===
It was in French and I read it quite clearly. It had been sent from Vienna, but the address was in some code. “Suivez a Bokhare Saronov”—these were the words. I finished my collection of the cigarettes, and turned the lid over again on the telegram, so that its owner, if he chose to look for it diligently, might find it.

===Chinese===
电报是用法文写的，我看得清清楚楚。它是从维也纳（Vienna）发来的，不过收件地址用的是某种暗语。“Suivez a Bokhare Saronov”（意为“追踪至布哈拉，萨罗诺夫”）——这就是那几个字。我捡完了烟，又把箱盖翻回去盖在电报上，好让它的主人若肯用心找一找，还能找得着。

===Original===
When we sat in the car going home, Jenkinson absorbed in meditation on the plaques, I was coming to something like a decision. A curious feeling of inevitability possessed me. I had collected by accident a few odd disjointed pieces of information, and here by the most amazing accident of all was the connecting link. I knew I had no evidence to go upon which would have convinced the most credulous common jury. Pavia knew Pitt-Heron; so probably did Lumley. Lumley knew Pavia, possibly was identical with him. Somebody in Pavia’s house got a telegram in which a trip to Bokhara was indicated. It didn’t sound much. Yet I was absolutely convinced, with the queer subconscious certitude of the human brain, that Pitt-Heron was or was about to be in Bokhara, and that Pavia-Lumley knew of his being there and was deeply concerned in his journey.

===Chinese===
回家的车上，詹金森沉浸在饰板的世界里出神，而我则渐渐拿定了主意。一种奇异的、势所难免的感觉攫住了我。我不过是出于偶然，零零碎碎地搜罗了几条互不连贯的消息，而眼前这最不可思议的一次偶然，恰恰是把它们串联起来的关键。我心里明白，自己手中这点根据，连最轻信的陪审团也说服不了。帕维亚认识皮特-赫伦；拉姆利多半也认识。拉姆利认识帕维亚，甚至可能与他本是同一人。帕维亚的宅子里有人收到了一份电报，上面暗示着一次布哈拉（Bokhara）之行。听起来没什么了不起。然而我深信不疑——带着人脑那种古怪的下意识的笃定——皮特-赫伦此刻正在布哈拉，或即将前往布哈拉，而帕维亚-拉姆利知道他的行踪，且与这趟旅程有着极深的干系。

===Original===
That night after dinner I rang up Mrs. Pitt-Heron.

She had had a letter from Tommy, a very dispirited letter, for he had had no luck. Nobody in Moscow had seen or heard of any wandering Englishman remotely like Charles, and Tommy, after playing the private detective for three weeks, was nearly at the end of his tether and spoke of returning home.

I told her to send him the following wire in her own name: “Go on to Bokhara. Have information you will meet him there.”

She promised to send the message next day and asked no further questions. She was a pearl among women.

===Chinese===
那天晚上吃过晚饭，我给皮特-赫伦太太拨了电话。

她收到了汤米的一封信——一封十分沮丧的信，因为他一无所获。莫斯科没有一个人见过或听说过任何形迹像查尔斯的英国游荡者。汤米当了三个星期的私家侦探，已是强弩之末，说起了打道回府的话来。

我让她以自己的名义给汤米拍一封电报：“继续前往布哈拉。据情报，你会在那里与他不期而遇。”

她答应第二天就拍出去，一个多余的问题也没问。她是女人中的一颗明珠。

## III / III

### Tells of a Midsummer Night / 记一个仲夏之夜

===Original===
Hitherto I had been the looker-on; now I was to become a person of the drama. That telegram was the beginning of my active part in this curious affair. They say that everybody turns up in time at the corner of Piccadilly Circus if you wait long enough. I was to find myself like a citizen of Bagdad in the days of the great Caliph, and yet never stir from my routine of flat, chambers, club, and flat.

I am wrong; there was one episode out of London, and that perhaps was the true beginning of my story.

===Chinese===
此前我一直是袖手旁观的看客；如今，我却要成为这出戏里的一名角色了。那封电报，便是我在这一桩离奇案子里亲身入局的起点。人们常说，只要你在皮卡迪利广场（Piccadilly Circus）的拐角处等得够久，天下人迟早都会在那里出现。我发觉自己仿佛成了哈里发（the Caliph）盛世里的一名巴格达（Bagdad）百姓，奇遇接踵而至，可我分明连"公寓、事务所、俱乐部、再回公寓"这一成不变的日子半步也不曾挪动。

我说错了；有一桩事发生在伦敦城外，而那或许才是我这故事真正的开端。

===Original===
Whitsuntide that year came very late, and I was glad of the fortnight’s rest, for Parliament and the Law Courts had given me a busy time. I had recently acquired a car and a chauffeur called Stagg, and I looked forward to trying it in a tour in the West country. But before I left London I went again to Portman Square.

I found Ethel Pitt-Heron in grave distress. You must remember that Tommy and I had always gone on the hypothesis that Charles’s departure had been in pursuance of some mad scheme of his own which might get him into trouble. We thought that he had become mixed up with highly undesirable friends, and was probably embarking in some venture which might not be criminal but was certain to be foolish. I had long rejected the idea of blackmail, and convinced myself that Lumley and Pavia were his colleagues. The same general notion, I fancy, had been in his wife’s mind. But now she had found something which altered the case.

===Chinese===
那年圣灵降临节（Whitsuntide）来得极晚，我乐得有这半个月的休息，因为议会和法院两头已让我忙得不可开交。我不久前刚置了一辆汽车，还雇了个叫斯塔格（Stagg）的司机，正盼着驾着它到西部乡间去跑一趟长路。可动身离京之前，我又去了一趟波特曼广场（Portman Square）。

我见到埃塞尔·皮特-赫伦正陷于深重的忧烦之中。你当记得，汤米和我向来都抱着这样一个推断：查尔斯的出走，是为着某桩他自己异想天开的疯狂计划，而那计划很可能会给他招来麻烦。我们料想他结交了一帮极要不得的朋友，八成正在干一桩虽未必犯法、却注定愚蠢的勾当。我早就不信什么敲诈勒索之说，反倒认定拉姆利（Lumley）和帕维亚（Pavia）是他的同伙。我想，他妻子心里大抵也是这般盘算。可如今，她发现了一样东西，把整个事情的性质都改变了。

===Original===
She had ransacked his papers in the hope of finding a clue to the affair which had taken him abroad, but there was nothing but business letters, notes of investments, and suchlike. He seemed to have burned most of his papers in the queer laboratory at the back of the house. But, stuffed into the pocket of a blotter on a bureau in the drawing-room where he scarcely ever wrote, she had found a document. It seemed to be the rough draft of a letter, and it was addressed to her. I give it as it was written; the blank spaces were left blank in the manuscript.

===Chinese===
她把他所有的文件都翻了个遍，指望找出点线索，好弄清究竟是什么事把他带到了国外，可找到的尽是些商业信函、投资记录之类。他似乎已把大部分文件都在宅子后头那间古怪的实验室里烧掉了。但是，在客厅一张写字台上吸墨纸板的夹袋里——而那间客厅他几乎从不去写信——她翻出了一份文件。那似乎是一封信的草稿，收信人正是她自己。我照原样抄录如下；手稿中留空之处，此处亦照留空白。

===Original===
“You must have thought me mad, or worse, to treat you as I have done. But there was a terrible reason, which some day I hope to tell you all about. I want you as soon as you get this to make ready to come out to me at… You will travel by… and arrive at… I enclose a letter which I want you to hand in deepest confidence to Knowles, the solicitor. He will make all arrangements about your journey and about sending me the supplies of money I want. Darling, you must leave as secretly as I did, and tell nobody anything, not even that I am alive﻿—that least of all. I would not frighten you for worlds, but I am on the edge of a horrible danger, which I hope with God’s help and yours to escape﻿…”

===Chinese===
"你一定以为我疯了，或者更糟，才会这样待你。可是这其中有一个可怕的原委，但愿有朝一日我能把它向你和盘托出。我要你一收到这封信，便立刻打点行装，到……来与我相聚。你将乘……动身，抵达……随信附上一封书信，务必在绝密之中面交事务律师诺尔斯（Knowles）。由他为你此行作出一切安排，并替我把所需的钱款汇来。亲爱的，你必须像我一样悄然离去，对任何人只字不提，甚至连我还活着也不要说——这一点尤其要紧。我万不愿吓着你，可我此刻正濒临一场可怖的危难，但愿靠着上帝和你的帮助，我能得以脱身……"

===Original===
That was all﻿—obviously the draft of a letter which he intended to post to her from some foreign place. But can you conceive a missive more calculated to shatter a woman’s nerves? It filled me, I am bound to say, with heavy disquiet. Pitt-Heron was no coward, and he was not the man to make too much of a risk. Yet it was clear that he had fled that day in May under the pressure of some mortal fear.

===Chinese===
信到此为止——这分明是一封信的草稿，他本打算从某个异国他乡寄出。可你还能想出一封更足以击溃一个女人神经的信来么？我必须说，它在我心里也填满了沉甸甸的不安。皮特-赫伦绝非懦夫，也不是那种会夸大风险的人。然而显而易见，他在五月的那一天仓皇出走，确是被某种要命的恐惧所驱使。

===Original===
The affair in my eyes began to look very bad. Ethel wanted me to go to Scotland Yard, but I dissuaded her. I have the utmost esteem for Scotland Yard, but I shrank from publicity at this stage. There might be something in the case too delicate for the police to handle, and I thought it better to wait.

===Chinese===
这桩案子在我眼里开始显得十分不妙。埃塞尔要我去找苏格兰场（Scotland Yard），却被我劝住了。我对苏格兰场向来极为敬重，可到了这一步，我还不愿把事情闹得满城风雨。这案子说不定有某些过于微妙、不便于警方插手之处，我以为还是暂且按兵不动为上。

---

===Original===
I reflected a great deal about the Pitt-Heron business the first day or two of my trip, but the air and the swift motion helped me to forget it. We had a fortnight of superb weather, and sailed all day through a glistening green country under the hazy blue heavens of June. Soon I fell into the blissful state of physical and mental ease which such a life induces. Hard toil, such as deerstalking, keeps the nerves on the alert and the mind active, but swimming all day in a smooth car through a heavenly landscape mesmerises brain and body.

===Chinese===
旅途头一两天，我把皮特-赫伦那桩事翻来覆去想了许多，可清风与疾驰渐渐帮我把它淡忘。我们碰上了整整两周极好的天气，整日穿行在六月朦胧蓝天下那一片闪闪发亮的绿色乡野之间。不多时我便陷入了这般生活所特有的那种身心俱泰的怡然之境。繁重的劳作，譬如猎鹿，能叫人神经紧绷、心思活跃；可整日里在一辆平稳的汽车里优哉游哉地穿行于如画的山水之间，却会催眠你的头脑与身躯。

===Original===
We ran up the Thames valley, explored the Cotswolds, and turned south through Somerset till we reached the fringes of Exmoor. I stayed a day or two at a little inn high up in the moor, and spent the time tramping the endless ridges of hill or scrambling in the arbutus thickets where the moor falls in steeps to the sea. We returned by Dartmoor and the south coast, meeting with our first rain in Dorset, and sweeping into sunlight again on Salisbury Plain. The time came when only two days remained to me. The car had behaved beyond all my hopes, and Stagg, a sombre and silent man, was lyrical in his praises.

===Chinese===
我们沿泰晤士河谷（the Thames valley）北上，探寻了科茨沃尔德（Cotswolds），再折向南穿过萨默塞特（Somerset），直到埃克斯穆尔（Exmoor）的边缘。我在荒原高处一家小客栈住了两日，把光阴都消磨在徒步走遍那绵延不绝的山脊，或在荒原陡然跌落入海之处攀爬杨梅灌木丛上。归途我们取道达特穆尔（Dartmoor）和南海岸，在多塞特（Dorset）遇上了这趟旅程头一场雨，待驶入索尔兹伯里平原（Salisbury Plain）便又重见天日。日子一天天过去，留给我的只剩两天了。汽车的表现远胜我一切的期盼，连斯塔格这个阴沉寡言的人，也赞不绝口，话里都带上了诗意。

===Original===
I wanted to be in London by the Monday afternoon, and to insure this I made a long day of it on the Sunday. It was the long day which brought our pride to a fall. The car had run so well that I resolved to push on and sleep in a friend’s house near Farnham. It was about half-past eight, and we were traversing the somewhat confused and narrow roads in the neighbourhood of Wolmer Forest, when, as we turned a sharp corner, we ran full into the tail of a heavy carrier’s cart. Stagg clapped on the brakes, but the collision, though it did no harm to the cart, was sufficient to send the butt-end of something through our glass screen, damage the tyre of the near front-wheel, and derange the steering-gear. Neither of us suffered much hurt, but Stagg got a long scratch on his cheek from broken glass, and I had a bruised shoulder.

===Chinese===
我想在周一下午赶回伦敦，为稳妥起见，礼拜天我便足足赶了一整天的路。正是这漫长的一天，叫我们的得意栽了跟头。汽车一路跑得实在太顺，我便决意再往前赶一程，到法纳姆（Farnham）附近一位朋友家借宿。大约八点半光景，我们正穿行在沃尔默森林（Wolmer Forest）一带那些颇为杂乱狭窄的道路间，刚一拐过急弯，便一头撞上了一辆沉重货运马车的车尾。斯塔格猛踩刹车，可这一撞虽未伤着那马车分毫，却足以让某件物的末端戳穿我们的挡风玻璃屏，撞坏前轮靠里一侧的轮胎，还弄乱了转向装置。我们俩都没受什么大伤，只是斯塔格被碎玻璃在脸颊上划了一道长口子，我的肩膀也淤伤了一块。

===Original===
The carrier was friendly but useless, and there was nothing for it but to arrange for horses to take the car to Farnham. This meant a job of some hours, and I found on inquiry at a neighbouring cottage that there was no inn where I could stay within eight miles. Stagg borrowed a bicycle somehow and went off to collect horses, while I morosely reviewed the alternatives before me.

===Chinese===
那赶车的为人友善却帮不上忙，眼下别无他法，只能安排马匹把汽车拖到法纳姆去。这意味着得花上好几个钟头，我到附近一间村舍一打听，方圆八英里之内连个可投宿的客栈都没有。斯塔格不知从哪儿弄来一辆自行车，骑去张罗马匹了，我则闷闷不乐地盘算着眼前这几条出路。

===Original===
I did not like the prospect of spending the June night beside my derelict car, and the thought of my friend’s house near Farnham beckoned me seductively. I might have walked there, but I did not know the road, and I found that my shoulder was paining me, so I resolved to try to find some gentleman’s house in the neighbourhood where I could borrow a conveyance. The south of England is now so densely peopled by Londoners that even in a wild district where there are no inns and few farms there are certain to be several weekend cottages.

===Chinese===
要在这六月的夜晚守着我那辆抛锚的汽车过夜，这前景我可不大乐意；而法纳姆附近朋友家那个念头，正诱惑地向我招手。我本可以徒步走去，可我不认得路，又发觉肩膀疼得厉害，便决意试试在附近找一户体面人家，好借上一辆代步的车。如今英格兰南部住了太多伦敦人，密度极高，即便在那种既无客栈、又少农舍的荒僻地带，也准能撞见几座度周末用的小别墅。

===Original===
I walked along the white ribbon of road in the scented June dusk. At first it was bounded by high gorse, then came patches of open heath, and then woods. Beyond the woods I found a park-railing, and presently an entrance-gate with a lodge. It seemed to be the place I was looking for, and I woke the lodge-keeper, who thus early had retired to bed. I asked the name of the owner, but was told the name of the place instead﻿—it was High Ashes. I asked if the owner was at home, and got a sleepy nod for answer.

===Chinese===
我沿着那条白色缎带似的路走去，暮色里六月的空气芬芳扑鼻。起初路两边是高高的荆豆，接着是一片片开阔的荒原，再往后便是树林。穿过树林，我看见一道庄园的围栏，不多时便到了一座带门房间的大门。这里似乎正是我要寻的地方，我把那早已上床安寝的门房唤醒。我问主人的姓名，得到的却是这地方的名字——它叫海艾什斯（High Ashes）。我又问主人是否在家，换来的只是一个困倦的点头。

===Original===
The house, as seen in the half-light, was a long whitewashed cottage, rising to two storeys in the centre. It was plentifully covered with creepers and roses, and the odour of flowers was mingled with the faintest savour of wood-smoke, pleasant to a hungry traveller in the late hours. I pulled an old-fashioned bell, and the door was opened by a stolid young parlourmaid.

===Chinese===
半明半暗中看去，那宅子是一长排粉刷过的乡舍，正中隆起两层高。墙面上爬满了藤蔓和蔷薇，花香里混着一缕若有若无的柴烟气息，对一个深更半夜又饥又乏的旅人来说，闻着煞是受用。我拉动一只老式的门铃，开门的是一个神情呆板的女佣。

===Original===
I explained my errand, and offered my card. I was, I said, a Member of Parliament and of the Bar, who had suffered a motor accident. Would it be possible for the master of the house to assist me to get to my destination near Farnham? I was bidden enter, and wearily seated myself on a settle in the hall.

===Chinese===
我说明来意，递上名片。我说，我是下议院的议员，也是律师界（the Bar）的一员，途中遭遇了车祸。敢问宅子的主人能否行个方便，助我赶到法纳姆附近的目的地去？我被让进屋里，疲惫地在门厅一张长靠背椅上坐下。

===Original===
In a few minutes an ancient housekeeper appeared, a grim dame whom at other times I should have shunned. She bore, however, a hospitable message. There was no conveyance in the place, as the car had gone that day to London for repairs. But if I cared to avail myself of the accommodation of the house for the night it was at my service. Meantime my servant could be looking after the car, and a message would go to him to pick me up in the morning.

===Chinese===
不多时，一位上了年纪的女管家现身——一位神情严厉的老妇人，换在别处我定要避之不及。然而她带来的却是一番好客的口信。宅子里没有可供差遣的车，因为那辆车当天已送进伦敦修理去了。不过，若我愿意在此将就过夜，屋里的床铺尽可任我使用。与此同时，我的仆人可留下来照看那辆汽车，明日一早自会传话叫他来接我。

===Original===
I gratefully accepted, for my shoulder was growing troublesome, and was conducted up a shallow oak staircase to a very pleasant bedroom with a bathroom adjoining. I had a bath, and afterwards found a variety of comforts put at my service, from slippers to razors. There was also some Elliman for my wounded shoulder. Clean and refreshed, I made my way downstairs and entered a room from which I caught a glow of light.

===Chinese===
我感激地应允了，因为肩膀已越发作痛，于是被人领着沿一道舒缓的橡木楼梯上楼，进了一间十分舒适的卧室，里头还连着一间浴室。我洗了个澡，过后又发现一应俱全的日用之物已为我备好，从拖鞋到剃须刀一概不缺。另外还备着些埃利曼（Elliman）药油，正好给我那受伤的肩膀用。洗漱清爽、精神一振之后，我下了楼，走进一间透出暖光的屋子。

===Original===
It was a library, the most attractive I think I have ever seen. The room was long, as libraries should be, and entirely lined with books, save over the fireplace, where hung a fine picture, which I took to be a Raeburn. The books were in glass cases, which showed the beautiful shallow mouldings of a more artistic age. A table was laid for dinner in a corner, for the room was immense, and the shaded candlesticks on it, along with the late June dusk, gave such light as there was. At first I thought the place was empty, but as I crossed the floor a figure rose from a deep chair by the hearth.

===Chinese===
那是一间书房，我想，这大概是我所见过的最叫人赏心悦目的一间。屋子很长——书房本就该长——四壁满满当当全是书，唯独壁炉上方挂着一幅精美的画作，我猜那是雷伯恩（Raeburn）的手笔。书都收在玻璃橱柜里，显出一种更为雅致的年代所特有的那种秀浅的线脚之美。屋角摆着一张铺好的餐桌——因为这屋子极为宽敞——桌上带罩的烛台，再加上六月暮色那点残余的天光，便是屋里全部的光源了。起初我以为屋里空无一人，可我正穿过房间，便见壁炉旁一张深深的靠椅里站起一个人影。

===Original===
“Good evening, Mr. Leithen,” a voice said. “It is a kindly mischance which gives a lonely old man the pleasure of your company.”

===Chinese===
"晚上好，利思滕先生，"一个声音说道。"这一桩好心的意外，让一个孤寂的老人有幸得您作伴。"

===Original===
He switched on an electric lamp, and I saw before me﻿—what I had not guessed from the voice﻿—an old man. I was thirty-four at the time, and counted anything over fifty old, but I judged my host to be well on in the sixties. He was about my own size, but a good deal bent in the shoulders as if from study. His face was clean-shaven and extraordinarily fine, with every feature delicately chiselled. He had a sort of Hapsburg mouth and chin, very long and pointed, but modelled with a grace which made the full lower lip seem entirely right. His hair was silver, brushed so low on the forehead as to give him a slightly foreign air, and he wore tinted glasses, as if for reading.

===Chinese===
他拧亮一盏电灯，我便看见面前——这是那声音所不曾让我料到的——是一位老人。其时我三十四岁，但凡过五十的便一律算作老，但我估量我这主人已年过六十好几。他身材与我相仿，只是双肩微驼，像是常年伏案之故。他面庞剃得干干净净，清俊异常，每一处五官都雕琢得极为精致。他生着一种哈布斯堡（Hapsburg）式的嘴和下巴，又长又尖，却勾勒得极有风度，使那丰满的下唇看上去恰到好处。他一头银发，在额前压得极低，给他平添几分异国情调，鼻梁上架着副有色眼镜，像是看书用的。

===Original===
Altogether it was a very dignified and agreeable figure who greeted me in a voice so full and soft that it belied his obvious age.

===Chinese===
总而言之，向我致意的，是一位极为雍容而可亲的人物，那嗓音浑厚又柔和，与他那显而易见的年纪全不相称。

===Original===
Dinner was a light meal, but perfect in its way. There were soles, I remember, an exceedingly well-cooked chicken, fresh strawberries and a savoury. We drank a ’95 Perrier-Jouet and some excellent Madeira. The stolid parlourmaid waited on us, and, as we talked of the weather and the Hampshire roads, I kept trying to guess my host’s profession. He was not a lawyer, for he had not the inevitable lines on the cheek. I thought that he might be a retired Oxford don, or one of the higher civil servants, or perhaps some official of the British Museum. His library proclaimed him a scholar, and his voice a gentleman.

===Chinese===
晚餐并不丰盛，却恰到好处地精致。我记得有鳎鱼，一只烹制得极其入味的鸡，新鲜的草莓，还有一道咸味点心。我们喝了一瓶九五年的佩里耶-茹埃（Perrier-Jouet），又喝了些极好的马德拉酒（Madeira）。那神情呆板的女佣在一旁侍候，我们谈着天气和汉普郡（Hampshire）的道路，我心里则不住地揣测主人的营生。他不是律师，因为脸上并无那一行注定要有的纹路。我心想他也许是位退休的牛津（Oxford）学监，或是文官里的高阶人物，又或许是大英博物馆（the British Museum）的某位要员。他的书房昭示他是个学者，他的嗓音则昭示他是个绅士。

===Original===
Afterwards we settled ourselves in armchairs and he gave me a good cigar. We talked about many things﻿—books, the right furnishing of a library, a little politics in deference to my M.P.-ship. My host was apathetic about party questions, but curious about defence matters and in his way an amateur strategist. I could fancy him inditing letters to *The Times* on national service.

===Chinese===
饭后我们各自在扶手椅里坐定，他递给我一支上好的雪茄。我们天南地北地聊——书籍、书房该当如何布置，还有少许政治，那是顾及我国会议员的身份才聊起的。主人对党派之事兴致寥寥，对国防事务却颇好奇，论起来也算得一位业余的战略家。我几乎能想象他正挥笔给《泰晤士报》（The Times）写信，力陈实行国民兵役制的主张。

===Original===
Then we wandered into foreign affairs, where I found his interest acute, and his knowledge immense. Indeed he was so well informed that I began to suspect that my guesses had been wrong, and that he was a retired diplomat. At that time there was some difficulty between France and Italy over customs duties, and he sketched for me with remarkable clearness the weak points in the French tariff administration. I had been recently engaged in a big South American railway case, and I asked him a question about the property of my clients. He gave me a much better account than I had ever got from the solicitors who briefed me.

===Chinese===
随后我们信步走入外交的话题，我发现他对此兴致极高，所知极广。他这般见闻渊博，我倒开始怀疑自己方才的种种揣测都错了，他莫非是位退休的外交官。那时法国与意大利正因关税之事闹了些别扭，他为我条分缕析地勾勒出法国关税行政的几处软肋，清晰得令人惊叹。我近来正接手一桩重大的南美铁路案，便就我当事人名下的产业向他讨教了一问。他所给的回答，比那些为我递送案卷的事务律师所讲过的，都要高明得多。

===Original===
The fire had been lit before we finished dinner, and presently it began to burn up and light the figure of my host, who sat in a deep armchair. He had taken off his tinted glasses, and as I rose to get a match I saw his eyes looking abstractedly before him.

===Chinese===
我们吃完饭之前，炉火已经生起，此刻它渐渐烧旺，照亮了我主人那坐在一张深扶手椅里的身影。他已摘下那副有色眼镜，我起身去取火柴时，看见他的双目正出神地望着前方。

===Original===
Somehow they reminded me of Pitt-Heron. Charles had always a sort of dancing light in his, a restless intelligence which was at once attractive and disquieting. My host had this and more. His eyes were paler than I had ever seen in a human head﻿—pale, bright, and curiously wild. But, whereas Pitt-Heron’s had only given the impression of reckless youth, this man’s spoke of wisdom and power as well as of endless vitality.

===Chinese===
不知怎的，那双眼睛叫我想起了皮特-赫伦。查尔斯的眼睛里总带着一种跳荡的光，一种坐立不安的聪慧，既叫人着迷，又令人不安。我这位主人眼里的这种光，比那更甚。他的眼睛比我曾见过的任何人的眼睛都要淡——淡而亮，透着一种古怪的狂野之气。只是，皮特-赫伦的眼睛只给人一种少年轻狂的印象，而这个人的眼睛，却在活力无穷之外，还诉说着智慧与权势。

===Original===
All my theories vanished, for I could not believe that my host had ever followed any profession. If he had, he would have been at the head of it, and the world would have been familiar with his features. I began to wonder if my recollection was not playing me false, and I was in the presence of some great man whom I ought to recognise.

===Chinese===
我先前那些揣测统统烟消云散了，因为我简直无法相信，主人这一生曾从事过任何一桩职业。倘若他从事过，他必定已坐上那一行的头把交椅，而世人也早该对他的尊容耳熟能详了。我开始怀疑，莫不是自己的记忆在作祟，眼前这位其实是我本该认得的某位大人物？

===Original===
As I dived into the recesses of my memory I heard his voice asking if I were not a lawyer.

I told him, Yes. A barrister with a fair common-law practice and some work in Privy Council appeals.

===Chinese===
正当我往记忆深处打捞时，只听他开口问我，我是不是干律师这一行的。

我回答说是的。一个出庭律师，有些寻常的普通法业务，也兼做一些枢密院（Privy Council）的上诉案。

===Original===
He asked me why I chose the profession.

“It came handiest,” I said. “I am a dry creature, who loves facts and logic. I am not a flier, I have no new ideas, I don’t want to lead men and I like work. I am the ordinary educated Englishman, and my sort gravitates to the Bar. We like feeling that, if we are not the builders, at any rate we are the cement of civilisation.”

===Chinese===
他问我为何选了这一行。

"图的是它最顺手，"我说。"我是个枯燥的人，爱的是事实与逻辑。我不是那种锋芒毕露的人，没有什么新主意，不想领袖群伦，只喜欢做事。我不过是个受过寻常教育的英国人，我这一类人，自然而然便汇聚到了律师界。我们喜欢这样一种感觉：纵然算不上大厦的建造者，至少我们也是黏合文明的灰泥。"

===Original===
He repeated the words “cement of civilisation” in his soft voice.

“In a sense you are right. But civilisation needs more than the law to hold it together. You see all mankind are not equally willing to accept as divine justice what is called human law.”

===Chinese===
他用他那柔和的嗓音，把"黏合文明的灰泥"几个字复述了一遍。

"在某种意义上，你说得对。但要把文明黏合在一起，光靠法律还不够。你要知道，并非全人类都同样甘心情愿地把那所谓的人间法律，当作神明的公正来接受。"

===Original===
“Of course there are further sanctions,” I said. “Police and armies and the goodwill of civilisation.”

He caught me up quickly. “The last is your true cement. Did you ever reflect, Mr. Leithen, how precarious is the tenure of the civilisation we boast about?”

===Chinese===
"当然，此外还有别的制裁力量，"我说。"警察、军队，以及文明本身的善意。"

他马上截住我的话。"最后这一项，才是你真正的灰泥。利思滕先生，你可曾想过，我们所夸耀的这种文明，其根基其实岌岌可危到何种地步？"

===Original===
“I should have thought it fairly substantial,” I said, “and the foundations grow daily firmer.”

He laughed. “That is the lawyer’s view, but believe me you are wrong. Reflect, and you will find that the foundations are sand. You think that a wall as solid as the earth separates civilisation from barbarism. I tell you the division is a thread, a sheet of glass. A touch here, a push there, and you bring back the reign of Saturn.”

===Chinese===
"我倒以为它根基相当扎实，"我说，"而且这根基与日俱坚。"

他笑了起来。"这是律师的见解，可相信我，你错了。你只要细想一想，便会发觉那根基原是沙土。你以为横亘在文明与野蛮之间的，是一堵坚如大地的城墙。我告诉你，那分野不过是一根丝线、一层薄玻璃。这儿一碰，那儿一推，萨图恩（Saturn）的时代便会卷土重来。"

===Original===
It was the kind of paradoxical, undergraduate speculation which grown men indulge in sometimes after dinner. I looked at my host to discover his mood, and at the moment a log flared up again.

His face was perfectly serious. His light wild eyes were intently watching me.

===Chinese===
这不过是那种故作惊人之论的大学生式玄想，成年人酒足饭饱之后有时也会沉迷一番。我打量主人，想看透他的心境，恰在此时，一段木柴又噼啪燃起。

他的脸庞一本正经。那双淡淡的、狂野的眼睛，正目不转睛地盯着我。

===Original===
“Take one little instance,” he said. “We are a commercial world, and have built up a great system of credit. Without our cheques and bills of exchange and currency the whole of our life would stop. But credit only exists because behind it we have a standard of value. My Bank of England notes are worthless paper unless I can get sovereigns for them if I choose. Forgive this elementary disquisition, but the point is important. We have fixed a gold standard, because gold is sufficiently rare, and because it allows itself to be coined into a portable form. I am aware that there are economists who say that the world could be run on a pure credit basis, with no metal currency at the back of it; but, however sound their argument may be in the abstract, the thing is practically impossible. You would have to convert the whole of the world’s stupidity to their economic faith before it would work.

===Chinese===
"举一个小小的例子，"他说。"我们身处一个商业的世界，业已建立起一套庞大的信用制度。离开了我们的支票、汇票和货币，我们整个生活便会停摆。可信用之所以存在，全赖其背后有一种价值本位。我手里的英格兰银行（Bank of England）钞票，若不能在我情愿时拿去兑换金币，便不过是废纸一张。恕我这一番浅显的叨唠，可这一点至关重要。我们确立了金本位，因为黄金足够稀少，又便于铸成可以随身携带的形状。我知道有些经济学家主张，世界完全可以建立在纯粹的信用之上，背后无须任何金属通货支撑；然而他们的论证无论在抽象意义上多么站得住脚，在实际上却行不通。你非得把全世界的愚蠢都尽数感化、皈依他们那套经济信仰，这事才推行得开。

===Original===
“Now, suppose something happened to make our standard of value useless. Suppose the dream of the alchemists came true, and all metals were readily transmutable. We have got very near it in recent years, as you will know if you interest yourself in chemical science. Once gold and silver lost their intrinsic value, the whole edifice of our commerce would collapse. Credit would become meaningless, because it would be untranslatable. We should be back at a bound in the age of barter, for it is hard to see what other standard of value could take the place of the precious metals. All our civilisation, with its industries and commerce, would come toppling down. Once more, like primitive man, I would plant cabbages for a living and exchange them for services in kind from the cobbler and the butcher. We should have the simple life with a vengeance﻿—not the self-conscious simplicity of the civilised man, but the compulsory simplicity of the savage.”

===Chinese===
"那么，假定发生了某桩事，使我们的价值本位变得毫无用处。假定炼金术士的迷梦成真，一切金属皆可轻易互相转化。近年以来我们离这一步已近在咫尺，你若留意化学这门学问，自然知道。金银一旦丧失其内在价值，我们整座商业大厦便会轰然倾覆。信用将变得毫无意义，因为它无从折算。我们转眼之间就要退回物物交换的时代，因为很难想出还有什么别的价值本位能取代贵金属的位置。我们的全部文明——连同它的工业与商业——都将土崩瓦解。我不得不像原始人那样，靠种卷心菜糊口，再拿菜去和鞋匠、屠夫换取他们出力的活计。我们将过起一种变本加厉的简朴生活——不是文明人那种自觉的简朴，而是野蛮人那种被迫的简朴。"

===Original===
I was not greatly impressed by the illustration. “Of course, there are many key-points in civilisation,” I said, “and the loss of them would bring ruin. But these keys are strongly held.”

===Chinese===
这番譬喻并没怎样打动我。"文明之中当然有许多关键之处，"我说，"失了其中任何一个都会招致毁灭。但这些关键所在，都把守得十分牢固。"

===Original===
“Not so strongly as you think. Consider how delicate the machine is growing. As life grows more complex, the machinery grows more intricate and therefore more vulnerable. Your so-called sanctions become so infinitely numerous that each in itself is frail. In the Dark Ages you had one great power﻿—the terror of God and His Church. Now you have a multiplicity of small things, all delicate and fragile, and strong only by our tacit agreement not to question them.”

===Chinese===
"并不如你想的那样牢固。你且想想，这架机器正变得何等精密。生活愈是繁复，机械便愈是精巧，因而也愈发脆弱。你那些所谓的制裁力量，多到不可胜数，以至于每一项本身都弱不禁风。在黑暗时代，你只有一种强大的力量——对上帝及其教会的敬畏。而如今你有的是千千万万细小之物，无不精致而脆弱，其所以有力，全仗我们彼此心照不宣地不去诘问它们。"

===Original===
“You forget one thing,” I said﻿—“the fact that men really are agreed to keep the machine going. That is what I called the ‘goodwill of civilisation.’”

He got up from his chair and walked up and down the floor, a curious dusky figure lit by the rare spurts of flame from the hearth.

===Chinese===
"你忘了一件事，"我说——"那就是人们其实都真心愿意让这架机器继续运转下去。这正是我方才所说的'文明的善意'。"

他从椅子上起身，在屋里来回踱步，那昏暗的身影被壁炉里偶尔窜起的火光映得影影绰绰。

===Original===
“You have put your finger on the one thing that matters. Civilisation is a conspiracy. What value would your police be if every criminal could find a sanctuary across the Channel, or your law courts if no other tribunal recognised their decisions? Modern life is the silent compact of comfortable folk to keep up pretences. And it will succeed till the day comes when there is another compact to strip them bare.”

===Chinese===
"你这一指，正点在唯一要紧的关节上。文明是一桩共谋。倘若每一个罪犯都能在海峡对岸寻得一处避难所，你的警察又有何用？倘若再无别的法庭承认你家法庭的判决，你的法庭又有何用？现代生活，不过是安逸之辈心照不宣地共同维持着种种体面。这套把戏会一直奏效下去，直到有那么一天，出现了另一桩共谋，要把这些体面尽数剥个精光。"

===Original===
I do not think that I have ever listened to a stranger conversation. It was not so much what he said﻿—you will hear the same thing from any group of half-baked young men﻿—as the air with which he said it. The room was almost dark, but the man’s personality seemed to take shape and bulk in the gloom. Though I could scarcely see him, I knew that those pale strange eyes were looking at me. I wanted more light, but did not know where to look for a switch. It was all so eery and odd that I began to wonder if my host were not a little mad. In any case, I was tired of his speculations.

===Chinese===
我想，我还从未听过一场比这更离奇的谈话。倒不全在他所说的话——同样的话，你从任何一伙一知半解的年轻人口里都能听到——而在于他说这番话时那副气度。屋里几近全黑，可这人的气性却仿佛在幽暗中凝聚成形、膨胀开来。我虽几乎看不清他，却知道那双淡淡的怪眼正盯着我。我想要多点光亮，却不知开关在何处。这一切如此诡异、如此古怪，我竟开始怀疑主人是不是有些疯癫。无论如何，我对他这些玄谈已倦了。

===Original===
“We won’t dispute on the indisputable,” I said. “But I should have thought that it was the interest of all the best brains of the world to keep up what you call the conspiracy.”

He dropped into his chair again.

===Chinese===
"我们就不必为无可争辩之事争辩了，"我说。"不过我原以为，维持你所说的这桩'共谋'，正是天下一切最聪慧的头脑的切身利益所在。"

他又跌坐回椅子里。

===Original===
“I wonder,” he said slowly. “Do we really get the best brains working on the side of the compact? Take the business of Government. When all is said, we are ruled by the amateurs and the second-rate. The methods of our departments would bring any private firm to bankruptcy. The methods of Parliament﻿—pardon me﻿—would disgrace any board of directors. Our rulers pretend to buy expert knowledge, but they never pay the price for it that a business man would pay, and if they get it they have not the courage to use it. Where is the inducement for a man of genius to sell his brains to our insipid governors?”

===Chinese===
"我可说不准，"他慢悠悠地说。"当真有最聪慧的头脑，肯为我们这桩默契效力么？且看治理国家这桩事。说到底，统治我们的，不过是些业余之辈与二三流的角色。我们各部的做派，搁在哪一家私人商号都足以叫它破产。议会的做派——恕我直言——搁在任何一个董事会里都是耻辱。我们的统治者装作要采买专门的知识，可他们出的价，从不及一个生意人肯出的数目；纵然弄到了手，他们又没有胆量去用。一个天才，有什么诱因肯把自己的脑力出卖给这帮乏味的当朝诸公呢？"

===Original===
“And yet knowledge is the only power﻿—now as ever. A little mechanical device will wreck your navies. A new chemical combination will upset every rule of war. It is the same with our commerce. One or two minute changes might sink Britain to the level of Ecuador or give China the key of the world’s wealth. And yet we never dream that these things are possible. We think our castles of sand are the ramparts of the universe.”

===Chinese===
"然而知识才是唯一的力量——古今皆然。一个小小的机械装置，便能摧毁你的舰队。一种新的化学配方，便能推翻一切战争的法则。商业亦然。一两个细微的变动，便足以使不列颠沉沦到厄瓜多尔（Ecuador）那般地步，或者把世界财富的钥匙交到中国（China）手里。可我们做梦也想不到这些竟有可能。我们以为自家那几座沙堡，便是宇宙的城墙。"

===Original===
I have never had the gift of the gab, but I admire it in others. There is a morbid charm in such talk, a kind of exhilaration of which one is half ashamed. I found myself interested and more than a little impressed.

===Chinese===
我从无口若悬河的才具，却十分钦佩旁人有此本领。这种谈话里有一种病态的魅力，一种叫人半带羞愧的亢奋。我发觉自己不但兴致盎然，而且深受触动，远不止一点点。

===Original===
“But surely,” I said, “the first thing a discoverer does is to make his discovery public. He wants the honour and glory, and he wants money for it. It becomes part of the world’s knowledge, and everything is readjusted to meet it. That was what happened with electricity. You call our civilisation a machine, but it is something far more flexible. It has the power of adaptation of a living organism.”

===Chinese===
"可照理说，"我答道，"一个发现者所做的头一件事，便是把他的发现公之于世。他要的是荣誉与声名，也要借此牟利。于是这发现便成了天下知识的一部分，一切自会随之重新调整以适应它。电的发明便是如此。你把我们的文明称作一架机器，可它远比机器灵活得多。它有着生命体那般自我调适的本领。"

===Original===
“That might be true if the new knowledge really became the world’s property. But does it? I read now and then in the papers that some eminent scientist has made a great discovery. He reads a paper before some Academy of Science, and there are leading articles on it, and his photograph adorns the magazines. That kind of man is not the danger. He is a bit of the machine, a party to the compact. It is the men who stand outside it that are to be reckoned with, the artists in discovery who will never use their knowledge till they can use it with full effect. Believe me, the biggest brains are without the ring which we call civilisation.”

===Chinese===
"这话倘若那新知识真的成了天下的公产，倒也成立。可它果真成了公产么？我不时在报上读到，某位卓越的科学家作出了一项重大发现。他在某个科学院宣读论文，于是报上登出社论，他的玉照也缀满了杂志。这一类人并非祸患。他是机器上的一枚零件，是那桩共谋的一方。真正不可掉以轻心的，是置身其外的人——那些发现之上的艺术家，他们的知识一日不能发挥十足的效用，便一日不肯动用。相信我，最了不起的头脑，都站在我们所谓文明的那道圈外。"

===Original===
Then his voice seemed to hesitate. “You may hear people say that submarines have done away with the battleship, and that aircraft have annulled the mastery of the sea. That is what our pessimists say. But do you imagine that the clumsy submarine or the fragile aeroplane is really the last word of science?”

===Chinese===
随后他的声音似有迟疑。"你也许听人说过，潜水艇已经叫战舰过时，飞行器也已经废除了制海权。这是咱们那些悲观论者的话。可你难道以为，笨拙的潜水艇、脆弱的飞机，当真便是科学的尽头了么？"

===Original===
“No doubt they will develop,” I said, “but by that time the power of the defence will have advanced also.”

He shook his head. “It is not so. Even now the knowledge which makes possible great engines of destruction is far beyond the capacity of any defence. You see only the productions of second-rate folk who are in a hurry to get wealth and fame. The true knowledge, the deadly knowledge, is still kept secret. But, believe me, my friend, it is there.”

===Chinese===
"它们无疑还会发展，"我说，"可到那时，防御一方的力量也必已随之精进。"

他摇了摇头。"并非如此。即便此时此刻，那足以造就巨大毁灭器具的知识，早已远远超乎任何防御所能应付的范围。你所见的，不过是些急于求名求利的二三流人物的产物。真正致命的知识——那要命的知识——至今仍被秘而不宣。可是相信我，朋友，它确确实实存在着。"

===Original===
He paused for a second, and I saw the faint outline of the smoke from his cigar against the background of the dark. Then he quoted me one or two cases, slowly, as if in some doubt about the wisdom of his words.

It was these cases which startled me. They were of different kinds﻿—a great calamity, a sudden breach between two nations, a blight on a vital crop, a war, a pestilence. I will not repeat them. I do not think I believed in them then, and now I believe less. But they were horribly impressive, as told in that quiet voice in that sombre room on that dark June night. If he was right, these things had not been the work of Nature or accident, but of a devilish art. The nameless brains that he spoke of, working silently in the background, now and then showed their power by some cataclysmic revelation. I did not believe him, but, as he put the case, showing with strange clearness the steps in the game, I had no words to protest.

===Chinese===
他顿了一顿，我看见他的雪茄升起的那缕青烟，在黑暗的底色里勾出淡淡的轮廓。随后他缓缓地给我举了一两个事例，仿佛对他这番话该不该说出口，尚有几分犹疑。

正是这几桩事例把我吓了一跳。它们各色各样——一场大灾难、两国之间骤然的反目、一种要害作物的枯萎、一场战争、一场瘟疫。我不愿在此复述。当年我便未必信他，如今信得更少。可是在那个幽暗的房间里、在那个漆黑的六月之夜，由那样一个沉静的嗓音缓缓道来，它们实在骇人至深。倘若他所言不虚，这些事便不是天灾或偶然，而是某种穷凶极恶的机巧所致。他所说的那些无名的头脑，在暗处悄无声息地运作，偶尔借某一场惊天动地的暴露来显示它们的威力。我并不信他，可他那样条理分明地把案子的来龙去脉一一道来，我竟找不出半句话来反驳。

===Original===
At last I found my voice.

“What you describe is super-anarchy, and yet it makes no headway. What is the motive of those diabolical brains?”

===Chinese===
我终于找回了自己的声音。

"你所描绘的，是一种登峰造极的无政府状态，可它到底毫无进展。那些穷凶极恶的头脑，其动机究竟何在？"

===Original===
He laughed. “How should I be able to tell you? I am a humble inquirer, and in my researches I come on curious bits of fact. But I cannot pry into motives. I only know of the existence of great extra-social intelligences. Let us say that they distrust the machine. They may be idealists and desire to make a new world, or they may simply be artists, loving for its own sake the pursuit of truth. If I were to hazard a guess, I should say that it took both types to bring about results, for the second find the knowledge and the first the will to use it.”

===Chinese===
