他笑了。"我怎么可能告诉你呢？我不过是个谦卑的探索者，在研究之中偶然撞见几桩离奇的事实碎片罢了。可动机之事，我无从窥探。我所知的，唯有那些超乎社会之外的大智大慧的存在。姑且说，它们不信任这架机器吧。它们也许是理想主义者，一心要造出一个新世界；也许不过是艺术家，纯然为追求真理本身而乐此不疲。若硬要让我猜上一猜，我以为非得这两类人合力，方能有所成就——因为后者觅得知识，前者则有运用它的意志。"

===Original===
A recollection came back to me. It was of a hot upland meadow in Tyrol, where among acres of flowers and beside a leaping stream I was breakfasting after a morning spent in climbing the white crags. I had picked up a German on the way, a small man of the Professor class, who did me the honour to share my sandwiches. He conversed fluently, but quaintly in English, and he was, I remember, a Nietzschean, and a hot rebel against the established order. “The pity,” he cried, “is that the reformers do not know, and those who know are too idle to reform. Some day there will come the marriage of knowledge and will, and then the world will march.”

===Chinese===
一桩往事浮上心头。那是在蒂罗尔（Tyrol）一片炎热的山地草甸上，我在花海之间、一道欢腾的溪流旁吃早饭，刚消磨了一个早晨去攀登那些白色的峭壁。路上我结识了一个德国人，一个教授模样的矮小男子，承蒙他赏脸，与我共享了三明治。他英语说得流利而古怪，我记得，他是个尼采派（Nietzschean），对既定的秩序满腔愤懑、激烈反叛。"可悲的是，"他嚷道，"改革者一无所知，而有所知的人又太懒，不肯去改革。总有一天，知识与意志会结成连理，到那时，世界便会大步前行。"

===Original===
“You draw an awful picture,” I said. “But if those extra-social brains are so potent, why after all do they effect so little? A dull police-officer, with the machine behind him, can afford to laugh at most experiments in anarchy.”

“True,” he said, “and civilisation will win until its enemies learn from it the importance of the machine. The compact must endure until there is a counter-compact. Consider the ways of that form of foolishness which today we call nihilism or anarchy. A few illiterate bandits in a Paris slum defy the world, and in a week they are in jail. Half a dozen crazy Russian *intellectuels* in Geneva conspire to upset the Romanovs and are hunted down by the police of Europe. All the Governments and their not very intelligent police forces join hands, and hey, presto! there is an end of the conspirators. For civilisation knows how to use such powers as it has, while the immense potentiality of the unlicensed is dissipated in vapour. Civilisation wins because it is a worldwide league; its enemies fail because they are parochial. But supposing﻿—”

===Chinese===
"你描绘的图景实在骇人，"我说。"可那些超乎社会之外的头脑若当真如此厉害，到头来为何成效竟这般寥寥？一个迟钝的警官，背后有整部机器撑腰，也尽可对大多数无政府主义的试验一笑置之。"

"不错，"他说，"而且文明一路都会赢，直到它的敌人也向它学到了机器的重要性为止。这桩默契必定延续下去，直到出现一桩与之抗衡的反默契。且看我们今日称之为虚无主义或无政府主义的那种愚蠢行径的做派：巴黎（Paris）贫民窟里几个目不识丁的匪徒公然向天下叫板，不出一个礼拜便尽数入狱；日内瓦（Geneva）半打疯狂的俄国知识分子（intellectuels）密谋推翻罗曼诺夫王朝（the Romanovs），便遭全欧警察合力追缉。各国政府连同它们那些不大高明的警察携手合力，嘿，变！密谋者便就此了账。因为文明懂得如何运用自家拥有的力量，而那些不受约束之辈的巨大潜能，却在水汽般消散。文明之所以得胜，因其是一个世界范围的联盟；它的敌人之所以一败涂地，因其格局狭隘。然而假使——"

===Original===
Again he stopped and rose from his chair. He found a switch and flooded the room with light. I glanced up blinking to see my host smiling down on me, a most benevolent and courteous old gentleman. He had resumed his tinted glasses.

“Forgive me,” he said, “for leaving you in darkness while I bored you with my gloomy prognostications. A recluse is apt to forget what is due to a guest.”

===Chinese===
他又一次停下，从椅中站起。他寻到开关，把满屋子照得通亮。我眨着眼抬头一看，只见主人正含笑俯视着我，是一位再慈祥、再谦和不过的老绅士。他又戴上了那副有色眼镜。

"恕罪，"他说，"把你撂在黑暗里，又叫你听我那些阴郁的预言，实在失礼。一个闭门索居的人，往往容易忘了待客之道。"

===Original===
He handed the cigar-box to me, and pointed to a table where whisky and mineral waters had been set out.

“I want to hear the end of your prophecies,” I said. “You were saying﻿—?”

===Chinese===
他把雪茄匣递给我，又指了指一张桌子，上头早已摆好了威士忌和矿泉水。

"我想听听你那些预言的下文，"我说。"你方才正说到——？"

===Original===
“I said﻿—supposing anarchy learned from civilisation and became international. Oh, I don’t mean the bands of advertising donkeys who call themselves International Unions of Workers and suchlike rubbish. I mean if the real brain-stuff of the world were internationalised. Suppose that the links in the cordon of civilisation were neutralised by other links in a far more potent chain. The earth is seething with incoherent power and unorganised intelligence. Have you ever reflected on the case of China? There you have millions of quick brains stifled in trumpery crafts. They have no direction, no driving power, so the sum of their efforts is futile, and the world laughs at China. Europe throws her a million or two on loan now and then, and she cynically responds by begging the prayers of Christendom. And yet, I say, supposing﻿—”

===Chinese===
"我说——假使无政府主义也向文明学乖了，变成了国际性的呢。哦，我指的并非那些自封为什么'国际工人联合会'之类名目、四处招摇的蠢驴。我指的是，倘若世上真正的人才，也实现了国际化呢？假定文明那道封锁线上的各个环节，被另一条远为强大的锁链上的环节一一抵消了呢？这大地正涌动着支离破碎的力量与一盘散沙的才智。你可曾深思过中国的情形？那里有千千万万机敏的头脑，却埋没在种种无足轻重的营生里。他们既无方向，又无驱动的力量，所以他们的努力加在一起也是徒劳，于是天下人都来耻笑中国。欧洲不时借给她一两百万款项，她倒好，冷冷地回敬一句，求全天下的基督徒为她祷告。然而我还是要说，假使——"

===Original===
“It’s a horrible idea,” I said, “and, thank God, I don’t believe it possible. Mere destruction is too barren a creed to inspire a new Napoleon, and you can do with nothing short of one.”

“It would scarcely be destruction,” he replied gently. “Let us call it iconoclasm, the swallowing of formulas, which has always had its full retinue of idealists. And you do not want a Napoleon. All that is needed is direction, which could be given by men of far lower gifts than a Bonaparte. In a word, you want a powerhouse, and then the age of miracles will begin.”

===Chinese===
"这念头太可怕了，"我说，"感谢上帝，我绝不信它有实现的可能。纯粹的破坏这门教义太过贫瘠，激不起一个新的拿破仑（Napoleon）；而少了这样一号人物，你什么也干不成。"

"这算不得破坏，"他温和地答道。"我们姑且称之为偶像破坏，是对一切陈规旧范的吞噬——这门主张从来都不乏满朝满堂的理想主义者相随。你也无须一个拿破仑。所需的不过是方向而已，而给出方向的，可以是些禀赋远逊于波拿巴（Bonaparte）的人物。一言以蔽之，你要的只是一座动力室（powerhouse），届时奇迹的时代便会开端。"

===Original===
I got up, for the hour was late, and I had had enough of this viewy talk. My host was smiling, and I think that smile was the thing I really disliked about him. It was too﻿—what shall I say?﻿—superior and Olympian.

As he led me into the hall he apologised for indulging his whims. “But you, as a lawyer, should welcome the idea. If there is an atom of truth in my fancies, your task is far bigger than you thought. You are not defending an easy case, but fighting in a contest where the issues are still doubtful. That should encourage your professional pride﻿…”

===Chinese===
我站起身来，时辰已晚，这种好高骛远的空谈我也听够了。主人脸上挂着笑，我想，那笑容才是我真正厌恶他之处。那笑容太——怎么说呢？——太超然、太奥林波斯诸神般高高在上了。

他把我领进门厅时，为自己纵情于自家这些怪念头而致歉。"不过你身为律师，理当欢迎这个念头。倘若我的胡思乱想里有哪怕一星半点的真实，你的担子可就比你以为的重得多了。你并非在替一桩轻松的案子辩护，而是在一场胜负未分的角力里厮杀。这话该能激起你的职业自尊才是……"

---

===Original===
By all the rules I should have been sleepy, for it was past midnight, and I had had a long day in the open air. But that wretched talk had unsettled me, and I could not get my mind off it. I have reproduced very crudely the substance of my host’s conversation, but no words of mine could do justice to his eery persuasiveness. There was a kind of magnetism in the man, a sense of vast powers and banked-up fires, which would have given weight to the tritest platitudes. I had a horrible feeling that he was trying to convince me, to fascinate me, to prepare the ground for some proposal. Again and again I told myself it was crazy nonsense, the heated dream of a visionary, but again and again I came back to some details which had a horrid air of reality. If the man was a romancer he had an uncommon gift of realism.

===Chinese===
按一切常理，我本该困倦才是，因为已过了午夜，我又在户外奔忙了一整天。可那场可恶的谈话搅得我心神不宁，再也无法把它从心头驱走。我方才极其粗略地复述了主人那番话的要旨，可任凭我用什么言辞，也传达不出他那诡谲的说服力。这人身上有一种磁力，一种庞大的力量与蓄势待发的烈焰之感，纵使是再陈腐的老生常谈，经他这道磁力一衬，也会显得分量十足。我有一种可怕的感觉：他在竭力说服我、蛊惑我，在为某种提议铺平道路。我一遍又一遍地对自己说，这不过是疯话，是一个妄想家发热时的梦呓；可一遍又一遍地，我又回到那些细节上来，它们带着一种可怖的现实气息。这人若是个编故事的，那他确有一种不同寻常的写实本领。

===Original===
I flung open my bedroom window and let in the soft air of the June night and the scents from leagues of clover and pines and sweet grasses. It momentarily refreshed me, for I could not believe that this homely and gracious world held such dire portents.

===Chinese===
我一把推开卧室的窗，放进六月之夜那柔和的空气，和着方圆数里草地、松林与香草飘来的芬芳。这一刻它让我稍得舒展，因为我实在难以相信，这般亲切而优美的世间，竟会藏着那等凶险的征兆。

===Original===
But always that phrase of his, the “Powerhouse,” kept recurring. You know how twisted your thoughts get during a wakeful night, and long before I fell asleep towards morning I had worked myself up into a very complete dislike of that bland and smiling gentleman, my host. Suddenly it occurred to me that I did not know his name, and that set me off on another train of reflection.

===Chinese===
可他那个词——"动力室"——却不住地在脑中回响。你大概也知道，失眠的夜里人的念头会怎样拧成怪相；在天将破晓我终于入睡之前，我已把自己鼓捣得对那位和颜悦色、笑容可掬的主人——我的主人——厌恶到了十分。忽然我想到，我竟不知他的姓名，这一来，我的思绪又拐上了另一条岔道。

===Original===
I did not wait to be called, but rose about seven, dressed, and went downstairs. I heard the sound of a car on the gravel of the drive, and to my delight saw that Stagg had arrived. I wanted to get away from the house as soon as possible, and I had no desire to meet its master again in this world.

===Chinese===
我没等别人来叫，七点光景便起了床，穿戴停当，下了楼。我听见汽车碾过车道碎石的声响，令我大喜过望的是，斯塔格到了。我恨不得尽早离开这座宅子，更不奢望在这世上再与它的主人相见。

===Original===
The grim housekeeper, who answered my summons, received my explanation in silence. Breakfast would be ready in twenty minutes; eight was Mr. Lumley’s hour for it.

“Mr. Andrew Lumley?” I asked with a start.

“Mr. Andrew Lumley,” she said.

===Chinese===
那位神情严厉的女管家应声而来，对我的解释默然领受。早餐二十分钟后便好；八点是拉姆利先生用早餐的时辰。

"安德鲁·拉姆利（Andrew Lumley）先生？"我一怔，问道。

"安德鲁·拉姆利先生。"她说。

===Original===
So that was my host’s name. I sat down at a bureau in the hall and did a wildly foolish thing.

I wrote a letter, beginning “Dear Mr. Lumley,” thanking him for his kindness and explaining the reason of my early departure. It was imperative, I said, that I should be in London by midday. Then I added: “I wish I had known who you were last night, for I think you know an old friend of mine, Charles Pitt-Heron.”

===Chinese===
原来这便是主人的姓名。我在门厅一张写字台前坐下，干了一件荒唐透顶的事。

我写了一封信，起首"亲爱的拉姆利先生"，感谢他的款待，并解释我何以要早早告辞。我说，我务必在正午之前赶回伦敦。接着我又添上一句："但愿昨夜我便知道您是谁，因为我想您认识我的一位老友——查尔斯·皮特-赫伦。"

===Original===
Breakfastless I joined Stagg in the car, and soon we were swinging down from the uplands to the shallow vale of the Wey. My thoughts were very little on my new toy or on the midsummer beauties of Surrey. The friend of Pitt-Heron, who knew about his going to Bokhara, was the maniac who dreamed of the “Powerhouse.” There were going to be dark scenes in the drama before it was played out.

===Chinese===
我连早饭也没吃，便上了车与斯塔格会合，不多时我们便从高地一路下坡，驶入韦河（the Wey）那片浅浅的河谷。我的心思几乎不在那件新玩具上，也无心顾及萨里（Surrey）仲夏的种种胜景。皮特-赫伦那位知晓他远赴布哈拉（Bokhara）之行的好友，竟就是那个做着"动力室"之梦的疯子。这出戏在落幕之前，注定还要有重重阴森的场面。

## IV / IV

### I Follow the Trail of the Super-Butler / 我追踪那位超级管家

===Original===
My first thought, as I journeyed towards London, was that I was horribly alone in this business. Whatever was to be done I must do it myself, for the truth was I had no evidence which any authority would recognise. Pitt-Heron was the friend of a strange being who collected objects of art, probably passed under an alias in South London, and had absurd visions of the end of civilisation. That, in cold black and white, was all my story came to. If I went to the police they would laugh at me, and they would be right.

===Chinese===
在返回伦敦的途中，我头一个念头便是：这件事上我孤立无援到了可怕的地步。无论要做什么，都只能由我亲自去做，因为事实是，我手里没有任何一条会被当局认可的凭证。皮特-赫伦（Pitt-Heron）是一个怪人的朋友——那怪人搜罗艺术品，多半在南伦敦（South London）化名藏身，脑子里还装着些关于文明末日的荒唐幻象。冷冰冰地白纸黑字写下来，我这一整套故事不过如此。倘若我跑去报警，他们准会嘲笑我，而且他们嘲笑得有理。

===Original===
Now I am a sober and practical person, but, slender though my evidence was, it brought to my mind the most absolute conviction. I seemed to know Pitt-Heron’s story as if I had heard it from his own lips—his first meeting with Lumley and their growing friendship; his initiation into secret and forbidden things; the revolt of the decent man, appalled that his freakishness had led him so far; the realisation that he could not break so easily with his past, and that Lumley held him in his power; and last, the mad flight under the pressure of overwhelming terror.

===Chinese===
我本是个冷静务实的人，可是，尽管凭证单薄，它在我心里唤起的却是绝对的信服。我仿佛知晓了皮特-赫伦的全部经历，就像亲耳听他讲述一般——他初识拉姆利（Lumley），以及两人日渐深厚的交情；他被引入那些隐秘而禁忌的事物；这个体面人内心的反叛，惊恐地发现自己的一时乖张竟将他带得如此之远；他意识到自己无法轻易斩断过往，而拉姆利已将他攥在手心；最后，是在排山倒海般的恐惧压迫之下那一场疯狂的出逃。

===Original===
I could read, too, the purpose of that flight. He knew the Indian frontier as few men know it, and in the wild tangle of the Pamirs he hoped to baffle his enemy. Then from some far refuge he would send for his wife and spend the rest of his days in exile. It must have been an omnipotent terror to drive such a man, young, brilliant, rich, successful, to the fate of an absconding felon.

===Chinese===
我也能读懂那场出逃的目的。他对印度边境（Indian frontier）的熟悉，世上罕有人及；他指望在帕米尔（the Pamirs）那片蛮荒杂乱的群山之间甩掉仇敌。然后，他要从某个遥远的避难所把妻子接去，在流亡中了此残生。能逼着这样一个年轻、聪慧、富有、功成名就的人走上潜逃重犯的下场，那必定是一种无所不能的恐惧。

===Original===
But Lumley was on his trail. So I read the telegram I had picked up on the floor of the Blackheath house, and my business was to frustrate the pursuit. Someone must have gone to Bokhara, some creature of Lumley’s, perhaps the super-butler I had met in the County Court. The telegram, for I had noted the date, had been received on the 27th day of May. It was now the 15th of June, so if someone had started immediately on its receipt, in all probability he would by now be in Bokhara.

===Chinese===
可拉姆利已经盯上了他。这是我从布莱克希思（Blackheath）那宅子地板上捡到的那封电报里读出来的，而我要做的，便是挫败这场追捕。一定有人已经动身去了布哈拉（Bokhara）——某个拉姆利的爪牙，或许就是我在郡法院（County Court）遇见的那位“超级管家”。那封电报——我记得留意过日期——是五月二十七日收到的。如今已是六月十五日，所以倘若有人一收到电报便立刻启程，此刻十有八九已经到了布哈拉。

===Original===
I must find out who had gone and endeavour to warn Tommy. I calculated that it would have taken him seven or eight days to get from Moscow by the Transcaspian; probably he would find Pitt-Heron gone, but inquiries would set him on the track. I might be able to get in touch with him through the Russian officials. In any case, if Lumley were stalking Pitt-Heron, I, unknown and unsuspected, would be stalking Lumley.

===Chinese===
我必须查出究竟是谁去了，并设法向汤米示警。我估算过，他走外里海铁路（the Transcaspian）从莫斯科过来，路上大约要七八天；多半他会发现皮特-赫伦已经离去，可一番查问总能让他摸到踪迹。我也许能通过俄国的官员与他取得联络。无论如何，倘若拉姆利在追踪皮特-赫伦，那么我这个无人知晓、未受猜疑的人，便要在暗中追踪拉姆利。

===Original===
And then in a flash I realised my folly.

===Chinese===
就在这时，我猛然惊觉了自己的愚蠢。

===Original===
The wretched letter I had written that morning had given the whole show away. Lumley knew that I was a friend of Pitt-Heron, and that I knew that he was a friend of Pitt-Heron. If my guess was right, friendship with Lumley was not a thing Charles was likely to confess to, and he would argue that my knowledge of it meant that I was in Charles’s confidence. I would therefore know of his disappearance and its cause, and alone in London would connect it with the decorous bachelor of the Albany. My letter was a warning to him that he could not play the game unobserved, and I, too, would be suspect in his eyes.

===Chinese===
那天早晨我写的那封该死的信，已把整盘底细都抖搂了出去。拉姆利知道我是皮特-赫伦的朋友，也知道我晓得他是皮特-赫伦的朋友。倘若我猜得不错，与拉姆利交往这件事，查尔斯是断不肯对人承认的；他会推断，我既知道这层关系，便意味着我深受查尔斯信任。这么一来，我自然知道他的失踪及其缘由，而且在整个伦敦，只有我会把这件事同奥尔巴尼（the Albany）那位端方的单身绅士联系起来。我那封信等于向他发出警告：他休想在不被觉察的情况下继续他的把戏；于是，在他眼里，我也成了可疑之人。

===Original===
My first business was to get into touch with Tommy.

===Chinese===
我的头一桩要务，便是与汤米取得联络。

===Original===
At that time I had a friend in one of the Embassies, whose acquaintance I had made on a dry-fly stream in Hampshire. I will not tell you his name, for he has since become a great figure in the world’s diplomacy, and I am by no means certain that the part he played in this tale was strictly in accordance with official etiquette. I had assisted him on the legal side in some of the international worries that beset all Embassies, and we had reached the point of intimacy which is marked by the use of Christian names and by dining frequently together. Let us call him Monsieur Felix. He was a grave young man, slightly my senior, learned, discreet, and ambitious, but with an engaging boyishness cropping up now and then under the official gold lace. It occurred to me that in him I might find an ally.

===Chinese===
那时我在某家大使馆里有一位朋友，我是在汉普郡（Hampshire）一条用干蝇钓鳟鱼的溪畔与他结识的。我不愿说出他的名字，因为日后他成了世界外交舞台上的一位显赫人物，而我全然不敢断定，他在这桩故事里所扮演的角色是否严格合乎官场礼节。他曾就一些困扰着所有大使馆的国际纠纷，请我在法律方面予以协助，于是我们交情渐深，深到彼此直呼教名、频频共进晚餐的地步。我们姑且称他为费利克斯先生（Monsieur Felix）。他是个沉稳的年轻人，略长我几岁，博学、审慎而抱负不凡，却在官服的金绦之下，不时透出一种讨人喜欢的少年气。我心想，或许能在他身上觅得一位盟友。

===Original===
I reached London about eleven in the morning, and went straight to Belgrave Square. Felix I found in the little library off the big secretaries’ room, a sunburnt sportsman fresh from a Norwegian salmon river. I asked him if he had half an hour to spare, and was told that the day was at my service.

===Chinese===
我大约上午十一点钟抵达伦敦，径直赶往贝尔格雷夫广场（Belgrave Square）。我在大秘书室隔壁那间小小的图书室里找到了费利克斯——他刚从挪威一条钓鲑鱼的河上回来，是个晒得黝黑的运动健儿。我问他能否匀出半小时给我，得到的回答是：这一整天都听我差遣。

===Original===
“You know Tommy Deloraine?” I asked.

He nodded.

“And Charles Pitt-Heron?”

“I have heard of him.”

“Well, here is my trouble. I have reason to believe that Tommy has joined Pitt-Heron in Bokhara. If he has, my mind will be greatly relieved, for, though I can’t tell you the story, I can tell you that Pitt-Heron is in very considerable danger. Can you help me?”

===Chinese===
“你认识汤米·德洛兰（Tommy Deloraine）吧？”我问。

他点了点头。

“还有查尔斯·皮特-赫伦？”

“听说过此人。”

“是这样，我有件麻烦事。我有理由相信，汤米已经在布哈拉与皮特-赫伦会合了。若真如此，我便大大松了一口气；因为，详情虽不能奉告，我却可以告诉你，皮特-赫伦正身处相当大的危险之中。你能帮我么？”

===Original===
Felix reflected. “That should be simple enough. I can wire in cipher to the Military Governor. The police there are pretty efficient, as you may imagine, and travellers don’t come and go without being remarked. I should be able to give you an answer within twenty-four hours. But I must describe Tommy. How does one do that in telegraphese?”

===Chinese===
费利克斯思忖片刻。“这应当再简单不过。我可以拍一份密码电报给那位军政长官。那里的警察相当能干，这你可想而知，旅客的来去绝不会无人留意。二十四小时之内我就能给你回音。不过我得把汤米的样貌描述一番。在电报用语里，这可怎么个说法？”

===Original===
“I want you to tell me another thing,” I said. “You remember that Pitt-Heron has some reputation as a Central Asian traveller. Tommy, as you know, is as mad as a hatter. Suppose these two fellows at Bokhara, wanting to make a long trek into wild country—how would they go? You’ve been there, and know the lie of the land.”

===Chinese===
“我还要你告诉我另一桩事，”我说。“你总记得，皮特-赫伦作为中亚旅行家是颇有些名气的。而汤米，你也知道，疯起来简直不可理喻。假如这两个人在布哈拉打算深入蛮荒之地作一次长途跋涉——他们会怎么走？你到过那儿，熟悉那边的地形。”

===Original===
Felix got down a big German atlas, and for half an hour we pored over it. From Bokhara, he said, the only routes for madmen ran to the south. East and north you got into Siberia; west lay the Transcaspian desert; but southward you might go through the Hissar range by Pamirski Post to Gilgit and Kashmir, or you might follow up the Oxus and enter the north of Afghanistan, or you might go by Merv into northeastern Persia. The first he thought the likeliest route, if a man wanted to travel fast.

===Chinese===
费利克斯取下一大部德国地图册，我们对着它端详了半个钟头。他说，从布哈拉出发，留给疯子的去路只有向南。向东、向北便会进入西伯利亚（Siberia）；向西是外里海沙漠；唯有向南，你可以经由帕米尔斯基哨所（Pamirski Post）穿过希萨尔山脉（the Hissar range），前往吉尔吉特（Gilgit）和克什米尔（Kashmir）；也可以溯阿姆河（the Oxus）而上，进入阿富汗（Afghanistan）北部；还可以取道梅尔夫（Merv），进入波斯（Persia）东北部。倘若有人想快马加鞭地赶路，他认为头一条路最有可能。

===Original===
I asked him to put in his cable a suggestion about watching the Indian roads, and left him with a promise of early enlightenment.

===Chinese===
我请他在电报里附上一条建议，要他们留意一下印度那几条路；随后便告辞了，他答应尽快给我消息。

===Original===
Then I went down to the Temple, fixed some consultations, and spent a quiet evening in my rooms. I had a heavy sense of impending disaster, not unnatural in the circumstances. I really cannot think what it was that held me to the job, for I don’t mind admitting that I felt pretty queasy about it. Partly, no doubt, liking for Tommy and Ethel, partly regret for that unfortunate fellow Pitt-Heron, most of all, I think, dislike of Lumley. That bland superman had fairly stirred my prosaic antipathies.

===Chinese===
随后我便下楼去了律师学院区（the Temple），安排了几桩会商，在寓所里度过了一个平静的夜晚。我心头压着一种大祸临头的沉重感，处在这种境地，倒也并不反常。我实在想不出究竟是什么把我拴在了这桩差事上，因为我不妨承认，我对它心里着实发毛。一部分，无疑是出于对汤米和埃塞尔（Ethel）的情分；一部分，是出于对那个不幸的皮特-赫伦的怜惜；而最主要的，我想，是对拉姆利的厌恶。那位温和的超人，着实撩起了我这种务实之人的种种反感。

===Original===
That night I went carefully over every item in the evidence to try and decide on my next step. I had got to find out more about my enemies. Lumley I was pretty certain would baffle me, but I thought I might have a better chance with the super-butler. As it turned out I hit his trail almost at once.

===Chinese===
那天夜里，我把凭证的每一条都仔细过了一遍，好决定下一步该怎么走。我必须更多地摸清我的敌人们。拉姆利这一头，我十分肯定他会让我无从下手；可我想，对付那位“超级管家”，我或许还能多几分胜算。事实证明，我几乎立刻就摸到了他的踪迹。

---

===Original===
Next day I was in a case at the Old Bailey. It was an important prosecution for fraud, and I appeared, with two leaders, for the Bank concerned. The amazing and almost incredible thing about this story of mine is the way clues kept rolling in unsolicited, and I was to get another from this dull prosecution. I suppose that the explanation is that the world is full of clues to everything, and that, if a man’s mind is sharp-set on any quest, he happens to notice and take advantage of what otherwise he would miss.

===Chinese===
第二天，我在老贝利（Old Bailey）有一桩案子。那是一起重大的欺诈公诉，我与两位首席律师一同代表当事的银行出庭。我这段经历中最奇妙、几乎令人难以置信之处，便是线索源源不断地、不请自来地滚到我手里；而在这桩乏味的公诉中，我又要到手一条。我想，个中缘故无非是：这世上处处都藏着万事万物的线索，一个人的心思若是紧紧拴在某桩追寻上，他便会留意到、并利用那些平日里他会视而不见的东西。

===Original===
My leaders were both absent the first day, and I had to examine our witnesses alone. Towards the close of the afternoon I put a fellow in the box, an oldish, drink-sodden clerk from a Cannon Street bucket-shop. His evidence was valuable for our case, but I was very doubtful how he would stand a cross-examination as to credit. His name was Routh, and he spoke with a strong North-country accent. But what caught my attention was his face. His jaw looked as if it had been made in two pieces which did not fit, and he had little, bright protuberant eyes. At my first glance I was conscious of a recollection.

===Chinese===
第一天，我的两位首席律师都缺席了，我只好独自盘问己方证人。临近下午散庭时分，我让一个人上了证人席——坎农街（Cannon Street）一家投机经纪行（bucket-shop）里一个上了年纪、酒精浸透了的文书。他的证词对我们的案子颇有价值，可他能否经得住针对信用度的反诘问，我心里很没底。他姓劳思（Routh），说话带着浓重的北方口音。然而真正攫住我注意的，是他的脸。他的下巴看着像是分成两截拼成的、又没能拼拢，一双小而亮的眼睛向外鼓着。头一眼看去，我便觉出一种似曾相识之感。

===Original===
He was still in the box when the Court rose, and I informed the solicitors that before going further I wanted a conference with the witness. I mentioned also that I should like to see him alone. A few minutes later he was brought to my chambers, and I put one or two obvious questions on the case, till the managing clerk who accompanied him announced with many excuses that he must hurry away. Then I shut the door, gave Mr. Routh a cigar, and proceeded to conduct a private inquiry.

===Chinese===
散庭时他还在证人席上，我便告知律师方面，在继续下去之前，我要先和这位证人谈一谈。我又说，希望能单独见他。几分钟后，他被领到了我的议事室，我先就案子问了一两个显而易见的问题，直到陪同他的那位主管文书连连告罪、说必须赶着离去。随后我关上门，递给劳思先生一支雪茄，着手进行一场私下查问。

===Original===
He was a pathetic being, only too ready to talk. I learned the squalid details of his continuous misfortunes. He had been the son of a dissenting minister in Northumberland, and had drifted through half a dozen occupations till he found his present unsavoury billet. Truth was written large on his statement, he had nothing to conceal, for his foible was folly, not crime, and he had not a rag of pride to give him reticence. He boasted that he was a gentleman and well-educated, too, but he had never had a chance. His brother had advised him badly; his brother was too clever for a prosaic world; always through his reminiscences came this echo of fraternal admiration and complaint.

===Chinese===
他是个可怜虫，巴不得有个人听他说话。我从他嘴里听说了他那接二连三的不幸中种种寒伧的细节。他是诺森伯兰（Northumberland）一个非国教牧师的儿子，先后辗转了六七种行当，才落到眼下这个不光彩的差事。他的话里明明白白地写着真实二字，他没有什么要遮掩的，因为他的软肋在于糊涂，而非犯罪；他也没有一丁点自尊来教他三缄其口。他吹嘘自己是个绅士，也受过良好教育，只是从来没能碰上机会。都是他兄弟给他出的主意不好；他兄弟太聪明了，在这平庸的世道里吃不开了；在他这一整套回忆里，始终回荡着这般既崇拜又怨怼的手足之情。

===Original===
It was about the brother I wanted to know, and Mr. Routh was very willing to speak. Indeed, it was hard to disentangle facts from his copious outpourings. The brother had been an engineer and a highly successful one; had dallied with politics, too, and had been a great inventor. He had put Mr. Routh on to a South American speculation, where he had made a little money but speedily lost it again. Oh, he had been a good brother in his way, and had often helped him, but he was a busy man, and his help never went quite far enough. Besides, he did not like to apply to him too often. I gathered that the brother was not a person to take liberties with.

===Chinese===
我所要知道的，正是这位兄弟，而劳思先生十分乐意谈论他。说实在的，要从他那连篇累牍的倾吐里理出事实的头绪来，倒颇费功夫。这位兄弟曾是个工程师，而且极其成功；也曾在政界消遣过一阵，还是个了不起的发明家。他曾让劳思先生参与一桩南美（South American）的投机买卖，劳思先生在那上头小赚了一笔，可转眼又赔了个精光。噢，就他那方面而言，他算是个好兄弟，时常接济他，可他是个忙人，那点接济总也差着最要紧的一截。再者，他也不便过于频繁地向他开口。我听出言外之意：这位兄弟可不是个好随便造次的人。

===Original===
I asked him what he was doing now.

“Ah,” said Mr. Routh, “that is what I wish I could tell you. I will not conceal from you that for the moment I am in considerable financial straits, and this case, though my hands are clean enough, God knows, will not make life easier for me. My brother is a mysterious man, whose business often takes him abroad. I have never known even his address, for I write always to a London office from which my communications are forwarded. I only know that he is in some big electrical business, for I remember that he once let drop the remark that he was in charge of some power station. No, I do not think it is in London, probably somewhere abroad. I heard from him a fortnight ago, and he told me he was just leaving England for a couple of months. It is very annoying, for I want badly to get into touch with him.”

===Chinese===
我问他眼下在做什么。

“唉，”劳思先生说，“这正是我巴不得能告诉您的。我不瞒您说，眼下手头正紧得厉害，而这桩案子——老天知道，我两只手可是干干净净的——也不会让我的日子好过些。我兄弟是个神秘的人，事务常常要他出国。我连他的住址都不知道，因为我的信一律寄到伦敦的一个办事处，再由那儿转交。我只晓得他做着什么大电器买卖，因为我记得他有一次曾随口说过，他主管着某座发电站。不，我想那不在伦敦，多半是在海外什么地方。半个月前我收到过他的信，他说马上要离开英国两三个月。这真叫人恼火，因为我正急着要跟他联系上。”

===Original===
“Do you know, Mr. Routh,” I said, “I believe I have met your brother. Is he like you in any way?”

“We have a strong family resemblance, but he is taller and slimmer. He has been more prosperous, and has lived a healthier life, you see.”

===Chinese===
“劳思先生，你可知，”我说，“我相信我见过令兄。他可有哪处与你相像？”

“我们一家的相像是很深的，只是他比我高些、瘦些。您看，他更得意些，日子也过得比我康健。”

===Original===
“Do you happen to know,” I asked, “if he ever uses another name? I don’t think that the man I knew was called Routh.”

The clerk flushed. “I think it highly unlikely that my brother would use an alias. He has done nothing to disgrace a name of which we are proud.”

===Chinese===
“你可知，”我又问，“他是否用过别的名字？我记得我认识的那个人并不姓劳思。”

那文书涨红了脸。“我兄弟会使用化名，我看是极不可能的事。他绝没有做过任何有辱我们这个引以为豪的姓氏的事。”

===Original===
I told him that my memory had played me false, and we parted on very good terms. He was an innocent soul, one of those people that clever rascals get to do their dirty work for them. But there was no mistaking the resemblance. There, without the brains and force and virility, went my super-butler of Blackheath, who passed under the name of Tuke.

===Chinese===
我告诉他是我自己记错了，我们便十分客气地分了手。他是个清白无辜的人，正是那种被聪明的恶棍支使着去干脏活的料子。可那副相像是错不了的。在那儿走的，正是我在布莱克希思遇见的那位“超级管家”，只是少了他那份头脑、那股劲头和那点阳刚之气——他化名图克（Tuke）。

===Original===
The clerk had given me the name of the office to whose address he had written to his brother. I was not surprised to find that it was that of the firm of stockbrokers for whom I was still acting in the bearer-bonds case where I had heard Pavia’s name.

===Chinese===
那文书把他给兄弟写信的那个办事处的字号告诉了我。我查出，那正是我在一桩不记名债券案（bearer-bonds case）里仍受聘代理的那家股票经纪行——也正是我在那桩案子里头一次听到帕维亚（Pavia）这个名字的地方。这一发现倒并不令我意外。

===Original===
I rang up the partner whom I knew and told him a very plausible story of having a message for one of Mr. Pavia’s servants, and asked him if he were in touch with them and could forward letters. He made me hold the line, and then came back and told me that he had forwarded letters for Tuke, the butler, and one Routh who was a groom or footman. Tuke had gone abroad to join his master and he did not know his address. But he advised me to write to the White Lodge.

===Chinese===
我给认识的那位合伙人打了个电话，编了一套十分入情入理的话，说我有一桩口信要转给帕维亚先生手下一个仆人，便问他是否与那些仆人有联络、能否代为转信。他让我别挂线，过了一会儿回来告诉我，说他确实替一位名叫图克的管家、还有一个叫劳思的马夫（或是一名男仆）转转过信件。图克已经出国去侍奉主人了，他不知道图克的下落。不过他劝我不妨写信到白屋（the White Lodge）去。

===Original===
I thanked him and rang off. That was settled anyhow. Tuke’s real name was Routh, and it was Tuke who had gone to Bokhara.

===Chinese===
我谢过他，挂了电话。事情无论如何算是定了。图克的真名实姓便是劳思，而动身去了布哈拉的，也正是图克。

===Original===
My next step was to ring up Macgillivray at Scotland Yard and get an appointment in half an hour’s time. Macgillivray had been at the Bar—I had read in his chambers—and was now one of the heads of the Criminal Investigation Department. I was about to ask him for information which he was in no way bound to give me, but I presumed on our old acquaintance.

===Chinese===
我的下一步，便是给苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）的麦克吉利夫雷（Macgillivray）挂个电话，约他半小时后见一面。麦克吉利夫雷曾是律师界（the Bar）中人——我曾在他的事务所里做过见习生——如今是刑事调查局的几位主管之一。我要向他打听的消息，他全然没有义务奉告，可我只好倚老卖老，借我们那点旧交情了。

===Original===
I asked him first whether he had ever heard of a secret organisation which went under the name of the Powerhouse. He laughed out loud at my question.

“I should think we have several hundreds of such pet names on our records,” he said. “Everything from the Lodge of the Baldfaced Ravens to Solomon’s Seal No. X. Fancy nomenclature is the relaxation of the tired anarchist, and matters very little. The dangerous fellows have no names, no numbers even, which we can get hold of. But I’ll get a man to look up our records. There may be something filed about your Powerhouse.”

===Chinese===
我头一句便问他，可曾听说过一个名叫“动力室”的秘密组织。听了这问题，他纵声大笑。

“我想，我们的档案里少说也有几百个这类雅号，”他说，“从‘白脸渡鸦分会’到‘所罗门印记第十号’，应有尽有。给组织起些花哨名目，是疲惫的无政府主义者们的消遣，根本无关紧要。真正危险的家伙既没有名字，连我们能抓得住的编号都没有。不过我会叫个人去翻翻档案。关于你这个‘动力室’，也许存着点材料。”

===Original===
My second question he answered differently. “Routh! Routh! Why, yes, there was a Routh we had dealings with a dozen years ago, when I used to go the Northeastern circuit. He was a trade-union official who bagged the funds, and they couldn’t bring him to justice because of the ridiculous extralegal status they possess. He knew it, and played their own privileges against them. Oh, yes, he was a very complete rogue. I once saw him at a meeting in Sunderland, and I remember his face—sneering eyes, diabolically clever mouth, and with it all as smug as a family butler. He has disappeared from England—at least we haven’t heard of him for some years, but I can show you his photograph.”

===Chinese===
我的第二个问题，他的回答便大不相同了。“劳思！劳思！哟，有的，十几年前我们跟一个劳思打过交道，那时我还常走东北巡回法庭（the Northeastern circuit）。他是个工会干事，私吞了工会的款项，可由于工会那种荒唐的法外地位，他们没法将他治罪。他清楚这一点，便拿工会自己的特权来反制他们。噢，没错，他是个彻头彻尾的无赖。我有一次在桑德兰（Sunderland）的一个会上见过他，我还记得他那张脸——一双讥讽的眼睛，一张恶毒地透着精明的嘴，可这一副模样配在脸上，又活像个殷勤的管家。他已经从英国销声匿迹了——至少这几年我们没再听说他的消息，不过我可以拿他的照片给你看。”

===Original===
Macgillivray took from a lettered cabinet a bundle of cards, selected one and tossed it towards me. It was that of a man of thirty or so, with short side-whiskers and a drooping moustache. The eyes, the ill-fitting jaw, and the brow were those of my friend, Mr. Tuke, brother and patron of the sorrowful Mr. Routh, who had already that afternoon occupied my attention.

===Chinese===
麦克吉利夫雷从一个贴着标签的档案柜里抽出一沓卡片，拣出一张朝我扔过来。那是个三十来岁男子的照片，留着短短的连鬓胡子和一撇下垂的髭须。那双眼睛、那副拼不拢的下巴、那额头——全属于我的那位朋友图克先生，也就是那位叫人哀怜的劳思先生的兄弟兼恩主；而这后一位，恰好在那天下午已经占去了我不少心思。

===Original===
Macgillivray promised to make certain inquiries, and I walked home in a state of elation. Now I knew for certain who had gone to Bokhara, and I knew something, too, of the traveller’s past. A discredited genius was the very man for Lumley’s schemes—one who asked for nothing better than to use his brains outside the ring-fence of convention. Somewhere in the wastes of Turkestan the ex-trade-union official was in search of Pitt-Heron. I did not fancy that Mr. Tuke would be very squeamish.

===Chinese===
麦克吉利夫雷答应去查问一番，我便踏着一种扬眉吐气的心情走回了家。如今我确切地知道是谁去了布哈拉，也对这位旅人的过往有所了解。一个名誉扫地的天才，正是拉姆利那些图谋最合适的人选——这种人巴不得跳出常规的藩篱，去使唤自己的脑力。在这突厥斯坦（Turkestan）某处的荒原上，这位昔日的工会干事正在追寻皮特-赫伦的下落。我想，图克先生这种人，下手未必会有多少顾忌。

---

===Original===
I dined at the club and left early. Going home, I had an impression that I was being shadowed.

===Chinese===
我在俱乐部用了晚饭，早早地离席回去。回家的路上，我有一种被人盯梢的感觉。

===Original===
You know the feeling that someone is watching you, a sort of sensation which the mind receives without actual evidence. If the watcher is behind where you can’t see him you have a cold feeling between your shoulders. I daresay it is a legacy from the days when the caveman had to look pretty sharp to keep from getting his enemy’s knife between the ribs.

===Chinese===
你一定知道那种有人在暗中盯着你的感觉——一种心灵无须确凿凭证便能接收到的知觉。倘若那盯梢的人就在你看不见的背后，你会觉得两肩之间凉飕飕的。我猜想，这是穴居人时代传下来的遗产：那时候，洞穴人须得眼观六路，才不至于让敌人的刀子捅进肋骨之间。

===Original===
It was a bright summer evening, and Piccadilly had its usual crowd of motorcars and busses and foot passengers. I halted twice, once in St. James’s Street and once at the corner of Stratton Street, and retraced my steps for a bit, and each time I had the impression that someone a hundred yards or so off had done the same. My instinct was to turn round and face him, whoever he was, but I saw that that was foolishness. Obviously in such a crowd I could get no certainty in the matter, so I put it out of my mind.

===Chinese===
那是个明媚的夏日黄昏，皮卡迪利（Piccadilly）大街上照旧挤满了汽车、公共马车和来来往往的行人。我停过两次脚，一次在圣詹姆斯街（St. James’s Street），一次在斯特拉顿街（Stratton Street）的拐角，并且都往回走了一小段；而每一次，我都有这样的感觉：百来码开外有个人，也同我一样折了回来。我的本能是回过身去，不管他是谁，与他当面对峙，可我看得出这是愚蠢之举。在这样的人群里，我显然无法把事情看清看准，于是便索性不去想它。

===Original===
I spent the rest of the evening in my rooms, reading cases and trying to keep my thoughts off Central Asia. About ten I was rung up on the telephone by Felix. He had had his answer from Bokhara. Pitt-Heron had left with a small caravan on June 2nd by the main road through the Hissar range. Tommy had arrived on June 10th and on the 12th had set off with two servants on the same trail. Travelling the lighter of the two, he should have overtaken Pitt-Heron by the 15th at latest.

===Chinese===
那天余下的时光，我在寓所里翻阅案卷，竭力不让自己去想中亚的事。约莫十点钟，费利克斯打来电话。他已经得到布哈拉那边的回音了。皮特-赫伦已于六月二日带着一支小小的商队，沿着穿过希萨尔山脉的大道启程离去。汤米是六月十日到的，十二日便带着两名仆从上了同一条路。汤米行装较轻，至迟十五日便该追上皮特-赫伦。

===Original===
That was yesterday, and my mind was immensely relieved. Tommy in such a situation was a tower of strength, for, whatever his failings in politics, I knew no one I would rather have with me to go tiger-shooting.

===Chinese===
那已经是昨天的事了，我心里大大地松了一口气。处在这种境地，有汤米在便如同有了一根擎天柱；因为，不管他在政治上有多少短处，要论去猎虎这种事，再没有别人是我更愿与之同行的了。

===Original===
Next day the sense of espionage increased. I was in the habit of walking down to the Temple by way of Pall Mall and the Embankment, but, as I did not happen to be in Court that morning, I resolved to make a detour and test my suspicions. There seemed to be nobody in Down Street as I emerged from my flat, but I had not walked five yards before, turning back, I saw a man enter from the Piccadilly end, while another moved across the Hertford Street opening. It may have been only my imagination, but I was convinced that these were my watchers.

===Chinese===
第二天，那种遭人监视的感觉愈发强烈了。我平日里惯于取道波迈尔（Pall Mall）和滨河大道（the Embankment）步行去律师学院区，可那天早晨我恰巧无须出庭，便决意绕个弯子，验证一下自己的疑心。我走出寓所时，唐街（Down Street）上似乎空无一人，可我还没走出五码，回头一望，便看见一个人从皮卡迪利那头走了进来，与此同时，另一个人正从赫特福德街（Hertford Street）的路口横穿过去。这或许只是我的臆想，可我深信，这二人便是盯我梢的。

===Original===
I walked up Park Lane, for it seemed to me that by taking the Tube at the Marble Arch Station I could bring matters to the proof. I have a knack of observing small irrelevant details, and I happened to have noticed that a certain carriage in the train which left Marble Arch about 9:30 stopped exactly opposite the exit at the Chancery Lane Station, and by hurrying up the passage one could just catch the lift which served an earlier train and so reach the street before any of the other travellers.

===Chinese===
我沿着公园巷（Park Lane）走去，因为我心想，在大理石拱门站（Marble Arch Station）搭地铁，便可把事情验证个明白。我有个记取细枝末节的本事，而我又恰好留意到：约莫 9:30 从大理石拱门开出的一班列车里，某一节车厢正好停在大法官巷站（Chancery Lane Station）的出口对面，只要在过道里加快脚步，便能刚好赶上一部为一班更早列车服务的电梯，从而抢在其他所有旅客之前到达地面。

===Original===
I performed this manoeuvre with success, caught the early lift, reached the street and took cover behind a pillar-box from which I could watch the exit of passengers from the stairs. I judged that my tracker, if he missed me below, would run up the stairs rather than wait for the lift. Sure enough, a breathless gentleman appeared, who scanned the street eagerly, and then turned to the lift to watch the emerging passengers. It was clear that the espionage was no figment of my brain.

===Chinese===
我这一手果然得逞，赶上了那部较早的电梯，到了地面，便躲在一个邮筒后面，从那里可以盯着楼梯口的旅客出口。我料定，那个跟踪我的人倘若在下头跟丢了，宁可奔上楼梯，也不肯干等电梯。果不其然，一位气喘吁吁的绅士现身了，他急切地四下打量着街道，随即又转向电梯口，盯着鱼贯而出的旅客。事情已很清楚：这盯梢绝非我脑中的臆造。

===Original===
I walked slowly to my chambers and got through the day’s work as best I could, for my mind was preoccupied with the unpleasant business in which I found myself entangled. I would have given a year’s income to be honestly quit of it, but there seemed to be no way of escape. The maddening thing was that I could do so little. There was no chance of forgetting anxiety in strenuous work. I could only wait with the patience at my command, and hope for the one chance in a thousand which I might seize. I felt miserably that it was no game for me. I had never been brought up to harry wild beasts and risk my neck twice a day at polo like Tommy Deloraine. I was a peaceful, sedentary man, a lover of a quiet life, with no appetite for perils and commotions. But I was beginning to realize that I was very obstinate.

===Chinese===
我缓步走回事务所，尽最大努力应付当天的活计，因为我的心神全被我卷入的这桩恼人勾当占去了。我宁愿拿出整年的收入，只为能干干净净地脱身，可看来并无脱身之路。最叫人抓狂的是，我能做的竟如此之少。想靠拼命工作来忘却焦虑，根本无望。我只能以我仅有的耐心去等，去盼那千载一逢、也许我能攫住的机会。我沮丧地感到，这桩差事压根不是为我这种人设的。我从小所受的教养，绝不是要去骚扰野兽、一天两次冒着摔断脖子的危险打马球——那是汤米·德洛兰的做派。我是个安分守己、案牍度日的人，偏爱宁静的生活，对危险与动荡毫无胃口。可我也开始意识到，我这个人相当执拗。

===Original===
At four o’clock I left the Temple and walked to the Embassy. I had resolved to banish the espionage from my mind, for that was the least of my difficulties.

===Chinese===
四点钟，我离开律师学院区，步行前往大使馆。我已决意把那盯梢一事从心中驱逐出去，因为那是我诸多难处里最不值一提的一桩。

===Original===
Felix gave me an hour of his valuable time. It was something that Tommy had joined Pitt-Heron, but there were other matters to be arranged in that far country. The time had come, in my opinion, to tell him the whole story.

===Chinese===
费利克斯把他宝贵的一个钟头给了我。汤米既已与皮特-赫伦会合，这便是一桩进展；可在那遥远的国度里，还有别的事情要安排。我看，时候到了，该把事情的原委对他和盘托出。

===Original===
The telling was a huge relief to my mind. He did not laugh at me as I had half feared, but took the whole thing as gravely as possible. In his profession, I fancy, he had found too many certainties behind suspicions to treat anything as trivial. The next step, he said, was to warn the Russian police of the presence of the man called Saronov and the super-butler. Happily we had materials for the description of Tuke or Routh, and I could not believe that such a figure would be hard to trace. Felix cabled again in cipher, asking that the two should be watched, more especially if there was reason to believe that they had followed Tommy’s route. Once more we got out the big map and discussed the possible ways. It seemed to me a land created by Providence for surprises, for the roads followed the valleys, and to the man who travelled light there must be many shortcuts through the hills.

===Chinese===
一吐为快，对我心里是莫大的宽慰。他并没有像我半担心的那样笑话我，而是把整件事看得郑重其事之至。我想，在他那一行里，他见过太多由疑心而坐实的确凿之事，因而不会把任何事等闲视之。他说，下一步便是要警告俄国警方，留意一个名叫萨罗诺夫（Saronov）的人，还有那位“超级管家”。所幸我们手里有描述图克（或称劳思）这个人的材料，而我也相信，长成那样的一副模样，要追踪他不会太难。费利克斯又用密码发了一封电报，请他们对这两人加以监视，尤其要提防他们有沿着汤米的路线跟上去的迹象。我们再一次摊开那大幅地图，商议种种可能的路子。在我看来，那片土地简直天生就是为出人意表而造的：道路都顺着谷地走，而对于轻装上路的人，山间必有无数捷径可寻。

===Original===
I left the Embassy before six o’clock and, crossing the Square engrossed with my own thoughts, ran full into Lumley.

I hope I played my part well, though I could not repress a start of surprise. He wore a grey morning-coat and a white top-hat and looked the image of benevolent respectability.

“Ah, Mr. Leithen,” he said, “we meet again.”

===Chinese===
六点之前，我离开了大使馆；我正穿过广场、满腹心事地出神，一头撞了个正着的，正是拉姆利。

我希望我那一刻的神色演得还算得体，虽说我不免还是吃了一惊。他穿一件灰色晨礼服，戴一顶白色大礼帽，活脱脱一副慈祥而体面的模样。

“啊，利思滕先生，”他说，“我们又见面了。”

===Original===
I murmured something about my regrets at my early departure three days ago, and added the feeble joke that I wished he would hurry on his Twilight of Civilisation, for the burden of it was becoming too much for me.

===Chinese===
我咕哝了几句，为三天前自己的提早告辞表示歉意，又加了个拙劣的玩笑，说我盼他赶紧把那部《文明的暮色》（Twilight of Civilisation）写下去，因为这担子压在我身上，已经越来越吃不消了。

===Original===
He looked me in the eyes with all the friendliness in the world. “So you have not forgotten our evening’s talk? You owe me something, my friend, for giving you a new interest in your profession.”

“I owe you much,” I said, “for your hospitality, your advice, and your warnings.”

===Chinese===
他满眼都是天下最友善的神情，直视着我的眼睛。“这么说，你还没忘记我们那天晚上的谈话？朋友，我给了你这一行新的兴味，你是欠我一份人情的。”

“我欠您的多着呢，”我说，“多谢您的款待、您的指教，还有您的告诫。”

===Original===
He was wearing his tinted glasses and peered quizzically into my face.

“I am going to make a call in Grosvenor Place,” he said, “and shall beg in return the pleasure of your company. So you know my young friend, Pitt-Heron?”

With an ingenuous countenance I explained that he had been at Oxford with me and that we had common friends.

===Chinese===
他戴着那副有色眼镜，打趣似的端详着我的脸。

“我要到格罗夫纳街（Grosvenor Place）去拜访一个人，”他说，“便也斗胆请你赏光同行，聊作回报。这么说，你认识我那位年轻朋友皮特-赫伦？”

我一脸坦然地解释说，他跟我是牛津（Oxford）的同学，我们还有共同的朋友。

===Original===
“A brilliant young man,” said Lumley. “Like you, he has occasionally cheered an old man’s solitude. And he has spoken of me to you?”

“Yes,” I said, lying stoutly. “He used to tell me about your collections.” (If Lumley knew Charles well he would find me out, for the latter would not have crossed the road for all the treasures of the Louvre.)

===Chinese===
“一个才华横溢的年轻人，”拉姆利说。“跟你一样，他偶尔也来为老朽的孤寂解解闷。那么，他跟你提起过我？”

“提过，”我说，厚着脸皮撒了个谎。“他从前常跟我说起您的收藏。”

（倘若拉姆利与查尔斯相知甚深，他定能当场拆穿我，因为后者即便为了卢浮宫（the Louvre）的全部珍宝，也不肯多过一条马路。）

===Original===
“Ah, yes, I have picked up a few things. If ever you should care to see them I should be honoured. You are a connoisseur? Of a sort? You interest me for I should have thought your taste lay in other directions than the dead things of art. Pitt-Heron is no collector. He loves life better than art, as a young man should. A great traveller our friend—the Laurence Oliphant or Richard Burton of our day.”

===Chinese===
“啊，是了，我也搜罗了那么几件。倘若你有兴致一观，我荣幸之至。你是个鉴赏家？勉强算得上？你倒引起我的兴趣了，因为我本会以为，你的趣味所在，与艺术中那些死去的事物并不相干。皮特-赫伦可不是收藏家。他爱生命胜过爱艺术，年轻人理当如此。咱们这位朋友是个了不起的旅行家——堪称我们这个时代的劳伦斯·奥利芬特（Laurence Oliphant），或理查德·伯顿（Richard Burton）。”

===Original===
We stopped at a house in Grosvenor Place, and he relinquished my arm. “Mr. Leithen,” he said, “a word from one who wishes you no ill. You are a friend of Pitt-Heron, but where he goes you cannot follow. Take my advice and keep out of his affairs. You will do no good to him, and you may bring yourself into serious danger. You are a man of sense, a practical man, so I speak to you frankly. But, remember, I do not warn twice.”

===Chinese===
我们在格罗夫纳街的一宅门前停下，他松开了我的胳膊。“利思滕先生，”他说，“这是一个对你并无恶意的人的一句话。你是皮特-赫伦的朋友，可他要去的地方，你跟不了。听我一句劝，别再插手他的事。你帮不了他什么，倒可能给自己招来大祸。你是个明白人，一个务实的人，所以我把话跟你挑明了说。不过你记着，我从不警告第二次。”

===Original===
He took off his glasses, and his light, wild eyes looked me straight in the face. All benevolence had gone, and something implacable and deadly burned in them. Before I could say a word in reply he shuffled up the steps of the house and was gone.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
他摘下了眼镜，那双浅色的、狂乱的眼睛直勾勾地逼视着我。所有的慈祥都不见了，眼中燃烧着一种难以平息、致人死命的东西。不等我答上半句话，他便拖着脚步登上门前的台阶，消失不见了。……

## V / V

### I Take a Partner / 我找了个搭档

===Original===
That meeting with Lumley scared me badly, but it also clinched my resolution. The most pacific fellow on earth can be gingered into pugnacity. I had now more than my friendship for Tommy and my sympathy with Pitt-Heron to urge me on. A man had tried to bully me, and that roused all the worst stubbornness of my soul. I was determined to see the game through at any cost.

But I must have an ally if my nerves were to hold out, and my mind turned at once to Tommy’s friend Chapman. I thought with comfort of the bluff independence of the Labour member. So that night at the House I hunted him out in the smoking-room.

===Chinese===
与拉姆利的那次会面把我吓得不轻，却也叫我下定了决心。世上最与世无争的人也能被激出斗性来。如今驱使我向前的，已不止是我对汤米的友情和对皮特-赫伦的同情。有人竟想威吓我，这一下把我骨子里最犟的那股拧劲儿全勾了出来。我横下心，无论如何也要把这盘棋看到底。

可要撑住神经，我非得有个帮手不可，我的念头当即转到了汤米的朋友查普曼身上。一想到那位工党议员那副直来直去、不倚仗人的做派，我心里便觉踏实。于是那天晚上在下议院，我到吸烟室里把他寻了出来。

===Original===
He had been having a row with the young bloods of my party that afternoon and received me ungraciously.

===Chinese===
那天下午他刚跟我党里那帮年轻公子哥儿闹了一场，见了我便没什么好脸色。

===Original===
“I’m about sick of you fellows,” he growled. (I shall not attempt to reproduce Chapman’s accent. He spoke rich Yorkshire with a touch of the drawl of the western dales.) “They went and spoiled the best speech, though I say it as shouldn’t, which this old place has heard for a twelvemonth. I’ve been workin’ for days at it in the Library. I was tellin’ them how much more bread cost under Protection, and the Jew Hilderstein started a laugh because I said kilometres for kilogrammes. It was just a slip o’ the tongue, for I had it right in my notes, and besides there furrin’ words don’t matter a curse. Then that young lord as sits for East Claygate gets up and goes out as I was gettin’ into my peroration, and he drops his topper and knocks off old Higgins’s spectacles, and all the idiots laughed. After that I gave it them hot and strong, and got called to order. And then Wattles, him as used to be as good a socialist as me, replied for the Government and his blamed Board and said that the Board thought this and the Board thought that, and was damned if the Board would stir its stumps. Well I mind the day when I was hanging on to the Board’s coattails in Hyde Park to keep it from talking treason.”

===Chinese===
“你们这帮家伙，我可真受够了，”他嚷嚷道。（查普曼的口音我就不费力去学了。他操一口浓重的约克郡话，还带着西部谷地那种拖长腔。）“他们偏偏搅黄了这破地方十二个月以来听过的最好的一篇演说——这话本不该我自己夸口。我在图书馆里对着这篇稿子磨了好几天工夫。我正给他们讲保护主义之下面包涨了多少钱，那个犹太人希尔德斯坦（Hilderstein）倒好，竟哄堂大笑起来，就因为我把‘公斤’说成了‘公里’。那不过是舌头打了个滑，我笔记里原本写得一点不错，再说那些外国字眼儿压根儿不值一提。紧接着，那个代表东克莱盖特（East Claygate）的年轻勋爵偏偏在我讲到结尾高潮时站起来往外走，他那顶大礼帽一掉，把老希金斯（Higgins）的眼镜也给碰飞了，一屋子蠢货全乐了。打那以后我可就火力全开，狠狠给了他们一顿，结果被勒令遵守秩序。接下来沃特尔斯（Wattles）——那个从前跟我一样是个呱呱叫的社会主义者的家伙——居然替政府、替他那个该死的委员会答话，说什么委员会这样想、委员会那样想，委员会死活不肯挪窝儿。我还清楚记得从前的日子，那时候我还在海德公园（Hyde Park）拽着那委员会的后衣摆，好不让它说出叛国的话来呢。”

===Original===
It took me a long time to get Chapman settled down and anchored to a drink.

“I want you,” I said, “to tell me about Routh﻿—you know the fellow I mean﻿—the ex-Union-Leader.”

===Chinese===
我费了好大工夫，才把查普曼安抚下来，让他坐定在一杯酒跟前。

“我想请你，”我说，“跟我说说劳思（Routh）的事——你知道我说的是谁——那个从前当过工会领袖的家伙。”

===Original===
At that he fairly blazed up.

“There you are, you Tories,” he shouted, causing a pale Liberal member on the next sofa to make a hurried exit. “You can’t fight fair. You hate the Unions, and you rake up any rotten old prejudice to discredit them. You can find out about Routh for yourself, for I’m damned if I help you.”

===Chinese===
一听这话，他顿时火冒三丈。

“又来了，你们这些托利党人，”他大喊大叫，吓得隔壁沙发上一个面皮白净的自由党议员匆匆逃了席。“你们从来不肯光明正大地较量。你们恨工会，就不择手段地翻出任何陈年烂芝麻的偏见来给它抹黑。要打听劳思，你们自个儿打听去，见鬼去吧，我才不帮你们。”

===Original===
I saw I could do nothing with Chapman unless I made a clean breast of it, so for the second time that day I told the whole story.

===Chinese===
我看出，若不向查普曼和盘托出，便拿他毫无办法，于是那天我第二回把事情的原委从头到尾讲了一遍。

===Original===
I couldn’t have wished for a better audience. He got wildly excited before I was half through with it. No doubt of the correctness of my evidence ever entered his head, for, like most of his party, he hated anarchism worse than capitalism, and the notion of a highly capitalised, highly scientific, highly undemocratic anarchism fairly revolted his soul. Besides, he adored Tommy Deloraine. Routh, he told me, had been a young engineer of a superior type, with a job in a big shop at Sheffield. He had professed advanced political views, and, although he had strictly no business to be there, had taken a large part in Trade Union work, and was treasurer of one big branch. Chapman had met him often at conferences and on platforms, and had been impressed by the fertility and ingenuity of his mind and the boldness of his purpose. He was the leader of the left wing of the movement, and had that gift of half-scientific, half-philosophic jargon which is dear at all times to the hearts of the half-baked. A seat in Parliament had been repeatedly offered him, but he had always declined; wisely, Chapman thought, for he judged him the type which is more effective behind the scenes.

===Chinese===
再没有比他更好的听众了。我才讲到一半，他便已激动得不行。对我所说之事是否确凿，他脑子里压根儿没生过一丝疑——因为同他党内大多数人一样，他恨无政府主义甚于恨资本主义，而一个资本高度集中、技术高度发达、又高度反民主的无政府主义，这念头本身就使他满心厌恶。何况，他对汤米·德洛兰推崇备至。他告诉我，劳思从前是个出类拔萃的年轻工程师，在谢菲尔德（Sheffield）一家大工厂里任职。他标榜激进的政治主张，尽管按规矩他压根儿没资格掺和，却在工会事务里插手颇深，还当上了一个大分会的财务主管。查普曼常在各种会议和讲台上遇见他，对他那心思之活、主意之多、图谋之大，印象极深。他是这场运动里左翼的领袖，天生有那种半科学、半哲学的切口本领——这等切口，半瓶醋的人们历来最是受用。议席曾屡次三番地奉送给他，他一概谢绝；查普曼认为谢绝得对，因为他断定劳思这种人，在幕后效力远比在台前更为得力。

===Original===
But with all his ability he had not been popular. “He was a cold-blooded, sneering devil,” as Chapman put it, “a sort of Parnell. He tyrannised over his followers, and he was the rudest brute I ever met.”

===Chinese===
可他空有一身本事，人缘却不大好。“他是个冷血、刻薄的魔鬼，”查普曼如是说，“有点儿帕内尔（Parnell）那路货色。他对追随者专制得很，而且是我这辈子见过的最粗鲁的畜生。”

===Original===
Then followed the catastrophe, in which it became apparent that he had speculated with the funds of his Union and had lost a large sum. Chapman, however, was suspicious of these losses, and was inclined to suspect that he had the money all the time in a safe place. A year or two earlier the Unions, greatly to the disgust of old-fashioned folk, had been given certain extralegal privileges, and this man Routh had been one of the chief advocates of the Unions’ claims. Now he had the cool effrontery to turn the tables on them and use those very privileges to justify his action and escape prosecution.

===Chinese===
接下来便是那场祸事——事情败露出来，他原来拿工会的公款去做投机，亏了一大笔。不过查普曼对这些亏损心存疑虑，倒倾向于怀疑那笔钱其实一直被他好好地藏在某个稳妥之处。一两年前，工会争得了若干法外特权——这事令老派人物深恶痛绝——而这个劳思，正是当年为工会主张摇旗呐喊最力者之一。如今他竟厚着脸皮倒打一耙，恰恰利用这些特权来为自己的行径辩护，逃脱了起诉。

===Original===
There was nothing to be done. Some of the fellows, said Chapman, swore to wring his neck, but he did not give them the chance. He had disappeared from England, and was generally believed to be living in some foreign capital.

===Chinese===
对此谁也无可奈何。查普曼说，有些同伙发誓要拧断他的脖子，可他没给他们这个机会。他从此在英国销声匿迹，一般都相信他隐居在某座外国都城里。

===Original===
“What I would give to be even with the swine!” cried my friend, clenching and unclenching his big fist. “But we’re up against no small thing in Josiah Routh. There isn’t a crime on earth he’d stick at, and he’s as clever as the old Devil, his master.”

===Chinese===
“我愿拿一切来换，好跟这头猪猡算清这笔账！”我的朋友大叫，攥紧又松开他那只好大的拳头。“可我们这次在约西亚·劳思（Josiah Routh）身上碰到的，绝非等闲之辈。天底下没有一桩罪是他干不出来的，而且他跟他那位主子老魔鬼一样奸滑。”

===Original===
“If that’s how you feel, I can trust you to back me up,” I said. “And the first thing I want you to do is to come and stay at my flat. God knows what may happen next, and two men are better than one. I tell you frankly, I’m nervous, and I would like to have you with me.”

Chapman had no objection. I accompanied him to his Bloomsbury lodgings, where he packed a bag, and we returned to the Down Street flat. The sight of his burly figure and sagacious face was a relief to me in the mysterious darkness where I now found myself walking.

===Chinese===
“你既这么想，我便信得过你会给我撑腰，”我说。“我要你头一件做的事，就是搬来我公寓住。天知道接下来还会出什么事，两个人总比一个人强。我跟你直说吧，我心里发虚，想有你在我身边。”

查普曼没有异议。我陪他到布卢姆斯伯里（Bloomsbury）的寓所，看着他收拾了一只提包，便一同回到唐街（Down Street）的公寓。他那一副魁梧的身板和那张精明的脸，在我如今深陷其中的那片诡秘黑暗里，着实叫我心安。

---

===Original===
Thus began my housekeeping with Chapman﻿—one of the queerest episodes in my life. He was the best fellow in the world, but I found that I had misjudged his character. To see him in the House, you would have thought him a piece of granite, with his Yorkshire bluntness and hard, downright, north-country sense. He had all that somewhere inside him, but he was also as romantic as a boy. The new situation delighted him. He was quite clear that it was another case of the strife between Capital and Labour﻿—Tommy and I standing for Labour, though he used to refer to Tommy in public as a “gilded popinjay,” and only a month before had described me in the House as a “viperous lackey of Capitalism.” It was the best kind of strife, in which you had not to meet your adversary with long-winded speeches but might any moment get a chance to pummel him with your fists.

===Chinese===
我与查普曼的同住生涯便由此开端——那是我平生最古怪的一段插曲。他是世上头等的好人，可我发觉自己先前看走了眼，错判了他的性子。在下议院里瞧见他，你会当他是一块花岗岩，带着约克郡人的生硬和北方人那种硬邦邦、直通通的见识。他骨子里确实有这些，可同时他又浪漫得像个孩子。这新局面叫他乐坏了。他满心认定这又是一场资本与劳工之间的较量——汤米和我这一边代表劳工，虽说他在人前惯常管汤米叫“镀金的花花公子”，而就在一个月前，他还在下议院里把我形容成“资本主义那条毒蛇般的走狗”呢。这种较量是最对他的胃口的那种：你不必拿长篇大论的演说去会你的对手，随时都有可能捞着机会用拳头狠狠揍他一顿。

===Original===
He made me ache with laughter. The spying business used to rouse him to fury. I don’t think he was tracked as I was, but he chose to fancy he was, and was guilty of assault and battery on one butcher’s boy, two cabbies, and a gentleman who turned out to be a bookmaker’s assistant. This side of him got to be an infernal nuisance, and I had many rows with him. Among other things, he chose to suspect my man Waters of treachery﻿—Waters, who was the son of a gardener at home, and hadn’t wits enough to put up an umbrella when it rained.

===Chinese===
他常逗得我笑岔了气。盯梢这种事最能把他激得火冒三丈。我倒不觉得有人像我那样盯他的梢，可他偏要自个儿认定有，结果犯了殴打罪——揍了一个屠户家的小伙计、两个马车夫，还有一位后来查明是赌档跑腿的先生。他这一面简直成了要命的麻烦，我俩为此吵过好几回。此外，他还偏偏疑心我那个听差沃特斯（Waters）叛变——沃特斯，老家花匠的儿子，下雨天都不见得有那份机灵劲儿去撑把伞的。

===Original===
“You’re not taking this business rightly,” he maintained one night. “What’s the good of waiting for these devils to down you? Let’s go out and down them.” And he announced his intention, from which no words of mine could dissuade him, of keeping watch on Mr. Andrew Lumley at the Albany.

His resolution led to a complete disregard of his Parliamentary duties. Deputations of constituents waited for him in vain. Of course he never got a sight of Lumley. All that happened was that he was very nearly given in charge more than once for molesting peaceable citizens in the neighbourhood of Piccadilly and Regent Street.

===Chinese===
“你这桩事办得不对路，”有一夜他坚持道。“干等着这些魔鬼来收拾你，有什么用？咱们该主动出去收拾他们。”他还宣布了一项决心——任我怎么劝也劝不住——要去奥尔巴尼（the Albany）盯安德鲁·拉姆利先生的梢。

这一决心使他全然抛开了议员的职责。选区来的代表团白白等他，等了个空。他当然连拉姆利的影儿也没见着。唯一的收获，是他不止一回差点被扭送警局——罪名是在皮卡迪利（Piccadilly）和摄政街（Regent Street）一带骚扰安分守己的良民。

===Original===
One night, on my way home from the Temple, I saw in the bills of the evening papers the announcement of the arrest of a Labour Member. It was Chapman, sure enough. At first, I feared that he had got himself into serious trouble, and was much relieved to find him in the flat in a state of blazing anger. It seemed that he had found somebody whom he thought was Lumley, for he only knew him from my descriptions. The man was in a shop in Jermyn Street, with a car waiting outside, and Chapman had﻿—politely, as he swore﻿—asked the chauffeur his master’s name. The chauffeur had replied abusively, upon which Chapman had hauled him from the driver’s seat and shaken him till his teeth rattled. The owner came out, and Chapman was arrested and taken off to the nearest police-court. He had been compelled to apologise and had been fined five pounds and costs.

===Chinese===
一天夜里，我从律师学院区（the Temple）回家的路上，看见晚报招贴上登着一条工党议员被捕的消息。果然是查普曼。起先我还怕他闯下了大祸，后来见他怒气冲冲地回了公寓，才大大松了口气。原来他认定自己找着了拉姆利——可他只凭我那几番描述认人。那人正在杰明街（Jermyn Street）的一家铺子里，门外停着一辆汽车，查普曼便——他赌咒说是客客气气地——向司机打听他主人的名姓。司机回了一嘴脏话，查普曼便把他从驾驶座上拽下来，直摇得他牙齿咯咯作响。那主人出来，查普曼便被拘捕，押往最近的治安法庭。他被迫赔了不是，还被罚了五英镑并讼费。

===Original===
By the mercy of Heaven, the chauffeur’s master was a moneylender of evil repute, so the affair did Chapman no harm. But I was forced to talk to him seriously. I knew it was no use explaining that for him to spy on the Powerhouse was like an elephant stalking a gazelle. The only way was to appeal to his incurable romanticism.

===Chinese===
亏得上天保佑，那司机的主人是个声名狼藉的放高利贷的，所以这事倒没损及查普曼半点名头。但我不得不郑重地跟他谈一谈。我深知，跟他解释他去盯动力室的梢就好比一头大象偷偷逼近一只羚羊，这话是白费唇舌。唯一的法子，只能去打动他那无可救药的浪漫心性。

===Original===
“Don’t you see,” I told him, “that you are playing Lumley’s game? He will trap you sooner or later into some escapade which will land you in jail, and where will I be then? That is what he and his friends are out for. We have got to meet cunning with cunning, and lie low till we get our chance.”

He allowed himself to be convinced, and handed over to me the pistol he had bought, which had been the terror of my life.

“All right,” he said, “I’ll keep quiet. But you promise to let me into the big scrap when it comes off.”

I promised. Chapman’s notion of the grand finale was a Homeric combat in which he would get his fill of fisticuffs.

===Chinese===
“你难道看不出，”我对他说，“你这样正中拉姆利的下怀？他迟早要诱你闹出点什么乱子，叫你蹲进大牢，到那时候我又怎么办？这正是他和他那帮人所图的。我们得以狡诈对付狡诈，按兵不动，等咱们的机会来。”

他到底被说通了，还把他买的那把手枪交了出来——那玩意儿可一直是我心头之患。

“行，”他说，“我消停着就是。可你得答应我，等那场大仗打起来的时候，让我也插一手。”

我答应了。在查普曼的心目中，那场压轴大戏便是一场荷马式（Homeric）的大搏斗，好让他痛痛快快地打个够。

===Original===
He was an anxiety, but all the same he was an enormous comfort. His imperturbable cheerfulness and his racy talk were the tonics I wanted. He had plenty of wisdom, too. My nerves were getting bad those days, and, whereas I had rarely touched the things before, I now found myself smoking cigarettes from morning till night. I am pretty abstemious, as you know, but I discovered, to my horror, that I was drinking far too many whiskeys-and-sodas. Chapman knocked me off all that and got me back to a pipe and a modest nightcap.

He did more, for he undertook to put me in training. His notion was that we should win in the end by superior muscles. He was a square, thickset fellow, who had been a good middleweight boxer. I could box a bit myself, but I improved mightily under his tuition. We got some gloves, and used to hammer each other for half an hour every morning. Then might have been seen the shameful spectacle of a rising barrister with a swollen lip and a black eye arguing in court, and proceeding of an evening to his country’s legislature, where he was confronted from the opposite benches by the sight of a Leader of the People in the same vulgar condition.

===Chinese===
他是个叫人操心的人物，可同时他又是莫大的安慰。他那不动声色的好兴致、那有滋有味的谈吐，正是我所需要的补剂。他也有的是见识。那段日子我的神经越来越糟，从前我难得沾这些玩意儿，如今却从早到晚一支接一支地抽着纸烟。诸位知道，我向来颇有节制，可我惊恐地发觉，自己掺了苏打水的威士忌喝得实在太多。查普曼把这一切都给我戒了，让我重新叼上烟斗，睡前至多浅酌一杯。

他做的还不止于此——他竟担当起为我操练体魄的差事来。他的主意是，我们最终要靠更胜一筹的筋骨取胜。他是个敦实粗壮的汉子，从前是个不错的中量级拳手。我自己也会那么两下子，可经他一调教，进步斐然。我们弄来几副拳套，每天早晨对揍上半个钟头。于是人们便有幸目睹这样一桩丢人的光景：一位前程似锦的律师，肿着嘴唇、乌青着眼在法庭上慷慨陈词，到了晚上又去出席国家的立法机构——而在对面的议席上，赫然坐着一位同样鼻青脸肿的人民领袖，与他遥遥相对。

===Original===
In those days I wanted all the relief I could get, for it was a beastly time. I knew I was in grave danger, so I made my will and went through the other doleful performances consequent on the expectation of a speedy decease. You see, I had nothing to grip on, no clear job to tackle, only to wait on the off-chance, with an atmosphere of suspicion thickening around me. The spying went on﻿—there was no mistake about that﻿—but I soon ceased to mind it, though I did my best to give my watchers little satisfaction. There was a hint of bullying about the spying. It is disconcerting at night to have a man bump against you and look you greedily in the face.

===Chinese===
那段日子，我恨不得抓住一切能到手的慰藉，因为那实在是一段难熬的日子。我深知自己处境凶险，便立下了遗嘱，又把预期死期将至所连带的一连串丧气事一一办妥。诸位明白，我手里毫无可凭依的东西，没有一桩明确的差事可做，只能守株待兔地干等着，四周猜疑的空气却越来越浓。盯梢仍在继续——这一点错不了——可不久我便不大放在心上了，不过我仍尽力不给那些盯梢的半点满足。这种盯梢带着几分恫吓的意味。夜里走在路上，忽然有个人撞你一下，随即贪婪地盯着你的脸瞧，这滋味实在叫人心慌。

===Original===
I did not go again to Scotland Yard, but one night I ran across Macgillivray in the club.

He had something of profound interest to tell me. I had asked about the phrase, the “Powerhouse.” Well, he had come across it in the letter of a German friend, a private letter, in which the writer gave the results of his inquiries into a curious affair which a year before had excited Europe.

===Chinese===
我没再上苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）去，不过有一夜，我在俱乐部里撞见了麦克吉利夫雷（Macgillivray）。

他有一桩极有意思的事要告诉我。我曾向他打听过“动力室”这个说法。原来，他在一位德国朋友的来信里——一封私信——碰见了它，写信人在信中给出了他对一桩离奇事件调查的结果，那桩事件一年前曾轰动过整个欧洲。

===Original===
I have forgotten the details, but it had something to do with the Slav States of Austria and an Italian Students’ Union, and it threatened at one time to be dangerous. Macgillivray’s correspondent said that in some documents which were seized he found constant allusion to a thing called the *Krafthaus*, evidently the headquarters-staff of the plot. And this same word, *Krafthaus*, had appeared elsewhere﻿—in a sonnet of a poet-anarchist who shot himself in the slums of Antwerp, in the last ravings of more than one criminal, in the extraordinary testament of Professor M﻿—﻿—, of Jena, who, at the age of thirty-seven, took his life after writing a strange, mystical message to his fellow citizens.

===Chinese===
详情我已经忘了，只记得大约跟奥地利的斯拉夫诸邦（the Slav States of Austria）和一个意大利学生联合会（Italian Students’ Union）有些瓜葛，有一阵子还险些闹出大乱子。麦克吉利夫雷的通信人说，在被查获的若干文件里，他发现一再提到一个叫 *Krafthaus*（德语，意即“动力室”）的东西，显然是那场阴谋的参谋本部。而同一个词，*Krafthaus*，还在别处出现过——在一首出自一位诗人无政府主义者的十四行诗里，此人后来在安特卫普（Antwerp）的贫民窟里饮弹自尽；在不止一个罪犯临终的谵语里；在那份离奇的遗嘱里——遗嘱的主人，是耶拿（Jena）的 M——教授（Professor M——），他在三十七岁那年，给同胞们写下了一封古怪而神秘的遗书之后，便自尽了。

===Original===
Macgillivray’s correspondent concluded by saying that, in his opinion, if this *Krafthaus* could be found, the key would be discovered to the most dangerous secret organisation in the world. He added that he had some reason to believe that the motive power of the concern was English.

===Chinese===
麦克吉利夫雷的通信人最后说，依他之见，只要能找到这个 *Krafthaus*，便能找到通向世上最危险的那个秘密组织的钥匙。他又补了一句：他有若干理由相信，这个组织背后的原动力，是英国人。

===Original===
“Macgillivray,” I said, “you have known me for some time, and I fancy you think me a sober and discreet person. Well, I believe I am on the edge of discovering the secret of your *Krafthaus*. I want you to promise me that if in the next week I send you an urgent message you will act on it, however fantastic it seems. I can’t tell you more. I ask you to take me on trust, and believe that for anything I do I have tremendous reasons.”

He knit his shaggy grey eyebrows and looked curiously at me. “Yes, I’ll go bail for your sanity. It’s a good deal to promise, but if you make an appeal to me I will see that it is met.”

===Chinese===
“麦克吉利夫雷，”我说，“你认识我也有一阵子了，我想你总把我看作一个稳重谨慎的人。好吧，我相信我已濒临揭开你这个 *Krafthaus* 的秘密。我要你答应我：往后的这一个礼拜里，如果我给你送去一道紧急口信，不管那口信听起来多么荒诞离奇，你都要照办。详情我没法跟你多说。我只求你信我这一回，相信我无论做什么，都有极其重大的缘由。”

他皱起那两道浓密的灰眉毛，好奇地打量着我。“好吧，我愿为你神志清醒作保。这诺言许得可不轻，可你若当真向我求援，我一定让它落到实处。”

===Original===
Next day I had news from Felix. Tuke and the man called Saronov had been identified. If you are making inquiries about anybody it is fairly easy to find those who are seeking for the same person, and the Russian police, in tracking Tommy and Pitt-Heron, had easily come on the two gentlemen who were following the same trail. The two had gone by Samarkand, evidently intending to strike into the hills by a shorter route than the main road from Bokhara. The frontier posts had been warned, and the stalkers had become the stalked.

===Chinese===
第二天我得了费利克斯（Felix）的消息。图克（Tuke）和那个叫萨罗诺夫（Saronov）的人已被查明身份。你若在打听某个人的下落，便不难顺藤摸瓜地找到同样在找寻此人的那些人；俄国警察一路追踪汤米和皮特-赫伦，便轻而易举地撞上了这两位紧追同一条线索的先生。这两人取道撒马尔罕（Samarkand）前行，显然是想从那儿取一条比从布哈拉（Bokhara）来的大路更近的捷径，插进山里去。各处边卡都已接到了警戒，于是那跟踪别人的，反倒成了被跟踪的。

===Original===
That was one solid achievement, at any rate. I had saved Pitt-Heron from the worst danger, for first I had sent him Tommy, and now I had put the police on guard against his enemies. I had not the slightest doubt that enemies they were. Charles knew too much, and Tuke was the man appointed to reason with him, to bring him back, if possible; or, if not﻿—As Chapman had said, the ex-Union leader was not the man to stick at trifles.

===Chinese===
无论如何，这总算是一桩实打实的成果。我把皮特-赫伦从最凶险的境地里救了出来：我先给他送去了汤米，如今又使警察对他那些对头有了防备。我丝毫不疑心那些人便是他的对头。查尔斯知道得太多了，而图克正是被派来“开导”他、尽可能把他带回去的人；万一带不回去——那么，正如查普曼所言，这位前工会领袖可不是个会在小事上踌躇手软的人。

===Original===
It was a broiling June, the London season was at its height, and I had never been so busy in the Courts before. But that crowded and garish world was little more than a dream to me. I went through my daily tasks, dined out, went to the play, had consultations, talked to my fellows, but all the while I had the feeling that I was watching somebody else perform the same functions. I believe I did my work well, and I know I was twice complimented by the Court of Appeal.

===Chinese===
那是个酷热的六月，伦敦的社交旺季正酣，而我在法庭上的事务之繁忙，更是前所未有。可那个熙熙攘攘、浮华热闹的世界，于我而言不过是一场梦罢了。我照旧做着日常的活计，外出赴宴，去看戏，与人磋商案情，与同僚闲谈，可整个时间里，我总有一种感觉，仿佛自己在袖手旁观着另一个人做着这些同样的事。我相信我的活计干得不坏，我知道我还得过上诉法院（Court of Appeal）两次当庭褒奖。

===Original===
But my real interests were far away. Always I saw two men in the hot glens of the Oxus, with the fine dust of the loess rising in yellow clouds behind them. One of these men had a drawn and anxious face, and both rode hard. They passed by the closes of apricot and cherry and the green, watered gardens, and soon the Oxus ceased to flow wide among rushes and water-lilies and became a turbid hill-stream. By-and-by the roadside changed, and the horses of the travellers trod on mountain turf, crushing the irises and marigolds and thyme. I could feel the free air blowing from the roof of the world, and see far ahead the snowy saddle of the pass which led to India.

===Chinese===
可我真正的心思却在遥远的彼方。我眼前总是浮现出两个人，走在阿姆河（the Oxus）两岸酷热的峡谷里，身后扬起黄土（loess）的细尘，卷成一团团黄色的云雾。这两人中有一个面容憔悴忧急，两人都策马疾行。他们从一片片杏林、樱桃林和一座座浇得青翠的果园旁经过，不久，阿姆河便不再在芦苇与睡莲之间宽阔地漫流，而成了一条浑浊的山涧。渐渐地，路边的景致变了，旅人的马蹄踏上了山间的草皮，踩碎了鸢尾、金盏花和百里香。我能感觉到世界屋脊吹来的那种自由的风，也能望见远处前方那道通往印度的、马鞍形的雪白山口。

===Original===
Far behind the riders I saw two others, and they chose a different way, now over waterless plateaux, now in rugged nullahs. They rode the faster and their route was the shorter. Sooner or later they must catch up the first riders, and I knew, though how I could not tell, that death would attend the meeting.

===Chinese===
在那两个骑手远远的身后，我又看见另外两个人，他们另择了一条路，忽而越过无水的台地，忽而穿行于崎岖的干沟（nullahs）之中。他们策马更快，所走的路也更近。迟早他们要追上前面的两个骑手，而我虽不知其所以然，却知道死神定要出席那场相遇。

===Original===
I, and only I, sitting in London, four thousand miles away, could prevent disaster. The dream haunted me at night, and often, walking in the Strand or sitting at a dinner-table, I have found my eyes fixed clearly on the shining upland with the thin white mountains at the back of it, and the four dots, which were men, hurrying fast on their business.

===Chinese===
唯有我，唯有坐在四千英里之外的伦敦城里的我，才能阻止这场灾祸。这梦魇夜里缠绕着我，便是白天，走在河滨大道（the Strand）上，或是坐在饭桌跟前，我也常常发觉自己的两眼清清楚楚地凝望着那片闪光的高地——高地背后衬着一道道纤瘦的白色群山，四个小点，那是四个人，正匆匆地赶着他们各自的要紧事。

===Original===
One night I met Lumley. It was at a big political dinner given by the chief of my party in the House of Lords﻿—fifty or sixty guests, and a blaze of stars and decorations. I sat near the bottom of the table, and he was near the top, sitting between a famous General and an ex-Viceroy of India. I asked my right-hand neighbour who he was, but he could not tell me. The same question to my left-hand neighbour brought an answer:

===Chinese===
一天夜里，我又遇见了拉姆利。那是在上议院（House of Lords）我党党魁所设的一场盛大政治宴会上——五六十位宾客，满堂勋章绶带，光华耀眼。我坐在长桌靠末尾的位置，他却坐在靠上首的地方，介于一位名将军和一位前任印度总督之间。我问右手边的邻座那人是谁，他说不上来。同样的问题问左手边的邻座，倒问出了一个答复：

===Original===
“It is old Lumley. Have you never met him? He doesn’t go out much, but he gives a man’s dinner now and then which are the best in London. No. He’s not a politician, though he favours our side, and I expect has given a lot to our funds. I can’t think why they don’t make him a Peer. He’s enormously rich and very generous, and the most learned old fellow in Britain. My Chief”﻿—my neighbour was an Undersecretary﻿—“knows him, and told me once that if you wanted any out-of-the-way bit of knowledge you could get it by asking Lumley. I expect he pulls the strings more than anybody living. But he scarcely ever goes out, and it’s a feather in our host’s cap to have got him tonight. You never see his name in the papers, either. He probably pays the Press to keep him out, like some of those millionaire fellows in America.”

===Chinese===
“那是老拉姆利。你从没见过他？他不大出门，可偶尔也办一场纯男子的宴会，那是伦敦头一份的。不。他不是搞政治的，不过他偏着我们这一边，我估摸着给咱的党费也捐过不少。我想不通他们怎么不封他个贵族。他阔得很，出手又大方，还是不列颠最有学问的老先生。我的顶头上司”——我这邻座是位副国务大臣（Undersecretary）——“认识他，有一回跟我说，你想要哪一桩偏僻冷门的学问，去问拉姆利便得了。我估摸着，天底下暗中牵线操纵大局的，没一个人比得过他。可他几乎从不露面，今晚咱们这位主人能把他请了来，可真算得上一桩值得夸耀的事。你也从不见他的名字见报。他多半是花钱买通了报界，好把他从报纸上剔出去——就跟美国那些大富翁一个路数。”

===Original===
I watched him through dinner. He was the centre of the talk at his end of the table. I could see the blue ribbon bulging out on Lord Morecambe’s breast as he leaned forward to question him. He was wearing some foreign orders, including the Legion of Honour, and I could hear in the pause of conversation echoes of his soft, rich voice. I could see him beaming through his glasses on his neighbours, and now and then he would take them off and look mildly at a speaker. I wondered why nobody realised, as I did, what was in his light wild eyes.

===Chinese===
整顿饭我都在观察他。他是他那一端长桌上谈话的中心。我看得见莫克姆勋爵（Lord Morecambe）俯身向他请教时，胸前那枚蓝色绶带鼓凸出来的样子。他佩着几枚外国勋章，其中有荣誉军团勋章（Legion of Honour），在谈话的间歇里，我能听见他那柔和醇厚的嗓音的回响。我看得见他隔着眼镜，笑盈盈地望着左右的邻座，间或摘下眼镜，温和地望一望说话的人。我纳闷儿，为什么除了我，竟没有一个人识破他那双浅色的、狂乱的眼睛里藏着的是什么。

===Original===
The dinner, I believe, was excellent and the company was good, but down at my end I could eat little, and I did not want to talk. Here in this pleasant room, with servants moving softly about and a mellow light on the silver from the shaded candles, I felt the man was buttressed and defended beyond my reach. A kind of despairing hatred gripped me when I looked his way. For I was always conscious of that other picture﻿—the Asian desert, Pitt-Heron’s hunted face, and the grim figure of Tuke on his trail. That, and the great secret wheels of what was too inhuman to be called crime moving throughout the globe under this man’s hand.

===Chinese===
那晚的菜肴，我相信，是极精致的，座上宾客也很是体面，可在我那末尾的位子上，我几乎食不下咽，也不愿开口说话。在这间舒适的厅堂里，仆役们轻手轻脚地往来穿梭，罩着灯罩的烛火把柔和的光投在银器上——在这儿，我感到那个人被层层拱卫、重重庇护，远在我够不着的地方。每当我朝他那边望去，一种绝望的恨意便攫住我。因为我心头始终悬着另一幅图景——亚洲的荒漠、皮特-赫伦那张被人追猎的脸，以及紧紧咬住他不放的图克那阴森的身影。还有那架巨大的、秘密的齿轮——它太不近人情，简直算不上是犯罪——正在此人的操纵下，于整个地球上运转不息。

===Original===
There was a party afterwards, but I did not stay. No more did Lumley, and for a second I brushed against him in the hall at the foot of the big staircase.

He smiled on me affectionately.

===Chinese===
宴会之后还有一场招待会，我没留下。拉姆利也没留下，有那么一瞬，我在大厅里那座大楼梯的梯下与他擦肩而过。

他满面慈爱地朝我笑了笑。

===Original===
“Have you been dining here? I did not notice you.”

“You had better things to think of,” I said. “By the way, you gave me good advice some weeks ago. It may interest you to hear that I have taken it.”

===Chinese===
“你今晚也在这儿用饭？我倒没瞧见你。”

“您有更要紧的事要去想，”我说。“对了，几周前您给过我一个好忠告。您也许有兴趣听听——我已经照办了。”

===Original===
“I am so glad,” he said softly. “You are a very discreet young man.”

But his eyes told me that he knew I lied.

===Chinese===
“那太好了，”他柔声说。“你真是个十分知趣的年轻人。”

可他的眼睛告诉我，他知道我在撒谎。

## VI / VI

### The Restaurant in Antioch Street / 安提阿街的餐馆

===Original===
I was working late at the Temple next day, and it was nearly seven before I got up to go home. Macgillivray had telephoned to me in the afternoon saying he wanted to see me, and suggesting dinner at the Club, and I had told him I should come straight there from my Chambers. But just after six he had rung me up again and proposed another meeting place.

"I've got some very important news for you, and want to be quiet. There's a little place where I sometimes dine—Rapaccini's, in Antioch Street. I'll meet you there at half-past seven."

I agreed, and sent a message to Chapman at the flat, telling him I would be out to dinner. It was a Wednesday night, so the House rose early. He asked me where I was dining, and I told him, but I did not mention with whom. His voice sounded very cross, for he hated a lonely meal.

===Chinese===
第二天我在律师学院区（the Temple）加班到很晚，等我起身回家时已近七点。下午麦克吉利夫雷（Macgillivray）给我打过电话，说想见我，提议在俱乐部共进晚餐，我告诉他我会从事务所直接过去。可刚过六点他又打来电话，另换了一处见面地点。

"我有十分重要的消息要告诉你，想找个清静的地方。有家小店我有时去吃饭——安提阿街（Antioch Street）的拉帕奇尼餐馆（Rapaccini's）。我七点半在那儿等你。"

我答应了，又给公寓里的查普曼（Chapman）捎了个口信，告诉他我不回去吃晚饭了。那晚是星期三，所以下议院散会得早。他问我在哪儿用餐，我告诉了他，却没有说跟谁一起。听口气他很不痛快，因为他最讨厌一个人孤零零地吃饭。

===Original===
It was a hot, still night, and I had had a heavy day in Court, so heavy that my private anxieties had almost slipped from my mind. I walked along the Embankment, and up Regent Street towards Oxford Circus. Antioch Street, as I had learned from the Directory, was in the area between Langham Place and Tottenham Court Road. I wondered vaguely why Macgillivray should have chosen such an out-of-the-way spot, but I knew him for a man of many whims.

The street, when I found it, turned out to be a respectable little place, boardinghouses and architects' offices, with a few antiquity shops and a picture-cleaner's. The restaurant took some finding, for it was one of those discreet establishments, common enough in France, where no edibles are displayed in the British fashion, and muslin half-curtains deck the windows. Only the doormat, lettered with the proprietor's name, remained to guide the hungry.

I gave a waiter my hat and stick, and was ushered into a garish dining-room, apparently full of people. A single violinist was discoursing music from beside the grill. The occupants were not quite the kind one expects to find in an eating-house in a side street. The men were all in evening dress with white waistcoats, and the women looked either demimondaines or those who follow their taste in clothes. Various eyes looked curiously at me as I entered. I guessed that the restaurant had, by one of those odd freaks of Londoners, become for a moment the fashion.

===Chinese===
那是个闷热无风的夜晚，我在法庭上熬了繁重的一天，重得连我私下里的那些忧虑都几乎被挤出了脑海。我沿着滨河大道走，再折上摄政街朝牛津圆场去。我从地名录上查到，安提阿街在朗豪坊和托特纳姆法院路之间的那片地方。我心下隐约纳闷麦克吉利夫雷为什么挑了这么个偏僻角落，可我也知道他这人向来古怪念头多。

等我找到那条街，才发觉是个体面的去处，开着些供膳公寓和建筑师事务所，还有几家古董铺子和一间画作修复店。那家餐馆却不大好找，因为它是那种颇为低调的馆子——在法国屡见不鲜，却不学英国人那套把吃食陈列出来的做法，窗上只挂着半截细洋纱帘子。唯有门垫上印着店主姓氏的字母，还能给饥肠辘辘的来客指点迷津。

我把帽子和手杖交给一个侍者，被引进一间灯红酒绿的餐厅，里头看上去坐满了人。一个拉小提琴的独自站在烤炉旁奏着乐曲。在座的客人不太像人们在这种背街小馆里会遇见的那一类。男人们一律穿着礼服、套着白马甲，女人们看上去要么是风尘女子，要么便是仿着她们品味打扮的人。我进门时，许多双眼睛好奇地向我投来。我猜，这馆子大约是由于伦敦人那种古怪的流行癖好，一时之间竟成了时髦去处。

===Original===
The proprietor met me halfway up the room. He might call himself Rapaccini, but he was obviously a German.

"Mr. Geelvrai," he nodded. "He has engaged a private room. Vill you follow, sir?"

A narrow stairway broke into the wall on the left side of the dining-room. I followed the manager up it and along a short corridor to a door which filled its end. He ushered me into a brightly lit little room where a table was laid for two.

"Mr. Geelvrai comes often here," said the manager. "He vill be late—sometimes. Everything is ready, sir. I hope you vill be pleased."

===Chinese===
店主在餐厅中央迎上我来。他爱怎么自称拉帕奇尼就自称吧，可他分明是个德国人。

"吉尔弗莱（Geelvrai）先生，"他点点头。"他订了一间雅座。请随我来，先生？"

餐厅左侧的墙上开着一道窄窄的楼梯。我跟着经理上去，沿一条短短的走廊走到尽头一扇门前。他把我让进一间灯光明亮的小屋，里面摆着一张铺好的两人桌。

"吉尔弗莱先生常来这儿，"经理说。"他会晚些——有时候。一切都预备好了，先生。希望您中意。"

===Original===
It looked inviting enough, but the air smelt stuffy. Then I saw that, though the night was warm, the window was shut and the curtains drawn. I pulled back the curtains, and, to my surprise, saw that the shutters were closed.

"You must open these," I said, "or we'll stifle."

The manager glanced at the window. "I vill send a waiter," he said, and departed. The door seemed to shut with an odd click.

===Chinese===
桌上的摆设倒是颇为诱人，可屋里的空气闻着闷得很。我这才发现，虽说夜里天暖，窗子却关着，窗帘也拉得严严实实。我把窗帘拉开，却诧异地看见百叶窗也是合拢的。

"你得把这些打开，"我说，"不然非把人闷死不可。"

经理朝窗子瞟了一眼。"我差个侍者来，"说完便出去了。门关上时似乎发出了一声古怪的咔哒响。

===Original===
I flung myself down in one of the armchairs, for I was feeling pretty tired. The little table beckoned alluringly, for I was also hungry. I remember there was a mass of pink roses on it. A bottle of champagne, with the cork loose, stood in a wine-cooler on the sideboard, and there was an unopened bottle beside it. It seemed to me that Macgillivray, when he dined here, did himself rather well.

The promised waiter did not arrive, and the stuffiness was making me very thirsty. I looked for a bell, but could not see one. My watch told me it was now a quarter to eight, but there was no sign of Macgillivray. I poured myself out a glass of champagne from the opened bottle, and was just about to drink it when my eye caught something in a corner of the room.

===Chinese===
我一屁股坐进一张扶手椅里，因为我着实累了。那张小桌子诱人地向我招手，因为我也饿了。我记得桌上有一大簇粉红的玫瑰。餐柜上的冰桶里立着一瓶香槟，软木塞已经松开，旁边还放着另一瓶没开封的。我暗想，麦克吉利夫雷在这儿吃饭时，倒是挺讲究排场的。

说好的侍者迟迟不来，而那股闷气让我渴得要命。我四下找铃铛，却一个也看不见。表上已是差一刻八点，却仍不见麦克吉利夫雷的影子。我从那瓶开了封的香槟里给自己倒了一杯，正要喝下去，眼角却瞥见屋子角落里有一件东西。

===Original===
It was one of those little mid-Victorian corner tables—I believe they call them "what-nots"—which you will find in any boardinghouse, littered up with photographs and coral and "Presents from Brighton." On this one stood a photograph in a shabby frame, and I thought I recognised it.

I crossed the room and picked it up. It showed a man of thirty, with short side-whiskers and ill-fitting jaw and a drooping moustache. The duplicate of it was in Macgillivray's cabinet. It was Mr. Routh, the ex-Union leader.

There was nothing very remarkable about that, after all, but it gave me a nasty shock. The room now seemed a sinister place, as well as intolerably close. There was still no sign of the waiter to open the window, so I thought I would wait for Macgillivray downstairs.

But the door would not open. The handle would not turn. It did not seem to be locked, but rather to have shut with some kind of patent spring. I noticed that the whole thing was a powerful piece of oak, with a heavy framework, very unlike the usual flimsy restaurant doors.

===Chinese===
那是种维多利亚中期的小型墙角架——我想人们管它叫"杂物架"——随便哪家供膳公寓里都能见到，上头乱七八糟地堆着照片、珊瑚摆设和"布莱顿留念"。这一只上面立着一张相片，镶着个寒碜的镜框，我觉得自己认得出来。

我穿过屋子把它拿起来。照片上是个三十来岁的男人，留着短短的连鬓胡子、一副不齐整的下巴和两撇耷拉的小胡子。这张相片的副本就在麦克吉利夫雷的档案柜里。那是劳思（Routh）先生，那位前工会领袖。

说到底，这倒也没什么大不了的，可我还是狠狠吃了一惊。这间屋子此刻显得阴森起来，又闷得叫人难以忍受。仍旧不见有侍者来开窗，我便想下楼去等麦克吉利夫雷。

可门打不开了。把手转不动。看样子不像是锁上了，倒像是被某种特制的弹簧自动扣死的。我留意到整扇门是一块结实的橡木板，装着沉重的框架，全不似寻常餐馆那种单薄的门扇。

===Original===
My first instinct was to make a deuce of a row and attract the attention of the diners below. I own I was beginning to feel badly frightened. Clearly, I had got into some sort of trap. Macgillivray's invitation might have been a hoax, for it is not difficult to counterfeit a man's voice on the telephone. With an effort I forced myself into calmness. It was preposterous to think that anything could happen to me in a room not thirty feet from where a score or two of ordinary citizens were dining. I had only to raise my voice to bring inquirers.

Yes, but above all things I did not want a row. It would never do for a rising lawyer and a Member of Parliament to be found shouting for help in an upper chamber of a Bloomsbury restaurant. The worst deduction would be drawn from the open bottle of champagne. Besides, it might be all right after all. The door might have got stuck. Macgillivray at that very moment might be on his way up.

So I sat down and waited. Then I remembered my thirst, and stretched out my hand to the glass of champagne.

But at that instant I looked towards the window, and set down the wine untasted.

===Chinese===
我的头一个念头是大闹一场，好引得楼下用餐的人注意。我得承认，我开始害怕起来了。很显然，我已落进了某种圈套。麦克吉利夫雷的邀约说不定是个骗局，因为在电话里冒充一个人的嗓音并不困难。我费了一番力气才强自镇定下来。想想看，在这间离二三十个普通市民用餐处不出三十英尺的屋子里，能出什么事，实在是荒唐。我只要提高嗓门，自会有人上来查看。

话是这么说，可我比什么都更不愿意闹出动静。一个前程看好的律师兼下议院议员，被人发现在布卢姆斯伯里一家餐馆楼上的房间里大喊救命，这成何体统。那瓶开了封的香槟，必然招来最不堪的揣测。何况，说不定到头来什么事也没有。门也许只是卡住了。说不定麦克吉利夫雷此刻正上楼来呢。

于是我坐下等。接着我想起口渴，便伸手去够那杯香槟。

可就在那一刹那，我朝窗子望去，随即把那杯酒原封不动地放下了。

===Original===
It was a very odd window. The lower end was about flush with the floor, and the hinges of the shutters seemed to be only on one side. As I stared, I began to wonder whether it was a window at all.

Next moment my doubts were solved. The window swung open like a door, and in the dark cavity stood a man.

Strangely enough, I knew him. His figure was not one that is readily forgotten.

"Good evening, Mr. Docker," I said. "Will you have a glass of champagne?"

===Chinese===
那是扇极古怪的窗。窗的下沿几乎与地板齐平，百叶窗的铰链似乎只装在一侧。我盯着它看，渐渐怀疑起它究竟算不算是一扇窗了。

下一刻，我的疑团便解开了。那扇窗像门一样旋开了，黑黢黢的洞口里站着一个人。

说来也怪，我竟认得他。他那副身形可不是轻易能忘得了的。

"晚上好，多克（Docker）先生，"我说。"来杯香槟？"

===Original===
A year before, on the South Eastern Circuit, I had appeared for the defence in a burglary case. Criminal law was not my province, but now and then I took a case to keep my hand in, for it is the best training in the world for the handling of witnesses. This case had been peculiar. A certain Bill Docker was the accused, a gentleman who bore a bad reputation in the eyes of the police.
