The evidence against him was strong, but it was more or less tainted, being chiefly that of two former accomplices—a proof that there is small truth in the proverbial honour among thieves. It was an ugly business, and my sympathies were with the accused, for though he may very well have been guilty, yet he had been the victim of a shabby trick. Anyhow, I put my back into the case, and after a hard struggle got a verdict of "Not guilty." Mr. Docker had been kind enough to express his appreciation of my efforts, and to ask, in a hoarse whisper, how I had "squared the old bird," meaning the Judge. He did not understand the subtleties of the English law of evidence.

===Chinese===
一年前，在东南巡回法庭上，我曾为一起入室盗窃案担任辩护律师。刑法本非我的本行，可我偶尔也接一桩案子练练手，因为那是世上训练如何对付证人最好的法子。这桩案子颇为特别。被告是一个名叫比尔·多克（Bill Docker）的人，在警方眼里是个声名狼藉的家伙。对他不利的证据很扎实，却多多少少带着污点，因为主要出自两个昔日同伙之口——足见"盗亦有道"那句老话里实在没多少真理。这是桩难看的案子，我的同情心在被告这边，因为纵使他极可能确有其罪，却也是一桩卑劣伎俩的受害者。不管怎样，我全力以赴地办这案子，一番苦战之后争来了一个"无罪"的裁决。多克先生倒是承情，对我的努力颇为感念，还压着嗓子问我究竟怎样"摆平了那老东西"——他指的是法官。他实在不懂英国证据法里的那些微妙之处。

===Original===
He shambled into the room, a huge, hulking figure of a man, with the thickness of chest which, under happier circumstances, might have made him a terror in the prize-ring. His features wore a heavy scowl, which slowly cleared to a flicker of recognition.

"By God, it's the lawyer-chap," he muttered.

I pointed to the glass of champagne.

"I don't mind if I do," he said. " 'Ere's health!" He swallowed the wine at a gulp, and wiped his mouth on his sleeve. " 'Ave a drop yourself, guvnor," he added. "A glass of bubbly will cheer you up."

===Chinese===
他拖着脚步走进屋来，是个高大笨重的汉子，胸膛厚实，若是换个好些的处境，凭这副体格在拳台上足以叫人闻风丧胆。他满脸凶相地沉着一副怒容，慢慢地舒展开来，闪过一丝认出人的神色。

"老天爷，是那位律师老兄，"他嘟囔道。

我指了指那杯香槟。

"那我就不客气了，"他说。"祝你健康！"他把那杯酒一口吞下，拿袖子一抹嘴。"你自己也来一口吧，老爷，"他又说。"来杯冒泡的，提提神。"

===Original===
"Well, Mr. Docker," I said, "I hope I see you fit." I was getting wonderfully collected now that the suspense was over.

"Pretty fair, sir. Pretty fair. Able to do my day's work like an honest man."

"And what brings you here?"

"A little job I'm on. Some friends of mine wants you out of the road for a bit, and they've sent me to fetch you. It's a bit of luck for you that you've struck a pal. We needn't 'ave no unpleasantness, seein' we're both what you might call men of the world."

"I appreciate the compliment," I said. "But where do you propose to take me?"

"Dunno. It's some lay near the Docks. I've got a motorcar waitin' at the back of the 'ouse."

"But supposing I don't want to go?"

"My orders hadmit no hexcuse," he said solemnly. "You're a sensible chap, and can see that in a scrap I could down you easy."

===Chinese===
"那么，多克先生，"我说，"我看你气色不错。"悬虑一消，我反倒格外镇定起来了。

"还算好，先生。还算好。还能像个正经人那样干一天活。"

"那你到这儿来干什么？"

"我手上有点小差事。我有几个朋友想让你在道上消失一阵子，就派我来接你。算你走运，撞上了个老交情。咱们犯不着伤和气，既然彼此都是所谓见过世面的人。"

"承蒙夸奖，"我说。"可你打算把我带到哪儿去？"

"说不准。是码头区附近的什么名堂。我在屋子后头备了辆汽车。"

"可我要是不想去呢？"

"我的命令里头不容许有任何借口，"他一本正经地说。"你是个明白人，看得出来真动起手来，我轻轻松松就能把你放倒。"

===Original===
"Very likely," I said. "But, man, you must be mad to talk like that. Downstairs there is a dining-room fall of people. I have only to lift my voice to bring the police."

"You're a kid," he said scornfully. "Them geesers downstairs are all in the job. That was a flat-catching rig to get you up here so as you wouldn't suspect nothing. If you was to go down now—which you ain't going to be allowed to do—you wouldn't find a blamed soul in the place. I must say you're a bit softer than I 'oped after the 'andsome way you talked over the old juggins with the wig at Maidstone."

Mr. Docker took the bottle from the wine-cooler and filled himself another glass.

===Chinese===
"很可能，"我说。"可老兄，你这么说话准是疯了。楼底下有间餐厅坐满了人。我只要嚷一嗓子，就能把警察招来。"

"你是个毛孩子，"他鄙夷地说。"楼底下那些老家伙全是一伙的。那不过是骗你上楼的圈套，好叫你不起疑心。你这会儿要是下楼去——反正也不会让你下楼——保管连个鬼影都找不着。我得说，你在梅德斯通（Maidstone）把那个戴假发的老傻瓜哄得服服帖帖的，那副漂亮手段我可见识过，没想到你这人比我想的还软。"

多克先生从冰桶里拎出酒瓶，又给自己斟了一杯。

===Original===
It sounded horribly convincing. If I was to be kidnapped and smuggled away Lumley would have scored half a success. Not the whole, for, as I swiftly reflected, I had put Felix on the track of Tuke, and there was every chance that Tommy and Pitt-Heron would be saved. But for myself it looked pretty black. The more my scheme succeeded the more likely the Powerhouse would be to wreak its vengeance on me once I was spirited from the open-air world into its dark labyrinths.

I made a great effort to keep my voice even and calm.

===Chinese===
这话听着可怕地可信。若我当真被绑架、被偷偷运走，拉姆利便已赢了一半。不是全胜，因为——我飞快地盘算着——我已让费利克斯（Felix）去追踪图克（Tuke）了，汤米和皮特-赫伦十有八九能得救。可对我自己而言，前景相当不妙。我的计策越是得手，一旦我从这光天化日的世界被掳进动力室（the Powerhouse）那黑暗的迷宫，它就越是要在我身上泄愤。

我竭力使自己的声音保持平稳镇定。

===Original===
"Mr. Docker," I said. "I once did you a good turn. But for me you might be doing time now instead of drinking champagne like a gentleman. Your pals played you a pretty low trick, and that was why I stuck out for you. I didn't think you were the kind of man to forget a friend."

"No more I am," said he. "The man who says Bill Docker would go back on a pal is a liar."

"Well, here's your chance to pay your debts. The men who employ you are my deadly enemies, and want to do me in. I'm not a match for you. You're a stronger fellow and can drag me off and hand me over to them. But if you do I'm done for. Make no mistake about that. I put it to you as a decent fellow. Are you going to go back on the man who has been a good friend to you?"

===Chinese===
"多克先生，"我说。"我曾帮过你一个忙。要不是我，你这会儿多半在蹲大牢，而不是像个上等人似的喝香槟。你的同伙对你使了挺下作的招，我正是为这才为你据理力争的。我不把你当作那种会忘恩的朋友。"

"我确实不是，"他说。"谁要说比尔·多克会出卖朋友，那他就是个骗子。"

"那好，现在正是你还债的机会。雇你的那些人是我的死敌，想要我的命。我打不过你。你比我壮，大可以把我拖走，交到他们手里。可你若真那么做，我就完了。这点你千万别搞错。我把你当作一个体面人，把话对你挑明了。你当真要出卖那个待你不薄的朋友？"

===Original===
He shifted from one foot to another with his eyes on the ceiling. He was obviously in difficulties. Then he tried another glass of champagne.

"I dursn't, guv'nor. I dursn't let you go. Them I work for would cut my throat as soon as look at me. Besides, it ain't no good. If I was to go off and leave you there'd be plenty more in this 'ouse as would do the job. You're up against it, guv'nor. But take a sensible view and come with me. They don't mean you no real 'arm. I'll take my Bible oath on it. Only to keep you quiet for a bit, for you've run across one of their games. They won't do you no 'urt if you speak 'em fair. Be a sport and take it smiling-like—"

"You're afraid of them," I said.

"Yuss. I'm afraid. Black afraid. So would you be if you knew the gents. I'd rather take on the whole Rat Lane crowd—you know them as I mean—on a Saturday night, when they're out for business, than go back to my gents and say as 'ow I had shirked the job."

He shivered. "Good Lord, they'd freeze the 'eart out of a bull-pup."

===Chinese===
他把重心从一只脚换到另一只脚，眼睛盯着天花板。他显然左右为难。于是他又端起一杯香槟。

"我不敢哪，老爷。我不敢放你走。我替他们干活的那些主子，看一眼就会抹我的脖子。再说了，放不放都没用。就算我拍拍屁股走人、把你撂下，这宅子里多的是别人能来干这活。你是逃不掉了，老爷。还是看开点，跟我走吧。他们并不想真的伤你。我可以拿《圣经》发誓。不过是叫你安生一阵子，因为你撞破了他们一桩买卖。你只要好好跟他们说话，他们不会动你一根毫毛。痛快点儿，笑呵呵地认了吧——"

"你怕他们，"我说。

"是。我怕。怕得要死。你要是认得那几位爷，你也得怕。我宁愿在星期六晚上单挑整条拉特巷（Rat Lane）那帮人——你懂我指谁——他们那会儿正出来找茬的时候，也不愿回去跟我的主子们说我临阵脱逃了。"

他打了个寒颤。"老天爷，他们能把一条斗牛小狗的心都冻硬了。"

===Original===
"You're afraid," I said slowly. "So you're going to give me up to the men you're afraid of to do as they like with me. I never expected it of you, Bill. I thought you were the kind of lad who would send any gang to the devil before you'd go back on a pal."

"Don't say that," he said almost plaintively. "You don't 'alf know the 'ole I'm in." His eye seemed to be wandering, and he yawned deeply.

Just then a great noise began below. I heard a voice speaking, a loud peremptory voice. Then my name was shouted: "Leithen! Leithen! Are you there?"

There could be no mistaking that broad Yorkshire tongue. By some miracle Chapman had followed me and was raising Cain downstairs.

===Chinese===
"你怕了，"我缓缓地说。"所以你就打算把我交给你所害怕的那些人，任他们为所欲为。我万万没想到你会这样，比尔。我原以为你是那种汉子，宁可把任何一帮人都打发到魔鬼那儿去，也不肯出卖一个朋友。"

"别这么说，"他几乎是哀求地说。"你根本不知道我陷进了什么坑里。"他的眼神似乎有些涣散，重重地打了个哈欠。

就在这时，楼下响起一阵大动静。我听见一个声音在说话，又响又威严。接着有人高喊我的名字："利思滕！利思滕！你在吗？"

那浓重的约克郡口音是决不会听错的。不知怎的，查普曼竟一路跟了来，正在楼下大闹天宫。

===Original===
My heart leaped with the sudden revulsion. "I'm here," I yelled. "Upstairs. Come up and let me out!"

Then I turned with a smile of triumph to Bill.

"My friends have come," I said. "You're too late for the job. Get back and tell your masters that."

He was swaying on his feet, and he suddenly lurched towards me. "You come along. By God, you think you've done me. I'll let you see."

His voice was growing thick and he stopped short. "What the 'ell's wrong with me?" he gasped. "I'm goin' all queer. I …"

He was like a man far gone in liquor, but three glasses of champagne would never have touched a head like Bill's. I saw what was up with him. He was not drunk, but drugged.

"They've doped the wine," I cried. "They put it there for me to drink it and go to sleep."

===Chinese===
我的心脏因这骤然的逆转而猛地一跳。"我在这儿！"我高喊。"在楼上。快上来放我出去！"

随即我带着得胜的微笑转向比尔。

"我的朋友来了，"我说。"你这趟活儿晚了。回去告诉你主子吧。"

他身子直晃，冷不丁朝我扑来。"你跟我走。老天，你以为你得手了。我让你瞧瞧。"

他的嗓音渐渐含混，猛地打住了。"我……我这是怎么了？"他喘着气说。"我浑身发飘。我……"

他那副模样就像烂醉如泥的人，可三杯香槟决上不了比尔这样一颗脑袋。我明白他是怎么了。他不是喝醉了，而是被下了药。

"他们在酒里下了迷药，"我叫道。"他们把它摆在那儿，就是给我喝了好叫我睡过去的。"

===Original===
There is always something which is the last straw to any man. You may insult and outrage him and he will bear it patiently, but touch the quick in his temper and he will turn. Apparently for Bill drugging was the unforgivable sin. His eye lost for a moment its confusion. He squared his shoulders and roared like a bull.

"Doped, by God," he cried. "Who done it?"

"The men who shut me in this room. Burst that door and you will find them."

He turned a blazing face on the locked door and hurled his huge weight on it. It cracked and bent but the lock and hinges held. I could see that sleep was overwhelming him and that his limbs were stiffening, but his anger was still strong enough for another effort. Again he drew himself together like a big cat and flung himself on the woodwork. The hinges tore from the jambs and the whole outfit fell forward into the passage in a cloud of splinters and dust and broken plaster.

===Chinese===
对任何人来说，总有那么一件事是压垮骆驼的最后一根稻草。你尽可以羞辱他、欺凌他，他会耐心地忍下去，可一旦触动了他脾气的要害，他就会发作。对比尔而言，下药显然是不可饶恕的罪过。他的眼神一时褪去了迷惘。他端起双肩，像公牛一般怒吼起来。

"下了药，老天，"他嚷道。"谁干的？"

"把我关在这屋里的人。撞开那扇门，你就能找到他们。"

他带着一张暴怒的脸转向那扇紧锁的门，把硕大的身躯狠狠砸了上去。门板嘎吱作响、变了形，可锁和铰链仍顶住了。我看得出睡意正压倒他，他的四肢正在发僵，但他的怒火还足以支撑他再使一把劲。他再度像一头大猫那样蓄满力气，朝那门板扑过去。铰链从门框上撕裂开来，整扇门连框向前栽进走廊，扬起一阵木屑、灰尘和碎石灰的烟雾。

===Original===
It was Mr. Docker's final effort. He lay on the top of the wreckage he had made, like Samson among the ruins of Gaza, a senseless and slumbering hulk.

I picked up the unopened bottle of champagne—it was the only weapon available—and stepped over his body. I was beginning to enjoy myself amazingly.

As I expected, there was a man in the corridor, a little fellow in waiter's clothes, with a tweed jacket instead of a dress coat. If he had a pistol I knew I was done, but I gambled upon the disinclination of the management for the sound of shooting.

He had a knife, but he never had a chance to use it. My champagne bottle descended on his head and he dropped like a log.

===Chinese===
这便是多克先生的最后一搏了。他趴在自己造出的那堆残骸之上，犹如参孙（Samson）横陈于迦萨（Gaza）的废墟之中，成了一具人事不省、沉沉昏睡的庞然大物。

我抄起那瓶尚未开封的香槟——这是手边唯一的武器——跨过他的身子。我开始觉得这事儿妙不可言了。

不出所料，走廊里有个人，是个穿侍者衣裳的小个子，上身却罩着件花呢外套而不是礼服。他若是有枪，我知道我就完了，但我赌经营者不愿听见枪响。

他有把刀，却根本没机会使出来。我的香槟瓶照他脑袋落下去，他像段木头似的直挺挺栽倒在地。

===Original===
There were men coming upstairs—not Chapman, for I still heard his hoarse shouts in the dining-room. If they once got up they could force me back through that hideous room by the door through which Docker had come, and in five minutes I should be in their motorcar.

There was only one thing to do. I jumped from the stairhead right down among them. I think there were three, and my descent toppled them over. We rolled in a wild, whirling mass and cascaded into the dining-room, where my head bumped violently on the parquet.

I expected a bit of a grapple, but none came. My wits were pretty woolly, but I managed to scramble to my feet. The heels of my enemies were disappearing up the staircase. Chapman was pawing my ribs to discover if there were any bones broken. There was not another soul in the room except two policemen who were pushing their way in from the street.

===Chinese===
有人正上楼来——不是查普曼，因为我仍听见他在餐厅里粗着嗓子嚷。他们一旦上来，就能从多克进来的那扇门把我硬拽回那间可怖的屋子，不出五分钟我就会落进他们的汽车里。

只有一件事可做。我从楼梯口一跃而下，直直跳进他们当中。我估摸有三个人，我这一砸把他们全扑倒了。我们乱作一团、打着旋儿滚成一堆，瀑布般地冲进了餐厅，我的脑袋重重地磕在镶花地板上。

我原以为免不了一阵扭打，却没有。我的脑袋还晕乎乎的，可我挣扎着爬了起来。敌人的脚后跟正消失在楼梯顶端。查普曼正在摸我的肋骨，查看可有骨头断了。屋里除了两个正从街上挤进来的警察，再没别人。

===Original===
Chapman was flushed and breathing heavily: his coat had a big split down the seams at the shoulder, but his face was happy as a child's.

I caught his arm and spoke in his ear. "We've got to get out of this at once. How can we square these policemen? There must be no inquiry and nothing in the papers. Do you hear?"

"That's all right," said Chapman. "These bobbies are friends of mine, two good lads from Wensleydale. On my road here I told them to give me a bit of law and follow me, for I thought they might be wanted. They didn't come too soon to spoil sport, for I've been knocking furriners about for ten minutes. You seem to have been putting up a tidy scrap yourself."

"Let's get home first," I said, for I was beginning to think of the bigger thing.

===Chinese===
查普曼涨红了脸，喘得厉害：他的外套从肩头那道缝线处裂开好大一条口子，可他的脸却像孩子一样快活。

我一把抓住他的胳膊，凑到他耳边说。"我们得马上离开这儿。这些警察怎么打点？绝不能有什么调查，也不能见报。你听见没有？"

"包在我身上，"查普曼说。"这两个条子是我的朋友，文斯利代尔（Wensleydale）来的两个好小伙。我来的路上叫他们给我行个方便、跟着我，因为我估摸着兴许用得着。他们来得不算太早、没耽误好戏，我已经把那帮外国佬揍了足足十分钟。看样子你自己也打得挺利索。"

"先回家再说，"我说，因为我开始盘算起更大的事来。

===Original===
I wrote a chit for Macgillivray which I asked one of the constables to take to Scotland Yard. It was to beg that nothing should be done yet in the business of the restaurant, and above all that nothing should get into the papers. Then I asked the other to see us home. It was a queer request for two able-bodied men to make on a summer evening in the busiest part of London, but I was taking no chances. The Powerhouse had declared war on me, and I knew it would be war without quarter.

I was in a fever to get out of that place. My momentary lust of battle had gone, and every stone of that building seemed to me a threat. Chapman would have liked to spend a happy hour rummaging through the house, but the gravity of my face persuaded him. The truth is I was bewildered. I could not understand the reason of this sudden attack. Lumley's spies must long ago have told him enough to connect me with the Bokhara business. My visits to the Embassy alone were sufficient proof. But now he must have found out something new, something which startled him, or else there had been wild doings in Turkestan.

===Chinese===
我给麦克吉利夫雷写了张便条，托其中一位警员送到苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）。信里恳请餐馆这桩事暂且不要动作，尤其不能走漏半点风声到报纸上去。然后我请另一位送我们回家。两个身强力壮的大男人，在夏日夜晚、伦敦最热闹的地段，提出这样的要求，着实古怪，可我不敢冒半点险。动力室已经向我宣了战，我知道这将是一场不留情面的战争。

我急不可耐地要离开那地方。方才那一阵短暂的斗兴已经消散，那栋建筑的每一块砖石在我看来都像是一个威胁。查普曼本想痛痛快快地把那宅子搜个把钟头，可我脸上凝重的神色说服了他。实情是，我满心惶惑。我弄不懂这次突袭的缘由。拉姆利的眼线想必早把足以把我同布哈拉那桩事联系起来的情形报给了他。单凭我几度出入大使馆，便已是铁证。可如今他一定是又查出了什么新的动静，什么叫他吃惊的动静，要不然，便是突厥斯坦（Turkestan）那边出了大乱子。

===Original===
I won't forget that walk home in a hurry. It was a fine July twilight. The streets were full of the usual crowd, shopgirls in thin frocks, promenading clerks, and all the flotsam of a London summer. You would have said it was the safest place on earth. But I was glad we had the policeman with us, who at the end of one beat passed us on to his colleague, and I was glad of Chapman. For I am morally certain I would never have got home alone.

The queer thing is that there was no sign of trouble till we got into Oxford Street. Then I became aware that there were people on those pavements who knew all about me. I first observed it at the mouth of one of those little dark side-alleys which run up into mews and small dingy courts. I found myself being skilfully edged away from Chapman into the shadow, but I noticed it in time and butted my way back to the pavement. I couldn't make out who the people were who hustled me. They seemed nondescripts of all sorts, but I fancied there were women among them.

===Chinese===
回家的那一路，我一时半会儿忘不了。那是七月里一个晴好的黄昏。街上照旧满是行人，穿薄裙子的女店员、散步的职员，还有伦敦夏天一切杂七杂八的人。你准会说这是天下最安全的地方。可我庆幸身边有个警察，他在一段巡区的尽头把我们交接给他的同僚，我也庆幸有查普曼在。因为我十拿九稳，独自一人是断然到不了家的。

奇怪的是，直到我们走进牛津街，才看出要有麻烦的苗头。那时我才觉察，那些人行道上有一些人对我的一切了如指掌。我头一回注意到这事，是在一条幽暗小巷的口子上——那种通向马厩巷和灰扑扑小院落的小巷。我发觉自己正被人巧妙地从查普曼身边往阴影里挤，但我及时察觉，用肩膀硬顶回了人行道上。我辨不清那些推搡我的是什么人。他们像是各色各样的三教九流，但我隐约觉得里头有女人。

===Original===
This happened twice, and I got wary, but I was nearly caught before we reached Oxford Circus. There was a front of a big shop rebuilding, and the usual wooden barricade with a gate. Just as we passed it there was a special throng on the pavement and I, being next the wall, got pushed against the gate. Suddenly it gave and I was pressed inward. I was right inside before I realised my danger, and the gate was closing. There must have been people there, but I could see nothing in the gloom.

It was no time for false pride. I yelled to Chapman and the next second his burly shoulder was in the gap. The hustlers vanished and I seemed to hear a polite voice begging my pardon.

After that Chapman and I linked arms and struck across Mayfair. But I did not feel safe till I was in the flat with the door bolted.

===Chinese===
这样的事发生了两回，我警觉起来，可还没到牛津圆场我就险些着了道。有一家大商铺正在翻修门面，照例围着木板栅栏，还开了一道门。我们经过时，人行道上恰好涌来一股特别的人流，我靠着墙这一侧，被挤到了那道门上。门忽然一让，我被推了进去。等我意识到危险时，人已在里面了，门正在合拢。那里面必定有人，可我在昏暗中什么也看不见。

这当口顾不得什么体面了。我朝查普曼大喊，下一秒他粗壮的肩膀便挤进了门缝。那些推搡的人霎时散去，我似乎听见一个客气的声音在请我原谅。

此后查普曼和我挽起胳膊，穿过梅费尔（Mayfair）而去。可直到我进了公寓、插上门闩，我才觉出安全。

===Original===
We had a long drink and I stretched myself in an armchair, for I was as tired as if I had come out of a big game of Rugby football.

"I owe you a good deal, old man," I said. "I think I'll join the Labour Party. You can tell your fellows to send me their whips. What possessed you to come to look for me?"

The explanation was simple. I had mentioned the restaurant in my telephone message, and the name had awakened a recollection in Chapman's mind. He could not fix it at first, but by and by he remembered that the place had cropped up in the Routh case. Routh's London headquarters had been at the restaurant in Antioch Street. As soon as he remembered this he got into a taxi and descended at the corner of the street, where by sheer luck he fell in with his Wensleydale friends.

===Chinese===
我们狠狠灌了一大杯酒，我在扶手椅里伸展开四肢，因为我累得简直像刚打完一场激烈的橄榄球。

"我欠你一份大人情，老兄，"我说。"我看我要加入工党了。你尽管告诉你的伙计们，把他们的督导令给我寄来。你究竟哪根筋搭错了，会跑来找我？"

事情的原委很简单。我在电话口信里提到了那家餐馆，这个名字唤起了查普曼记忆中的一桩旧事。他起初想不起来，可渐渐记起那地方曾在劳思案里出现过。劳思在伦敦的据点，就在安提阿街的那家餐馆。他一想起这事，立刻钻进一辆出租车，在街角下了车，在那儿纯属机缘巧合地遇上了他文斯利代尔的老乡。

===Original===
He said he had marched into the restaurant and found it empty, but for an ill-favoured manager, who denied all knowledge of me. Then fortunately he chose to make certain by shouting my name, and heard my answer. After that he knocked the manager down, and was presently assaulted by several men whom he described as "furrin' muck." They had knives, of which he made very little, for he seems to have swung a table as a battering ram and left sore limbs behind him.

He was on the top of his form. "I haven't enjoyed anything so much since I was a lad at school," he informed me. "I was beginning to think your Powerhouse was a washout, but Lord! it's been busy enough tonight. This is what I call life!"

My spirits could not keep pace with his. The truth is that I was miserably puzzled—not afraid so much as mystified. I couldn't make out this sudden dead-set at me. Either they knew more than I bargained for or I knew far too little.

"It's all very well," I said, "but I don't see how this is going to end. We can't keep up the pace long. At this rate it will be only a matter of hours till they get me."

===Chinese===
他说他大步迈进那家餐馆，发现里面空无一人，只有一个面目可憎的经理，矢口否认知道我的下落。幸而这时他决定喊我的名字确认一下，于是听见了我的回应。此后他一拳把那经理打翻在地，紧接着便遭到几条汉子的围攻——他把他们叫作"外国渣滓"。他们带着刀子，他却没怎么放在眼里，因为他似乎抡起一张桌子当作撞城锤，留下一地喊疼的胳膊腿。

他正处在兴头的顶点。"打从我还是个学生娃起，就没这么痛快过，"他告诉我。"我本来还开始想你那动力室是个银样镴枪头呢，可老天！今晚它可够忙活的。这才叫过日子！"

我的兴致却跟不上他。实情是，我苦恼地困惑着——与其说是害怕，不如说是摸不着头脑。我弄不明白这突如其来的穷追猛打。要么是他们知道的远比我以为的多，要么就是我知道的实在太少。

"话是不错，"我说，"可我看不出这事怎么收场。我们撑不了多久的。照这架势，他们逮住我不过是几个钟头的事。"

===Original===
We pretty well barricaded ourselves in the flat, and, at his earnest request, I restored to Chapman his revolver. Then I got the clue I had been longing for.

It was about eleven o'clock, while we were sitting smoking, when the telephone bell rang. It was Felix who spoke.

"I have news for you," he said. "The hunters have met the hunted and one of the hunters is dead. The other is a prisoner in our hands. He has confessed."

===Chinese===
我们简直把公寓设成了堡垒，又应他再三请求，把左轮手枪还给了查普曼。随后，我便得到了我翘首以盼的线索。

大约十一点钟光景，我们正坐着抽烟，电话铃响了。是费利克斯打来的。

"我有消息给你，"他说。"猎人遇上了猎物，猎人死了一个。另一个做了我们的阶下囚。他已经招供了。"

===Original===
It had been black murder in intent. The frontier police had shadowed the two men into the cup of a glen where they met Tommy and Pitt-Heron. The four had spoken together for a little, and then Tuke had fired deliberately at Charles and had grazed his ear. Whereupon Tommy had charged him and knocked the pistol from his hand. The assailant had fled, but a long shot from the police on the hillside had toppled him over. Tommy had felled Saronov with his fists, and the man had abjectly surrendered. He had confessed, Felix said, but what the confession was he did not know.

===Chinese===
蓄意的是一桩彻头彻尾的谋杀。边防警察一路盯梢那两人，跟到一个山谷的洼地里，他们就在那儿遇上了汤米和皮特-赫伦。四人交谈了片刻，随后图克蓄意向查尔斯开了一枪，擦伤了他的耳朵。汤米当即朝他扑去，一拳打掉了他手里的手枪。开枪那人拔腿就跑，可山坡上的警察一记远射便把他撂倒了。汤米用拳头揍倒了萨罗诺夫（Saronov），那人乖乖地束手就擒。他已经招供了，费利克斯说，但供词的内容是什么，他却不得而知。

## VII / VII

### I Find Sanctuary / 寻得避难所

===Original===
My nervousness and indecision dropped from me at the news. I had won the first round, and I would win the last, for it suddenly became clear to me that I had now evidence which would blast Lumley. I believed that it would not be hard to prove his identity with Pavia and his receipt of the telegram from Saronov; Tuke was his creature, and Tuke’s murderous mission was his doing. No doubt I knew little and could prove nothing about the big thing, the Powerhouse, but conspiracy to murder is not the lightest of criminal charges. I was beginning to see my way to checkmating my friend, at least so far as Pitt-Heron was concerned. Provided﻿—and it was a pretty big proviso﻿—that he gave me the chance to use my knowledge.

===Chinese===
听到这消息，我的紧张与犹豫一下子全消了。我已赢了第一回合，最后一回合我也要赢，因为我忽然看清了：我手里如今握着能把拉姆利打得粉碎的证据。我相信，要证明他与帕维亚（Pavia）是同一人、并曾收到萨罗诺夫（Saronov）发来的电报，并非难事；图克（Tuke）是他的爪牙，图克那桩杀人差事正是他一手促成。那桩大买卖——动力室——我固然所知甚少、什么也证明不了，但阴谋杀人这条罪名，在刑律之中也绝非等闲。我开始看清一条将死我这位「朋友」的路子，至少在皮特-赫伦这桩事上可以做到。前提是——而且这个前提相当要紧——他得给我施展这番见识的机会。

===Original===
That I foresaw, was going to be the difficulty. What I knew now Lumley had known hours before. The reason of the affair at Antioch Street was now only too clear. If he believed that I had damning evidence against him﻿—and there was no doubt he suspected it﻿—then he would do his best to stop my mouth. I must get my statement lodged in the proper quarter at the earliest possible moment.

===Chinese===
我预感到，难就难在这里。我此刻所知的，拉姆利在几个钟头前就知道了。安提阿街（Antioch Street）那场变故的缘由，如今再清楚不过。他若相信我手里攥着能定他罪的铁证——而毫无疑问，他已有所怀疑——那他必将竭力封我的口。我必须尽早把我的陈述送交到该管的人手里。

===Original===
The next twenty-four hours, I feared, were going to be too sensational for comfort. And yet I cannot say that I was afraid. I was too full of pride to be in a funk. I had lost my awe of Lumley through scoring a point against him. Had I known more I should have been less at my ease. It was this confidence which prevented me doing the obvious safe thing﻿—ringing up Macgillivray, telling him the gist of my story, and getting him to put me under police protection. I thought I was clever enough to see the thing through myself. And it must have been the same overconfidence which prevented Lumley getting at me that night. An organisation like his could easily have got into the flat and done for us both. I suppose the explanation is that he did not yet know how much I knew and was not yet ready to take the last steps in silencing me.

===Chinese===
接下来这二十四小时，我担心会闹得太过惊心动魄，叫人不得安生。然而我不能说自己害怕。我满心骄傲，顾不上胆怯。既然胜了他一着，我对拉姆利那点敬畏也就烟消云散了。倘若我再多知几分，反倒不会这般心安。正是这份自信，害我没去做那桩明摆着最稳妥的事——给麦克吉利夫雷打电话，把事情的大要告诉他，请他派警察保护我。我以为凭自己的机灵，足以把这事应付过去。想必也正是同样一份过分自信，使拉姆利那晚没能对我下手。像他那般的组织，要闯进我这公寓、把我和查普曼一并除掉，本非难事。我想，缘故只在于他那时还不知道我究竟知道多少，也尚未下决心走那封口的最后一步。

===Original===
I sat up till the small hours, marshalling my evidence in a formal statement and making two copies of it. One was destined for Macgillivray and the other for Felix, for I was taking no risks. I went to bed and slept peacefully and was awakened as usual by Waters. My man slept out, and used to turn up in the morning about seven. It was all so normal and homely that I could have believed my adventures of the night before a dream. In the summer sunlight the ways of darkness seemed very distant. I dressed in excellent spirits and made a hearty breakfast.

===Chinese===
我一直坐到后半夜，把证据整理成一份正式陈述，并誊了两份副本。一份预备交给麦克吉利夫雷，另一份给费利克斯，因为我不想冒半点风险。我上了床，睡得安稳，一早照例被沃特斯（Waters）叫醒。我那男仆不住在公寓里，惯常清晨七点上下上门。一切如此寻常、如此居家，我几乎要以为昨夜的种种历险不过是一场梦。夏日的阳光之下，黑暗中那些勾当仿佛远在天边。我兴致极好地穿戴停当，饱饱地吃了一顿早饭。

===Original===
Then I gave the docile Chapman his instructions. He must take the document to Scotland Yard, ask to see Macgillivray, and put it into his hands. Then he must ring me up at once at Down Street and tell me that he had done this. I had already telephoned to my clerk that I would not be at the Temple that day.

===Chinese===
接着我向那百依百顺的查普曼交代差事。他得把这份文件送到苏格兰场，求见麦克吉利夫雷，亲手交到他手上。然后立刻打电话到唐街告诉我事情办妥了。我已经事先打电话给我的文书，说那天我不到律师学院区去了。

===Original===
It seems a simple thing to travel less than a mile in the most frequented part of London in broad daylight, and perform an easy act like carrying a letter; but I knew that Lumley’s spies would be active, and would connect Chapman sufficiently with me to think him worth following. In that case there might be an attempt at violence. I thought it my duty to tell him this, but he laughed me to scorn. He proposed to walk, and he begged to be shown the man who would meddle with him. Chapman after last night was prepared to take on all comers. He put my letter to Macgillivray in his inner pocket, buttoned his coat, crushed down his felt hat on his head, and defiantly set forth.

===Chinese===
在伦敦最热闹的地段、大白日里走不上区区一英里路、办一桩送信这般轻松的差事，听来似乎再简单不过；可我知道拉姆利的眼线定会四处活动，而且会把查普曼与我联系得足以认定他值得跟踪。果真如此，便可能动粗。我觉得有义务把这话告诉他，他却把我好生嗤笑了一通。他打算走着去，还说倒要看看有哪个敢招惹他。经过昨夜那一遭，查普曼正摩拳擦掌，谁来都奉陪。他把那封写给麦克吉利夫雷的信塞进内袋，扣好外套，把毡帽往头上一扣，雄赳赳地出门去了。

===Original===
I expected a message from him in half an hour, for he was a rapid walker. But the half hour passed, then the three-quarters, and nothing happened. At eleven I rang up Scotland Yard, but they had no news of him.

===Chinese===
我本指望半个钟头内便有他的回话，因为他走路向来飞快。可半个钟头过去了，三刻钟也过去了，毫无动静。十一点我打电话到苏格兰场，那边也没有他的消息。

===Original===
Then I became miserably anxious, for it was clear that some disaster had overtaken my messenger. My first impulse was to set out myself to look for him, but a moment’s reflection convinced me that that would be playing into the enemy’s hands. For an hour I wrestled with my impatience, and then a few minutes after twelve I was rung up by St. Thomas’s Hospital.

===Chinese===
这下我可苦闷焦急起来，因为显然我的信使已遭了什么祸事。我头一个念头是自己出门去找他，可略一思忖便明白，那无异于正中敌人下怀。我整整一个钟头强压着焦躁，到了十二点过几分，圣托马斯医院（St. Thomas’s Hospital）给我打来了电话。

===Original===
A young doctor spoke, and said that Mr. Chapman had asked him to tell me what had happened. He had been run down by a motorcar at the corner of Whitehall﻿—nothing serious﻿—only a bad shake and some scalp wounds. In a day or so he would be able to leave.

===Chinese===
接电话的是一位年轻医生，他说查普曼先生托他转告我事情的经过。他在白厅（Whitehall）街角被一辆汽车撞倒——伤得不重——只是狠狠震了一下，头上磕破了几处。一两天后便能出院。

===Original===
Then he added what drove the blood from my heart. “Mr. Chapman personally wished me to tell you,” he said, “that the letter has gone.” I stammered some reply asking his meaning. “He said he thinks,” I was told, “that, while he was being assisted to his feet, his pocket was picked and a letter taken. He said you would know what he meant.”

===Chinese===
接着他加上一句，把我的血都抽凉了。「查普曼先生特意要我转告您，」他说，「信没了。」我结结巴巴地追问究竟。「他说他认为，」我被告知，「在他被人扶起来的时候，有人摸了他的口袋，拿走了一封信。他说您会明白他的意思。」

===Original===
I knew only too well what he meant. Lumley had got my statement, and realised precisely how much I knew and what was the weight of evidence against him. Before he had only suspected, now he knew. He must know, too, that there would be a copy somewhere which I would try to deliver. It was going to be harder than I had fancied to get my news to the proper ears, and I had to anticipate the extreme of violence on the part of my opponents.

===Chinese===
我岂止明白他的意思。拉姆利已拿到了我的陈述，一清二楚地知道了我知道多少、对他不利的证据分量几何。从前他只是怀疑，如今他知道了。他也一定明白，别处必还有一份副本，我会设法送出。要想把这消息送到该管的人耳中，比我想象的要难，而我不得不防备对手使出最极端的暴力。

===Original===
The thought of the peril restored my coolness. I locked the outer door of my flat, and telephoned to the garage where I kept my car, bidding Stagg call for me at two o’clock precisely. Then I lit a pipe and strove to banish the whole business from my thoughts, for fussing would do me no good.

===Chinese===
想到身处险境，我反倒冷静下来。我锁上公寓的外门，打电话到停放我汽车的修车厂，吩咐斯塔格（Stagg）两点整来接我。然后我点上烟斗，竭力把整件事从脑子里赶出去，因为瞎操心于事无补。

===Original===
Presently it occurred to me to ring up Felix and give him some notion of the position. But I found that my telephone was now broken and connection was impossible. The spoken as well as the written word was to be denied me. That had happened in the last half hour and I didn’t believe it was by accident. Also my man Waters, whom I had sent out on an errand after breakfast, had never returned. The state of siege had begun.

===Chinese===
过了一阵，我想不如给费利克斯打个电话，让他大致知道我的处境。可我发现我这电话此刻已经坏了，怎么也接不通。口信也好、书信也好，都要从我手里被夺走。这事就发生在过去半个钟头里，我不信是出于偶然。再者，我早饭过后打发出去办事的沃特斯，也一去没有回来。围困已然开始。

===Original===
It was a blazing hot midsummer day. The water-carts were sprinkling Piccadilly, and looking from my window I could see leisurely and elegant gentlemen taking their morning stroll. A florist’s cart full of roses stood below me in the street. The summer smell of town﻿—a mixture of tar, flowers, dust and patchouli﻿—rose in gusts through the hot air. It was the homely London I knew so well, and I was somehow an exile from it. I was being shepherded into a dismal isolation, which, unless I won help, might mean death. I was cool enough now, but I will not deny that I was miserably anxious. I cursed my false confidence the night before. By now I might have had Macgillivray and his men by my side. As it was I wondered if I should ever see them.

===Chinese===
那是个酷热的仲夏日。洒水车正在皮卡迪利（Piccadilly）洒水，我从窗里望出去，看得见悠闲优雅的绅士们正在晨间散步。楼下街面停着一辆花贩的车，满载着玫瑰。都市的夏日气息——柏油、花卉、尘土与广藿香混在一处——一阵阵随着热气升腾上来。这是我再熟悉不过的、亲切的伦敦，而此刻我竟莫名成了它的流亡者。我正被驱赶进一种阴惨的孤立之中，若不能求得外援，那孤立便意味着死。我现在倒够冷静的了，但我不否认，我心里苦闷地焦灼着。我咒骂自己昨夜那副虚假的自信。换作稳当的处置，这会儿我身边早该有麦克吉利夫雷和他的人马了。如今我却只怕再也没机会见到他们。

===Original===
I changed into a flannel suit, lunched off sandwiches and a whisky and soda, and at two o’clock looked for Stagg and my car. He was five minutes late, a thing which had never happened before. But I never welcomed anything so gladly as the sight of that car. I had hardly dared to hope that it would reach me.

===Chinese===
我换上一身法兰绒西服，午饭拿三明治和一杯威士忌苏打发过去，两点钟便等着斯塔格和我的车。他迟到了五分钟，这是从未有过的事。可我一生从未像瞧见那辆车那样高兴过。我几乎不敢指望它还能到我跟前。

===Original===
My goal was the Embassy in Belgrave Square, but I was convinced that if I approached it directly I should share the fate of Chapman. Worse, for from me they would not merely snatch the letter. What I had once written I could write again, and if they wished to ensure my silence it must be by more drastic methods. I proposed to baffle my pursuers by taking a wide circuit round the western suburbs of London, returning to the Embassy when I thought the coast clear.

===Chinese===
我的目的地是贝尔格雷夫广场（Belgrave Square）的大使馆，可我确信，若直奔过去，必将落得和查普曼一样的下场。更糟，因为他们不会只从我手里抢走那封信。我既写过一遍，就能再写一遍，他们若要确保我闭嘴，非得用更决绝的法子不可。我打算先绕着伦敦西郊兜一个大圈子，把追踪者甩开，等觉得风头过去了，再折回大使馆。

===Original===
It was a tremendous relief to go down the stairs and emerge into the hot daylight. I gave Stagg his instructions, and lay back in the closed car with a curious fluttering sense of anticipation. I had begun the last round in the wild game. There was a man at the corner of Down Street who seemed to peer curiously at the car. He was doubtless one of my watchers.

===Chinese===
走下楼梯、踏入那炎热的日光之中，真有说不出的如释重负。我向斯塔格交代了路线，在密闭的车厢里向后一靠，心里怀着一种古怪的、扑腾着的期待。这场荒唐赌局的最后一回合，已经开场。唐街街角站着一个男人，似乎好奇地打量着这辆车。他无疑就是盯我的梢的人之一。

===Original===
We went up Park Lane into the Edgeware Road, my instructions to Stagg being to make a circuit by Harrow and Brentford. Now that I was ensconced in my car I felt a trifle safer, and my tense nerves relaxed. I grew drowsy and allowed myself to sink into a half doze. The stolid back of Stagg filled my gaze, as it had filled it a fortnight ago on the western road, and I admired lazily the brick-red of his neck. He had been in the Guards, and a Boer bullet at Modder River had left a long scar at the nape of his neck, which gave to his hair the appearance of being badly cut. He had told me the story on Exmoor.

===Chinese===
我们沿公园巷（Park Lane）上行，驶入埃奇韦尔路（the Edgeware Road），我给斯塔格的吩咐是经由哈罗（Harrow）和布伦特福德（Brentford）兜一个圈子。此刻我安坐在车里，自觉稍稍安稳了些，紧绷的神经也松了下来。我犯起困来，任自己坠入半睡半醒之中。斯塔格那厚实的背影占满了我的视野，正如两礼拜前在西行路上占满它时一样，我懒懒地欣赏着他后颈那砖红色。他原是近卫旅出身，在莫德尔河（Modder River）吃过一颗布尔人的子弹，后颈上留下一道长长的疤，使他的头发看上去像是剪坏了似的。这故事是他在埃克斯穆尔（Exmoor）讲给我听的。

===Original===
Suddenly I rubbed my eyes. There was no scar there; the hair of the chauffeur grew regularly down to his coat-collar. The resemblance had been perfect, the voice was Stagg’s, but clearly it was not Stagg who now drove my car.

===Chinese===
我猛地揉了揉眼睛。那里根本没有疤；那司机的头发整整齐齐地长到外套领口。相似之处本已天衣无缝，声音也和斯塔格一模一样，可此刻开我这车的，显然并不是斯塔格。

===Original===
I pulled the blind down over the front window as if to shelter myself from the sun. Looking out I saw that we were some distance up the Edgeware Road, nearing the point where the Marylebone Road joins it. Now or never was my chance, for at the corner there is always a block in the traffic.

===Chinese===
我把前窗的遮阳帘拉下来，仿佛要遮一遮日头。朝外一望，我们已在埃奇韦尔路驶出老远，快到马里波恩路（Marylebone Road）与之交汇的路口了。此时不溜，更待何时，因为那个路口的车流向来堵成一团。

===Original===
The car slowed down in obedience to a policeman’s uplifted hand, and very gently I opened the door on the left side. Since the car was new it opened softly, and in two seconds I had stepped out, shut it again, and made a dive between a butcher’s cart and a motorbus for the sidewalk. I gave one glance back and saw the unconscious chauffeur still rigid at the wheel.

===Chinese===
车在警察举起的手势下放慢了速度，我便极其轻巧地推开左侧的车门。这车是新车，门开得悄无声息，两秒钟工夫我已跨了出去、把门带上，从一辆肉贩的板车和一辆公共汽车之间一头钻到了人行道上。我回头瞥了一眼，只见那毫无察觉的司机仍直挺挺地坐在方向盘前。

===Original===
I dodged unobtrusively through the crowd on the pavement, with my hand on my breast-pocket to see that my paper was still there. There was a little picture-shop near by to which I used to go occasionally, owned by a man who was an adept at cleaning and restoring. I had sent him customers and he was likely to prove a friend. So I dived into his doorway, which made a cool pit of shade after the glaring street, and found him, spectacles on nose, busy examining some dusty prints.

===Chinese===
我不引人注目地穿过人行道上的人群，一手按着胸前的口袋，确认文件还在。近处有一家小画作铺子，我从前偶尔去，店主是清洗修复旧画的好手。我给他介绍过主顾，他多半肯做个朋友。于是我钻进他的店门，那门洞在刺眼的街道之后成了一方凉爽的阴翳，只见他鼻梁上架着眼镜，正忙着端详几幅积尘的版画。

===Original===
He greeted me cordially and followed me into the back shop.

“Mr. Levison,” I said, “have you a back door?”

He looked at me in some surprise. “Why, yes; there is the door into the lane which runs from Edgeley Street into Connaught Mews.”

“Will you let me use it? There is a friend outside whom I wish to avoid. Such things happen, you know.”

===Chinese===
他热络地招呼我，跟着我走进后铺。

「莱维森（Levison）先生，」我说，「你这儿有后门吗？」

他略带惊讶地望着我。「哦，有啊；有一道门通向那条小巷，从埃奇利街（Edgeley Street）一直通到康诺特马厩巷（Connaught Mews）。」

「能让我借用一下吗？外头有位朋友，我想躲他一躲。这种事，你懂的。」

===Original===
He smiled comprehendingly. “Certainly, sir. Come this way,” and he led me through a dark passage hung with dingy Old Masters to a little yard filled with the debris of picture frames. There he unlocked a door in the wall and I found myself in a narrow alley. As I emerged I heard the bell of the shop-door ring. “If anyone inquires, you have not seen me here, remember,” I said, and Mr. Levison nodded. He was an artist in his small way and liked the scent of a mystery.

===Chinese===
他会意地一笑。「当然可以，先生。这边走。」他领我穿过一条阴暗的过道，两侧挂着霉旧的古典名作，来到一个堆满画框残骸的小院子。他在墙边打开一道门，我便置身于一条窄巷之中。我刚一现身，就听见店门的铃响了。「要是有人来问，你就说没在这儿见过我，记住了，」我说，莱维森先生点了点头。他好歹也算个艺术家，爱嗅几分神秘的气味。

===Original===
I ran down the lane and by various cross streets made my way into Bayswater. I believed that I had thrown my trackers for the moment off the scent, but I had got to get to the Embassy, and that neighbourhood was sure to be closely watched. I came out on the Bayswater Road pretty far west, and resolved to strike southeast across the Park. My reason was that the neighbourhood of Hyde Park Corner was at that time of day certain to be pretty well crowded, and I felt more security in a throng than in the empty streets of Kensington. Now that I come to think of it, it was a rash thing to do, for since Lumley knew the full extent of my knowledge, he was likely to deal more violently with me than with Chapman, and the seclusion of the Park offered him too good a chance.

===Chinese===
我顺着巷子跑下去，借着几条横街穿进了贝斯沃特（Bayswater）。我相信暂时已把追踪者甩开了气味，可我还得到大使馆去，而那一带定然被严密盯着。我在颇靠西的地方走上贝斯沃特路（Bayswater Road），决意向东南穿过公园。我的盘算是：海德公园角（Hyde Park Corner）那一带在那个时辰必定相当拥挤，我觉着人多处比肯辛顿（Kensington）那些空荡荡的街面更稳妥。如今回想起来，此举未免莽撞，因为拉姆利既已尽知我掌握的底细，对付我多半会比对付查普曼更狠下毒手，而公园里的幽僻正给了他再好不过的机会。

===Original===
I crossed the riding-track and struck over the open space where the Sunday demonstrations are held. There was nothing there but nurses and perambulators, children at play, and dogs being exercised. Presently I reached Grosvenor Gate, where on the little green chairs well-dressed people were taking the air. I recognised several acquaintances and stopped for a moment to talk to one of them. Then I emerged in Park Lane and walked down it to Hamilton Place.

===Chinese===
我跨过骑马道，穿过那片每逢周日举行集会的开阔地。那儿别无他人，只有保姆和婴儿车、玩耍的孩子，还有被人牵着遛的狗。不久我到了格罗夫纳门（Grosvenor Gate），那儿一排绿色小椅上坐着衣冠楚楚的人们在纳凉。我认出好几个熟人，还停下与其中一位寒暄了几句。随后我从公园巷走出来，顺着它走到汉密尔顿坊（Hamilton Place）。

===Original===
So far I thought I had not been followed, but now once more I had the indefinable but unerring sensation of being watched. I caught a man looking eagerly at me from the other side of the street, and it seemed to me that he made a sign to someone farther off. There was now less than a quarter of a mile between me and Belgrave Square, but I saw that it would be a hard course to cover.

===Chinese===
至此我以为自己没被跟上，可此刻我又一次涌起那种说不清道不明却绝不会有错的感觉——有人盯着我。我瞥见街对面一个男人正急切地打量我，似乎还朝远处某人打了个暗号。此时我与贝尔格雷夫广场之间已不足四分之一英里，可我看得出，这段路要硬闯过去并不容易。

===Original===
Once in Piccadilly there could be no doubt about my watchers. Lumley was doing the thing in style this time. Last night it had only been a trial trip, but now the whole energies of the Powerhouse were on the job. The place was filled with the usual mid-season crowd, and I had to take off my hat several times. Up in the bow-window of the Bachelors’ Club a young friend of mine was writing a letter and sipping a long drink with an air of profound boredom. I would have given much for his ennui, for my life at the moment was painfully exciting. I was alone in that great crowd, isolated and proscribed, and there was no help save in my own wits. If I spoke to a policeman he would think me drunk or mad, and yet I was on the edge of being made the victim of a far subtler crime than fell within the purview of the Metropolitan force.

===Chinese===
一进皮卡迪利，那些盯梢的就再无疑义了。这回拉姆利是摆开了大阵仗。昨夜不过是一次试手，如今动力室的全部气力都压到了这桩事上。街上满是旺季寻常的人潮，我一路上不得不好几次举帽致意。单身汉俱乐部（the Bachelors’ Club）那扇凸窗里，我的一位年轻朋友正写着信，啜着一大杯冷饮，一副百无聊赖的神气。我宁愿拿许多东西去换他那份倦怠，因为我眼下的日子刺激得叫人吃不消。我置身于那茫茫人海之中，却孤立无援、被人通缉，除了凭自家的机变之外再无外援。我若去跟一个警察搭话，他准会当我是个醉鬼或疯子；然而我正处于沦为某种远为精巧的罪行之牺牲品的边缘——那种罪行，可不是伦敦警察厅管得到的。

===Original===
Now I saw how thin is the protection of civilisation. An accident and a bogus ambulance﻿—a false charge and a bogus arrest﻿—there were a dozen ways of spiriting me out of this gay, bustling world. I foresaw that, if I delayed, my nerve would break, so I boldly set off across the road.

===Chinese===
这下我看清了，文明所提供的庇护竟是何等脆弱。一场事故配上一辆假救护车——一桩诬告配上一场假逮捕——要把我从这花团锦簇、熙来攘往的世界里神不知鬼不觉地弄走，办法多的是。我预见到，再拖下去我的胆气就要崩溃，于是索性硬着头皮向街对面冲过去。

===Original===
I jolly nearly shared the fate of Chapman. A car which seemed about to draw up at a club door suddenly swerved across the street, and I had to dash to an island to escape it. It was no occasion to hesitate, so, dodging a bus and missing a motor bicycle by a hair’s breadth, I rushed across the remaining distance and reached the railings of the Green Park.

===Chinese===
我险些就重蹈了查普曼的覆辙。一辆看似要在某家俱乐部门前停靠的汽车，突然猛打方向横穿街道，我只得冲向一处安全岛才躲过它。这时根本容不得犹豫，于是我闪过一辆公共汽车，与一辆摩托车擦肩而过、只差那么一根头发丝，一路冲过剩下的路途，扑到了绿园（Green Park）的栅栏边。

===Original===
Here there were fewer people, and several queer things began to happen. A little group of workmen with their tools were standing by the kerb, and they suddenly moved towards me. A pavement artist, who looked like a cripple, scrambled to his feet and moved in the same direction. There was a policeman at the corner, and I saw a well-dressed man go up to him, say something and nod in my direction, and the policeman too began to move towards me.

===Chinese===
这儿人少了些，便有几桩蹊跷事接连发生。一小队带着工具的工人站在路缘，忽然一齐朝我走来。一个看着像瘸子的人行道画师手脚并用地爬起身，朝同一个方向挪动。拐角处有个警察，我看见一个衣冠楚楚的男人走上前去，对他低语了几句、朝我这边点了点头，那警察也朝我走来。

===Original===
I did not await them. I took to my heels and ran for my life down Grosvenor Place.

===Chinese===
我不再等他们凑上来。我撒腿就跑，顺着格罗夫纳坊（Grosvenor Place）拼命逃命。

===Original===
Long ago at Eton I had won the school mile, and at Oxford I was a second string for the quarter. But never at Eton or at Oxford did I run as I ran then. It was blisteringly hot, but I did not feel it, for my hands were clammy and my heart felt like a cold stone. I do not know how the pursuit got on, for I did not think of it. I did not reflect what kind of spectacle I must afford running like a thief in a London thoroughfare on a June afternoon. I only knew that my enemies were around and behind me, and that in front, a few hundred yards away, lay safety.

===Chinese===
很久以前，在伊顿（Eton）我曾赢过全校一英里赛跑，在牛津（Oxford）也曾是四分之一英里项目的二号选手。可无论在伊顿还是牛津，我都不曾像那一刻那样跑过。天气热得像要把人烤起泡来，我却全然不觉，因为我的双手冰凉黏湿，心口像压着一块冷石。追逐进行到什么地步我全不知道，因为我根本没去想它。我也没去思量自己这般模样——六月的一个下午、在伦敦大街上像个贼似的狂奔——落在旁人眼里是何等光景。我只知道敌人围在我四周、追在我身后，而前方几百码开外，便是生路。

===Original===
But even as I ran I had the sense to think out my movements, and to realise that the front door of the Embassy was impossible. For one thing it would be watched, and for another, before the solemn footmen opened it, my pursuers would be upon me. My only hope was the back door.

===Chinese===
然而即便在奔跑之中，我仍留着心思盘算下一步，并意识到大使馆的正门是断断走不得的。一来那里必有人盯着，二来，等那班煞有介事的男仆把门打开，追我的人早扑上来了。我唯一的指望是后门。

===Original===
I twisted into the mews behind the north side of the Square, and as I turned I saw two men run up from the Square as if to cut me off. A whistle was blown and more men appeared﻿—one entering from the far end of the mews, one darting from a public-house door, and one sliding down a ladder from a stable-loft. This last was nearest me and tried to trip me, but I rejoice to say that a left-hander on the chin sent him sprawling on the cobbles. I remembered that the Embassy was the fifth house from the end, and feverishly I tried to count the houses by their backs. It is not so easy as it sounds, for the modern London householder studs his back premises with excrescences which seem to melt into his neighbour’s. In the end I had to make a guess at the door, which to my joy was unlocked. I rushed in and banged it behind me. I found myself in a stone passage, with on one side a door opening on a garage. There was a wooden staircase leading to an upper floor, and a glass door in front which opened into a large disused room full of boxes. Beyond were two doors, one of which was locked. The other abutted on a steep iron stairway which obviously led to the lower regions of the house.

===Chinese===
我一头拐进广场北侧后面的马厩巷，刚一转身，就看见两个人从广场那头跑上来，像是来截我的。一声哨响，又冒出好几个——一个从巷子远端进来，一个从酒馆门里窜出，还有一个顺着梯子从马厩阁楼滑下来。这最后一个离我最近，想绊倒我，可我很高兴地说，一记左拳砸在他下巴上，把他撂了个四仰八叉、趴在鹅卵石上。我记得大使馆是从巷尾数过来第五栋，便焦灼地凭着房子的背面去数门牌。这事不如听上去那么容易，因为伦敦现今的住户总爱在自家后屋东搭西建出一堆赘瘤般的突出物，看上去与邻居的混作一团。到末了我只好凭猜断定一道门，所幸那门没锁。我冲进去，把门砰地关上。我发现自己置身于一条石板过道，一侧有扇门通向一间车库。前方一扇玻璃门，开进一间堆满箱子的大弃屋。再往后是两道门，一道锁着，另一道接上一架陡峭的铁梯，显然通往宅子的下层。

===Original===
I ran down the stair﻿—it was no more than a ladder﻿—crossed a small courtyard, traversed a passage, and burst into the kitchen, where I confronted an astonished white-capped chef in the act of lifting a pot from the fire.

His face was red and wrathful, and I thought that he was going to fling the pot at my head. I had disturbed him in some delicate operation, and his artist’s pride was outraged.

===Chinese===
我顺着那楼梯——其实不过是一架梯子——跑下去，穿过一个小院子，经过一条过道，一头闯进厨房，迎面撞见一位惊愕不已、戴着白色高帽的主厨，他正要把一口锅从炉火上端起来。

他那张脸涨得通红、满是怒意，我以为他要把那口锅朝我脑袋砸过来。我打搅了他某桩精微的操作，伤了一位艺术家的体面。

===Original===
“Monsieur,” I stammered in French, “I seek your pardon for my intrusion. There were circumstances which compelled me to enter this house by the back premises. I am an acquaintance of His Excellency, your patron, and an old friend of Monsieur Felix. I beg you of your kindness to direct me to Monsieur Felix’s room, or to bid someone take me there.”

My abject apologies mollified him.

“It is a grave offence, monsieur,” he said, “an unparalleled offence, to enter my kitchen at this hour. I fear you have irremediably spoiled the new casserole dish that I was endeavouring to compose.”

===Chinese===
「先生，」我用法语结结巴巴地说，「冒昧闯入，恳请宽宥。实有不得已的情由，迫使我由这宅子的后门进来。我认识阁下——您的东家——又是费利克斯先生的旧友。万望先生开恩，指引我去费利克斯先生的房间，或者着人带我去见他。」

我这番低声下气的赔罪消了他的气。

「这是桩大罪，先生，」他说，「在这个时辰闯进我的厨房，罪无可赦。只怕您已无可挽回地糟蹋了我正用心调理的那道新式砂锅。」

===Original===
I was ready to go on my knees to the offended artist.

“It grieves me indeed to have interfered with so rare an art, which I have often admired at His Excellency’s table. But there is danger behind me and an urgent mission in front. Monsieur will forgive me? Necessity will, sometimes, overrule the finest sensibility.”

He bowed to me and I bowed to him, and my pardon was assured.

===Chinese===
我几乎要向这位受了冒犯的艺术家跪下了。

「打扰了如此罕见的一门艺术，我实在痛心——这门艺术，我在阁下的餐桌上已屡屡领教。然而我身后有凶险，身前有急务。先生能宽恕我么？情势所迫，有时也只能压过最精微的体面了。」

他向我鞠了一躬，我也向他鞠了一躬，我的罪过便算赦免了。

===Original===
Suddenly a door opened, another than that by which I had entered, and a man appeared whom I took to be a footman. He was struggling into his livery coat, but at the sight of me he dropped it. I thought I recognised the face as that of the man who had emerged from the public-house and tried to cut me off.

“ ’Ere, Mister Alphonse,” he cried, “ ’elp me to collar this man. The police are after ’im.”

“You forget, my friend,” I said, “that an Embassy is privileged ground which the police can’t enter. I desire to be taken before His Excellency.”

===Chinese===
忽然一扇门开了——不是我来时的那扇——走出来一个男人，我当他是个男仆。他正费力地往身上套仆役的号衣，一瞧见我便把它扔下了。我觉得这张脸很眼熟，正是从酒馆里窜出来要截我的那个人。

「喂，阿尔丰斯（Alphonse）先生，」他嚷道，「帮我把这家伙摁住。警察正追他呢。」

「你忘了，我的朋友，」我说，「大使馆是享有治外特权的地方，警察进不来。我要被带到阁下面前。」

===Original===
“So that’s yer game,” he shouted. “But two can play at that. ’Ere, give me an ’and, moosoo, and we’ll ’ave him in the street in a jiffy. There’s two ’undred of the best in our pockets if we ’ands ’im over to them as wants ’im.”

The cook looked puzzled and a little frightened.

“Will you allow them to outrage your kitchen﻿—an Embassy kitchen too﻿—without your consent?” I said.

“What have you done?” he asked in French.

“Only what your patron will approve,” I replied in the same tongue. “*Messieurs les assassins* have a grudge against me.”

===Chinese===
「原来你打的是这个算盘，」他吼道。「可这一手谁不会使。喂，搭把手，法国佬，咱俩把他弄到街上去，一眨眼的事。把他交给想要他的人，咱们口袋里就能落进两百枚上好的金镑。」

厨子面露困惑，又有些发慌。

「你肯由着他们糟蹋你的厨房么——这还是大使馆的厨房——而未经你应允？」我说。

「您做了什么？」他用法语问。

「无非是令东家会赞许的事罢了。」我用同样的话答他。「『*Messieurs les assassins*（诸位刺客先生）』跟我有过节。」

===Original===
He still hesitated, while the young footman advanced on me. He was fingering something in his trousers pocket which I did not like.

Now was the time when, as they say in America, I should have got busy with my gun; but alas! I had no gun. I feared supports for the enemy, for the footman at the first sight of me had run back the way he had come, and I had heard a low whistle.

===Chinese===
他仍犹豫不决，那年轻的男仆却朝我逼上来。他手指在裤兜里摩挲着一件什么东西，我很不喜欢那物件。

这会儿正是——按美国人的说法——我该掏出家伙大干一场的时候；可惜！我并无家伙。我担心敌人来了帮手，因为那男仆一瞧见我，便沿原路退了回去，我还听见一声低低的哨响。

===Original===
What might have happened I do not know, had not the god appeared from the machine in the person of Hewins, the butler.

“Hewins,” I said, “you know me. I have often dined here, and you know that I am a friend of Monsieur Felix. I am on my way to see him on an urgent matter, and for various reasons I had to enter by Monsieur Alphonse’s kitchen. Will you take me at once to Monsieur Felix?”

Hewins bowed, and on his imperturbable face there appeared no sign of surprise. “This way, sir,” was all he said.

===Chinese===
后来究竟会怎样，我不得而知，幸而在这节骨眼上，「机械降神」般地现出了大使馆管家休因斯（Hewins）其人。

「休因斯，」我说，「你认得我。我常来此赴宴，你也知道我是费利克斯先生的朋友。我有急事正要见他，因着种种缘故，不得不从阿尔丰斯先生的厨房进来。你这就带我去见费利克斯先生，可好？」

休因斯鞠了一躬，那张波澜不惊的脸上没有半点惊讶之色。「这边走，先生，」他只说了这么一句。

===Original===
As I followed him I saw the footman plucking nervously at the something in his trousers-pocket. Lumley’s agents apparently had not always the courage to follow his instructions to the letter, for I made no doubt that the order had been to take me alive or dead.

I found Felix alone, and flung myself into an armchair. “My dear chap,” I said, “take my advice and advise His Excellency to sack the red-haired footman.”

===Chinese===
我跟着他走时，瞥见那男仆正紧张地扯弄着裤兜里那件东西。拉姆利的爪牙看来并非总有胆量把他的吩咐贯彻到底，因为我毫不怀疑，那道命令本是要将我死的活的都拿下。

我见费利克斯独自一人，便一头瘫进一张扶手椅里。「老兄，」我说，「听我一句劝，劝阁下把那个红头发男仆辞了。」

===Original===
From that moment I date that sense of mastery over a situation which drives out fear. I had been living for weeks under a dark pall and suddenly the skies had lightened. I had found sanctuary. Whatever happened to me now the worst was past, for I had done my job.

Felix was looking at me curiously, for, jaded, scarlet, dishevelled, I was an odd figure for a London afternoon. “Things seem to have been marching fast with you,” he said.

===Chinese===
正是从那一刻起，我心里生出了那种驾驭全局之感——它能把恐惧驱散。数周以来我一直活在一重阴惨的黑幕之下，忽然间云开雾散。我寻得了避难所。此刻无论我自身再遭什么，最险的关头都已过去，因为我已把该做的做完了。

费利克斯好奇地打量着我，因为以我这疲惫、涨红、蓬头垢面的模样，出现在伦敦的一个下午，实在是个怪人。「你这边的事，看来进展得飞快啊，」他说。

===Original===
“They have, but I think the march is over. I want to ask several favours. First, here is a document which sets out certain facts. I shall ring up Macgillivray at Scotland Yard and ask him to come here at 9:30 this evening. When he comes I want you to give him this and ask him to read it at once. He will know how to act on it.”

Felix nodded. “And the next?”

“Give me a telegraph form. I want a wire sent at once by someone who can be trusted.” He handed me a form and I wrote out a telegram to Lumley at the Albany, saying that I proposed to call upon him that evening at 8 sharp, and asking him to receive me.

“Next?” said Felix.

“Next and last, I want a room with a door which will lock, a hot bath, and something to eat about seven. I might be permitted to taste Monsieur Alphonse’s new casserole dish.”

===Chinese===
「是飞快，可我以为这番行军已到头了。我有几件事相求。头一件，这儿有份文书，开列着若干事实。我要给苏格兰场的麦克吉利夫雷打电话，请他今晚九点半到这儿来。他来时，我要你把这交给他，并请他立刻过目。他自然知道该据之如何行事。」

费利克斯点了点头。「下一件呢？」

「给我一张电报纸。我要立刻发一封电报，得托个靠得住的人去发。」他递给我一张表格，我便拟了一封发给拉姆利的电报，发往奥尔巴尼公寓（the Albany），说我拟于当晚八点整登门拜访，请他接见。

「还有呢？」费利克斯问。

「最后一桩，也是最后一件：我要一间门能上锁的屋子，一个热水澡，还有七点钟左右的一点吃的。也许可以容我尝一尝阿尔丰斯先生那道新式砂锅。」

===Original===
I rang up Macgillivray, reminded him of his promise, and told him what awaited him at 9:30. Then I had a wash, and afterwards at my leisure gave Felix a sketch of the day’s doings. I have never felt more completely at my ease, for whatever happened I was certain that I had spoiled Lumley’s game. He would know by now that I had reached the Embassy, and that any further attempts on my life and liberty were futile. My telegram would show him that I was prepared to offer terms, and I would certainly be permitted to reach the Albany unmolested. To the meeting with my adversary I looked forward without qualms, but with the most lively interest. I had my own theories about that distinguished criminal, and I hoped to bring them to the proof.

===Chinese===
我给麦克吉利夫雷打了电话，重提他的承诺，告诉他九点半有什么在等着他。然后我洗了一把，接着从容不迫地向费利克斯大略讲了一天的经过。我从未感到如此彻底的从容自在，因为无论事态如何，我确信自己已搅黄了拉姆利的全盘打算。他此刻必已知道我到了大使馆，再要对我不利性命和自由，都是徒劳。我的电报会让他明白，我准备跟他谈条件，而我必能毫发无伤地走到奥尔巴尼公寓。对于与这位对手的会面，我毫无惴惴之心，反倒怀着最强烈的好奇。对于这位显赫的罪犯，我自有我的一套揣度，我希望能把它们拿来验证一番。

===Original===
Just before seven I had a reply to my wire. Mr. Lumley said he would be delighted to see me. The telegram was directed to me at the Embassy, though I had put no address on the one I sent. Lumley of course knew all my movements. I could picture him sitting in his chair, like some Chief of Staff, receiving every few minutes the reports of his agents. All the same Napoleon had fought his Waterloo.

===Chinese===
将到七点时，我收到了电报的回音。拉姆利先生说他很乐意见我。回电是发到大使馆给我的，可我发出的那封上并未写地址。拉姆利自然对我的每一步行踪都了如指掌。我能想象他坐在自己的椅子里，活像某位总参谋长，每隔几分钟便接获一份爪牙的报告。话虽如此，拿破仑到底是打完他的滑铁卢了。

## VIII / VIII

### The Powerhouse / 动力室

===Original===
I left Belgrave Square about a quarter to eight and retraced my steps along the route which for me that afternoon had been so full of tremors. I was still being watched—a little observation told me that—but I would not be interfered with, provided my way lay in a certain direction. So completely without nervousness was I that at the top of Constitution Hill I struck into the Green Park and kept to the grass till I emerged into Piccadilly, opposite Devonshire House. A light wind had risen and the evening had grown pleasantly cool. I met several men I knew going out to dinner on foot and stopped to exchange greetings. From my clothes they thought I had just returned from a day in the country.

I reached the Albany as the clock was striking eight. Lumley’s rooms were on the first floor, and I was evidently expected, for the porter himself conducted me to them and waited by me till the door was opened by a manservant.

You know those rococo, late Georgian Albany rooms, large, square, clumsily corniced. Lumley’s was lined with books, which I saw at a glance were of a different type from those in his working library at his country house. This was the collection of a bibliophile, and in the light of the summer evening the rows of tall volumes in vellum and morocco lined the walls like some rich tapestry.

===Chinese===
我大约差一刻八点离开贝尔格雷夫广场（Belgrave Square），沿着那天下午令我心惊胆战的那条路折返。我仍在被监视——稍加留意便看得出来——可只要我的去向合乎某个方向，他们便不会来阻挠。我全无紧张之感，到了宪法山（Constitution Hill）顶上，便斜穿过绿园（Green Park），沿着草地一直走，直到在皮卡迪利（Piccadilly）德文郡大厦（Devonshire House）对面走出来。起了点微风，傍晚的空气凉爽宜人。我遇上几个熟人，他们正步行赴宴，便停下寒暄几句。看我的装束，他们都以为我刚从乡间消磨了一日回来。

时钟正敲八点时，我到了奥尔巴尼公寓（the Albany）。拉姆利的房间在二楼，显然他们已在等我，因为门房亲自领我上去，陪着我直等到一个男仆把门打开。

你知道奥尔巴尼公寓里那种洛可可式（rococo）、乔治时代晚期（late Georgian）的房间——阔大、方正、檐线粗笨。拉姆利的房间四壁全是书，我一眼便看出，这些与他乡间别墅那间实用书房里的藏书截然不同。这是一位藏书家（bibliophile）的收藏，在夏日傍晚的天光里，一排排犊皮纸（vellum）与摩洛哥皮（morocco）装帧的高大书卷沿墙而立，宛如一幅华美的挂毯。

===Original===
The valet retired and shut the door, and presently from a little inner chamber came his master. He was dressed for dinner and wore more than ever the air of the eminent diplomat. Again I had the old feeling of incredulity. It was the Lumley I had met two nights before at dinner, the friend of Viceroys and Cabinet Ministers. It was hard to connect him with Antioch Street or the red-haired footman with a pistol. Or with Tuke? Yes, I decided, Tuke fitted into the frame. Both were brains cut loose from the decencies that make life possible.

===Chinese===
贴身男仆退出去，带上了门，不一会儿，他的主人便从里头一间小内室走了出来。他穿好了赴宴的礼服，那副显赫外交家的气派比以往更甚。我又涌起那种难以置信的旧日感觉。这正是我两晚前在宴席上见到的那个拉姆利，是总督们（Viceroys）与内阁大臣（Cabinet Ministers）的朋友。要把他同安提阿街（Antioch Street）、同那个拿手枪的红发男仆联系起来，实在困难。那么同图克呢？对，我心想，图克倒是合得上这个框子。二者都是那种挣脱了使人世得以维系的体面之念的头脑。

===Original===
“Good evening, Mr. Leithen,” he said pleasantly. “As you have fixed the hour of eight, may I offer you dinner?”

“Thank you,” I replied, “but I have already dined. I have chosen an awkward time, but my business need not take long.”

“So,” he said. “I am always glad to see you at any hour.”

“And I prefer to see the master rather than the subordinates who have been infesting my life during the past week.”

===Chinese===
「晚上好，利思滕先生，」他愉快地说。「既然您把时间定在八点，可否赏光共进晚餐？」

「谢谢，」我答道，「但我已经用过了。我挑了个不便的时辰，不过我的事不必占用太久。」

「这样啊，」他说。「无论何时，我都乐意见到您。」

「而我宁愿见主人，总好过见过去这一周里纠缠着我日子的那些下属。」

===Original===
We both laughed. “I am afraid you have had some annoyance, Mr. Leithen,” he said. “But remember, I gave you fair warning.”

“True. And I have come to do the same kindness to you. That part of the game, at any rate, is over.”

“Over?” he queried, raising his eyebrows.

“Yes, over,” I said, and took out my watch. “Let us be quite frank with each other, Mr. Lumley. There is really very little time to waste. As you have doubtless read the paper which you stole from my friend this morning you know more or less the extent of my information.”

===Chinese===
我们俩都笑了。「恐怕您受了些烦扰，利思滕先生，」他说。「可请记得，我曾给过您明白的告诫。」

「不错。而我是来向您回报这份美意的。这场戏的这一幕，无论如何是收场了。」

「收场了？」他扬起眉毛，问道。

「是的，收场了，」我说着，掏出怀表。「我们彼此坦诚些吧，拉姆利先生。实在没有多少时间好虚掷了。今早您从我朋友那里偷走的那份文书，您想必已经读过，那我的情报到了何种地步，您多少也清楚。」

===Original===
“Let us have frankness by all means. Yes, I have read your paper. A very creditable piece of work, if I may say so. You will rise in your profession, Mr. Leithen. But surely you must realise that it carries you a very little way.”

“In a sense you are right. I am not in a position to reveal the full extent of your misdeeds. Of the Powerhouse and its doings I can only guess. But Pitt-Heron is on his way home, and he will be carefully safeguarded on that journey. Your creature, Saronov, has confessed. We shall know more very soon, and meantime I have clear evidence which implicates you in a conspiracy to murder.”

===Chinese===
「坦诚自然是好事。不错，我读过您那份文书了。可以这样说，这是一份相当见功力的东西。您会在您的本行里步步高升的，利思滕先生。不过您总该明白，它能带您走的路实在不远。」

「在某种意义上您说得对。您全部的罪行如何，我还说不上来。至于动力室（the Powerhouse）及其所作所为，我只能揣度。但皮特-赫伦正在归途之上，这一路上他会受到周密的护卫。您的那条走狗萨罗诺夫（Saronov），已经招供。我们很快便会知道得更多，而眼下，我手里有确凿的证据，将您牵涉进一桩谋杀阴谋。」

===Original===
He did not answer, but I wished I could see behind his tinted spectacles to the look in his eyes. I think he had not been quite prepared for the line I took.

“I need not tell you as a lawyer, Mr. Leithen,” he said at last, “that what seems good evidence on paper is often feeble enough in Court. You cannot suppose that I will tamely plead guilty to your charges. On the contrary, I will fight them with all the force that brains and money can give. You are an ingenious young man, but you are not the brightest jewel of the English Bar.”

“That also is true. I do not deny that some of my evidence may be weakened at the trial. It is even conceivable that you may be acquitted on some technical doubt. But you have forgotten one thing. From the day you leave the Court you will be a suspected man. The police of all Europe will be on your trail. You have been highly successful in the past, and why? Because you have been above suspicion, an honourable and distinguished gentleman, belonging to the best clubs, counting as your acquaintances the flower of our society. Now you will be a suspect, a man with a past, a centre of strange stories. I put it to you—how far are you likely to succeed under these conditions?”

He laughed.

===Chinese===
他没有答话，但我真希望能看透他那副有色眼镜，看清他眼中的神情。我想，他对我这一手棋，并无十分准备。

「利思滕先生，身为律师，我不必告诉您，」他终于开口道，「在纸面上看似过硬的证据，到了法庭上往往虚弱得很。您不会以为，我会乖乖地为您这些指控认罪吧。正相反，我要用脑力与金钱所能赋予的一切力量来迎战。您是个别出心裁的年轻人，可您还算不上英国律师界（the English Bar）最夺目的那颗明珠。」

「这一点也不错。我不否认，我的某些证据到了审判时可能被削弱。甚至可以设想，您会因某个技术性的疑点而获判无罪。但您忘了一件事。从您走出法庭的那一天起，您便成了一个受嫌疑的人。全欧洲的警察都会盯上您的行踪。您过去一帆风顺，所为何来？因为您一直置身于嫌疑之上，是一位体面而显赫的绅士，跻身于最好的俱乐部，把我国社会的精华引为相识。如今您将是一名嫌疑犯，一个带着不清白过往的人，一桩桩离奇传说的中心。我把话撂在这儿——在这样的境况下，您还能走多远？」

他笑了。

===Original===
“You have a talent for character drawing, my friend. What makes you think that I can work only if I live in the limelight of popularity?”

“The talent you mentioned,” I said. “As I read your character—and I think I am right—you are an artist in crime. You are not the common cutthroat who acts out of passion or greed. No, I think you are something subtler than that. You love power, hidden power. You flatter your vanity by despising mankind and making them your tools. You scorn the smattering of inaccuracies which passes for human knowledge, and I will not venture to say you are wrong. Therefore you use your brains to frustrate it. Unhappily the life of millions is built on that smattering, so you are a foe to society. But there would be no flavour in controlling subterranean things if you were yourself a mole working in the dark. To get the full flavour, the irony of it all, you must live in the light. I can imagine you laughing in your soul as you move about our world, praising it with your lips, patting it with your hands, and kicking its props away with your feet. I can see the charm of it. But it is over now.”

===Chinese===
「我的朋友，您有描绘人物的天赋。是什么让您以为，我只有活在众人的聚光灯下，才能有所作为？」

「您方才提到的那份天赋，」我说。「以我读您的品性——而且我想我并未看错——您是一个犯罪艺术家。您不是那种出于激情或贪欲而动手的寻常刺客。不，我想您远比这精微。您钟爱权力，钟爱那种隐而不露的权力。您以蔑视世人、驱使他们为工具，来餍足您的虚荣。您鄙夷那点被当作人类知识、实则错漏百出的一知半解，而我不敢妄言您错了。正因如此，您动用您的头脑去挫败它。不幸的是，千万人的性命正是建筑在这点一知半解之上，所以您是社会的死敌。然而，假如您自己也是一只在黑暗里掘洞的鼹鼠，那么操纵那些隐于地底的事物，便全无滋味可言。要尝尽其中况味、尽揽其中的反讽，您必须活在光天化日之下。我可以想象，您在我们的世界里走动时，灵魂深处正暗自发笑——您用嘴赞美它，用手拍抚它，却用脚踢倒它一根根的支柱。我看得出其中的妙处。但现在，这一切到头了。」

===Original===
“Over?” he asked.

“Over,” I repeated. “The end has come—the utter, final and absolute end.”

He made a sudden, odd, nervous movement, pushing his glasses close back upon his eyes.

“What about yourself?” he said hoarsely. “Do you think you can play against me without suffering desperate penalties?”

He was holding a cord in his hand with a knob on the end of it. He now touched a button in the knob and there came the faint sound of a bell.

===Chinese===
「到头了？」他问。

「到头了，」我重复道。「结局来了——彻底的、最终的、不容转圜的结局。」

他做了一个突如其来、古怪而紧张的动作，把眼镜死死推紧在眼上。

「那您自己呢？」他声音嘶哑地说。「您以为与我对弈，能不付出惨重的代价吗？」

他手里攥着一根绳子，绳头有个圆球。此刻他按了一下球上的按钮，传来一声极轻的铃声。

===Original===
The door was behind me and he was looking beyond me towards it. I was entirely at his mercy, but I never budged an inch. I do not know how I managed to keep calm, but I did it, and without much effort. I went on speaking, conscious that the door had opened and that someone was at my back.

“It is really quite useless trying to frighten me. I am safe, because I am dealing with an intelligent man and not with the ordinary half-witted criminal. You do not want my life in silly revenge. If you call in your men and strangle me between you what earthly good would it do you?”

He was looking beyond me and the passion—a sudden white-hot passion like an epilepsy—was dying out of his face.

“A mistake, James,” he said. “You can go.”

===Chinese===
门就在我身后，他越过我朝门看去。我完全任他宰割，可我纹丝未动。我不知道自己是怎么镇定下来的，但我做到了，而且并不怎么费劲。我继续说下去，心里清楚门已经开了，有人正立在我背后。

「想吓唬我是实在没用的。我很安全，因为与我打交道的是一个聪明人，而不是寻常那等蠢笨的罪犯。您不会为了愚蠢的报复而要我的命。倘若您唤进手下，合力把我掐死，对您又能有半点好处？」

他仍越过我望着，那一阵激情——一种骤然升腾、白热如癫痫般的激情——正从他脸上褪去。

「是个误会，詹姆斯（James），」他说。「你可以走了。」

===Original===
The door closed softly at my back.

“Yes. A mistake. I have a considerable admiration for you, Mr. Lumley, and should be sorry to be disappointed.”

He laughed quite like an ordinary mortal. “I am glad this affair is to be conducted on a basis of mutual respect. Now that the melodramatic overture is finished, let us get to the business.”

“By all means,” I said. “I promised to deal with you frankly. Well, let me put my last cards on the table. At half-past nine precisely the duplicate of that statement of mine which you annexed this morning will be handed to Scotland Yard. I may add that the authorities there know me, and are proceeding under my advice. When they read that statement they will act on it. You have therefore about one and a half, or say one and three-quarter hours to make up your mind. You can still secure your freedom, but it must be elsewhere than in England.”

===Chinese===
门在我身后轻轻关上了。

「不错，是误会。我对您颇为钦佩，拉姆利先生，实在不愿失望。」

他笑了起来，活像个寻常的凡夫俗子。「我很高兴，这件事能在互相尊重的基础上进行。如今这出情节剧的序曲既已奏完，我们便谈正事吧。」

「请便，」我说。「我答应过要坦诚相待。好，那就让我把最后几张牌摊在桌上。九点半整，我那份今早被您截去的文书的副本，就会送到苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）。我可以补充一句，那里的长官们都认得我，是依我的建言行事的。他们读到那份文书，便会据此动手。因此您大约还有一个半——或者一个又四分之三——小时来拿定主意。您仍能保住您的自由，但那自由必须在英国之外的地方。」

===Original===
He had risen to his feet, and was pacing up and down the room.

“Will you oblige me by telling me one thing,” he said. “If you believe me to be, as you say, a dangerous criminal, how do you reconcile it with your conscience to give me a chance of escape? It is your duty to bring me to justice.”

“I will tell you why,” I said. “I, too, have a weak joint in my armour. Yours is that you only succeed under the disguise of high respectability. That disguise, in any case, will be stripped from you. Mine is Pitt-Heron. I do not know how far he has entangled himself with you, but I know something of his weakness, and I don’t want his career ruined and his wife’s heart broken. He has learned his lesson, and will never mention you and your schemes to a mortal soul. Indeed, if I can help it, he will never know that anyone shares his secret. The price of the chance of escape I offer you is that Pitt-Heron’s past be buried forever.”

He did not answer. He had his arms folded, walking up and down the room, and suddenly seemed to have aged enormously. I had the impression that I was dealing with a very old man.

===Chinese===
他已站起身，在房间里来回踱步。

「劳您驾告诉我一件事，」他说。「您若果真相信我如所言是个危险的罪犯，又如何能在良心上，给我一条逃生的路？将我绳之以法，才是您的职责。」

「我告诉您缘由，」我说。「我也有一处铠甲上的薄弱环节。您那一处，在于您唯有披着显赫体面的伪装才能成事。这层伪装，无论如何都要从您身上剥下。而我这一处，是皮特-赫伦。我不知道他在您这头陷得有多深，但我多少知道他的弱点，我不愿他的前程毁于一旦，不愿他妻子的心为之破碎。他已得到了教训，此生绝不会向任何一个活人提起您和您的图谋。事实上，只要我能设法做到，他永远不会知道有人也共享着他的秘密。我给您这条生路，所要的代价是——皮特-赫伦的过往，从此永远埋葬。」

他没有答话。他双臂交抱，在屋里来回踱步，忽然之间仿佛苍老了许多。我竟有一种感觉，仿佛自己面对的是一位极老的人。

===Original===
“Mr. Leithen,” he said at last, “you are bold. You have a frankness which almost amounts to genius. You are wasted in your stupid profession, but your speculative powers are not equal to your other endowments, so you will probably remain in it, deterred by an illogical scruple from following your true bent. Your true métier, believe me, is what shallow people call crime. Speaking ‘without prejudice,’ as the idiot solicitors say, it would appear that we have both weak spots in our cases. Mine, you say, is that I can only work by using the conventions of what we agreed to call the Machine. There may be truth in that. Yours is that you have a friend who lacks your ironclad discretion. You offer a plan which saves both our weaknesses. By the way, what is it?”

===Chinese===
「利思滕先生，」他终于开口道，「您很大胆。您有一种近乎天才的坦诚。您在这门愚蠢的本行里是虚耗了，但您的思辨之力不及您的其他禀赋，所以您多半仍会留在其中，被一种不合逻辑的顾忌拦着，不去追随您真正的志向。说真的，您真正的本行（métier），正是浅薄之辈所谓的犯罪。『不抱偏见地讲』——套用那些蠢律师的说法——似乎我们两边的案子都各有软肋。您说我那一处，是我只能借助我们议定称之为『机器』（the Machine）的那套惯例才能行事。这话也许有几分道理。而您那一处，是您有一位缺乏您那般铁一般谨慎的朋友。您提出的方案，恰好保全了我们彼此的弱点。顺便一提，那方案是什么？」

===Original===
I looked at my watch again. “You have ample time to catch the night express to Paris.”

“And if not?”

“Then I am afraid there may be trouble with the police between ten and eleven o’clock.”

===Chinese===
我又看了一眼怀表。「您有充裕的时间赶上去巴黎的夜班快车。」

「要是不去呢？」

「那我恐怕，十点到十一点之间，会有警察来找您的麻烦。」

===Original===
“Which for all our sakes would be a pity. Do you know you interest me uncommonly, for you confirm the accuracy of my judgment. I have always had a notion that some day I should run across to my sorrow just such a man as you. A man of very great intellectual power I can deal with, for that kind of brain is usually combined with the sort of high-strung imagination on which I can work. The same with your over-imaginative man. Yes Pitt-Heron was of that type. Ordinary brains do not trouble me, for I puzzle them. Now you are a man of good average intelligence. Pray forgive the lukewarmness of the phrase; it is really a high compliment, for I am an austere critic. If you were that and no more you would not have succeeded. But you possess also a quite irrelevant gift of imagination. Not enough to upset your balance, but enough to do what your mere lawyer’s talent could never have done. You have achieved a feat which is given to few—you have partially understood me. Believe me, I rate you high. You are the kind of foursquare being bedded in the concrete of our civilisation, on whom I have always felt I might some day come to grief.… No, no, I am not trying to wheedle you. If I thought I could do that I should be sorry, for my discernment would have been at fault.”

===Chinese===
「为了我们双方的缘故，那未免可惜。您可知道，您让我格外感兴趣，因为您印证了我判断的准确。我一直有种预感，以为总有一天会遇见一个像您这样的人，让我栽个跟头。智力极为强大的人，我能应付，因为那种头脑通常伴随着一种高度紧张的想象，而我正可在这上面施展手段。想象力过于丰沛的人，也是如此。不错，皮特-赫伦便是那一类。寻常的头脑奈何不了我，因为我能把他们绕晕。而您呢，是个智力颇佳的中等之人。请原谅这评语听着平淡；这其实是很高的赞美了，因为我是个严苛的评判者。若您仅止于此、别无所长，您是断不能成功的。可您另有一种与正事全然无关的想象天赋。它还不足以打翻您的平衡，却足以做到您那点律师才分永远做不到的事。您成就了一件鲜有人能成就的壮举——您局部的、看懂了我。请相信我，我对您的评价很高。您正是那种四平八稳、深植于我们文明之混凝土中的人物，我始终觉得，自己也许有朝一日会栽在这样的人手里。……不，不，我并非想讨好您。我若以为能讨好得了您，我反而会难过，因为那就说明我的眼力出了差错。」

===Original===
“I warn you,” I said, “that you are wasting precious time.”

He laughed quite cheerfully.

“I believe you are really anxious about my interests,” he said. “That is a triumph indeed. Do you know, Mr. Leithen, it is a mere whimsy of fate that you are not my disciple. If we had met earlier and under other circumstances I should have captured you. It is because you have in you a capacity for discipleship that you have succeeded in your opposition.”

“I abominate you and all your works,” I said, “but I admire your courage.”

===Chinese===
「我提醒您，」我说，「您正在浪费宝贵的时间。」

他十分愉快地笑了。

「我看您是真心替我的利益着想了，」他说。「这真是一场凯旋。您可知道，利思滕先生，您不曾成为我的门徒，纯属命运的一时兴起。假若我们早些相遇、换个情境，我定会把您收伏。正因您骨子里有做门徒的资质，您才得以在与我作对时功成。」

「我痛恨您，也痛恨您一切的所作所为，」我说，「但我钦佩您的胆魄。」

===Original===
He shook his head gently.

“It is the wrong word. I am not courageous. To be brave means that you have conquered fear, but I have never had any fear to conquer. Believe me, Mr. Leithen, I am quite impervious to threats. You come to me tonight and hold a pistol to my head. You offer me two alternatives, both of which mean failure. But how do you know that I regard them as failure? I have had what they call a good run for my money. No man since Napoleon has tasted such power. I may be willing to end it. Age creeps on and power may grow burdensome. I have always sat loose from common ambitions and common affections. For all you know I may regard you as a benefactor.”

===Chinese===
他轻轻摇了摇头。

「这个词用错了。我并不勇敢。所谓勇敢，是说您已经战胜了恐惧，可我从来没有过任何恐惧需要去战胜。请相信我，利思滕先生，我对威胁完全无动于衷。您今夜来到我这里，拿手枪抵着我的脑袋。您给我两条出路，而两条都意味着失败。可您怎么知道，我把它们看作失败呢？人们所谓的不虚此行，我已尽得其乐。自拿破仑以降，再没有人尝过这般的权力。我也许正乐于就此了结。年岁渐长，权力也可能成为重负。我一向对寻常的野心与寻常的情爱看得很淡。在您看来，我说不定把您当作一位恩人呢。」

===Original===
All this talk looks futile when it is written down, but it was skilful enough, for it was taking every atom of exhilaration out of my victory. It was not idle brag. Every syllable rang true, as I knew in my bones. I felt myself in the presence of something enormously big, as if a small barbarian was desecrating the colossal Zeus of Pheidias with a coal hammer. But I also felt it inhuman, and I hated it and I clung to that hatred.

===Chinese===
这一番话写将下来，看着似是徒费唇舌，可它实在精明得很，因为它正从我这场胜利里，一星一点地抽走所有的快意。这不是无谓的吹嘘。每一个字都掷地有声，我打骨子里知道这一点。我感到自己正面对着某种极其庞大的东西，仿佛一个矮小的蛮人，正用一把煤锤亵渎着菲狄亚斯（Pheidias）那尊巨硕的宙斯神像。但我也感到它毫无人性，我憎恶它，而我把这份憎恶紧紧攥住不放。

===Original===
“You fear nothing and you believe nothing,” I said. “Man, you should never have been allowed to live.”

He raised a deprecating hand. “I am a sceptic about most things,” he said, “but, believe me, I have my own worship. I venerate the intellect of man. I believe in its undreamed-of possibilities, when it grows free like an oak in the forest and is not dwarfed in a flowerpot. From that allegiance I have never wavered. That is the God I have never forsworn.”

===Chinese===
「您无所畏惧，也一无所信，」我说。「老天，您这种人本不该被容许活在世上。」

他举起一只手，表示不必如此。「在大多数事情上，我是个怀疑者，」他说，「可是请相信，我有我自己的崇拜。我崇敬人的智识。我相信它那做梦也想不到的种种可能——当它像森林里的橡树一般自由生长，而不是被矮化在一只花盆里。这份忠诚，我从未动摇。这是我所从未背弃的神。」

===Original===
I took out my watch.

“Permit me again to remind you that time presses.”

“True,” he said smiling, “the continental express will not wait upon my confession. Your plan is certainly conceivable. There may be other and easier ways. I am not certain. I must think.… Perhaps it would be wiser if you left me now, Mr. Leithen. If I take your advice there will be various things to do.… In any case there will be much to do.…”

He led me to the door as if he were an ordinary host speeding an ordinary guest. I remember that on my way he pointed out a set of Aldines and called my attention to their beauty. He shook hands quite cordially and remarked on the fineness of the weather. That was the last I saw of this amazing man.

===Chinese===
我掏出怀表。

「请容我再次提醒您，时辰紧迫。」

「不错，」他微笑着说，「开往大陆的快车不会等我的告解。您的方案诚然是可行的。也许还有别的、更省事的路子。我还不确定。我得想一想。……利思滕先生，也许您现在先走，会比较妥当。我若采纳您的建议，便有种种事要办。……无论如何，都有许多事要办。……」

他把我送到门口，举止之间，仿佛一位寻常的主人礼送一位寻常的客人。我记得一路上，他还指给我看一套阿尔丁版本书（Aldines），要我留心它们的精美。他十分热忱地与我握手，又谈论了几句天气的晴好。这是我最后一次见到这位不可思议的人物。

===Original===
It was with profound relief that I found myself in Piccadilly in the wholesome company of my kind. I had carried myself boldly enough in the last hour, but I would not have gone through it again for a king’s ransom. Do you know what it is to deal with a pure intelligence, a brain stripped of every shred of humanity? It is like being in the company of a snake.

===Chinese===
重又置身皮卡迪利，置身于同类那令我安心的同伴之中，我才深深地松了一口气。过去这一小时里，我表现得够刚毅的了，可纵使拿国王的赎身之财来换，我也不愿再经历一遍。你可知道，同一个纯粹的智识、一具剥尽了每一丝人性的头脑打交道，是何滋味？那就像与一条蛇为伍。

===Original===
I drove to the club and telephoned to Macgillivray, asking him to take no notice of my statement till he heard from me in the morning. Then I went to the hospital to see Chapman.

That leader of the people was in a furious temper and he was scarcely to be appeased by my narrative of the day’s doings. Your Labour Member is the greatest of all sticklers for legality, and the outrage he had suffered that morning had grievously weakened his trust in public security. The Antioch Street business had seemed to him eminently right; if you once got mixed up in melodrama you had to expect such things. But for a Member of Parliament to be robbed in broad daylight next door to the House of Commons upset the foundations of his faith. There was little the matter with his body and the doctor promised that he would be allowed up next day, but his soul was a mass of bruises.

===Chinese===
我乘车去了俱乐部，给麦克吉利夫雷（Macgillivray）打了个电话，请他在次日清晨听我消息之前，暂且不必理会我那份文书。随后我便去医院看查普曼（Chapman）。

这位人民领袖正怒气冲天，我把这一天的事讲给他听，几乎安抚他不下来。大凡工党议员（Labour Member），都是最较真的守法之徒，而那天早晨他所受的暴行，已严重动摇了他对公共治安的信赖。安提阿街那一桩，在他看来本是天经地义；人一旦搅进了情节剧里，便得指望遇上这种事。可堂堂一名国会议员，竟在下议院（House of Commons）隔壁的光天化日之下遭人劫掠，这便动摇了他信仰的根基。他身上倒没受什么伤，医生答应次日便准他下床，可他的灵魂却是一大片青紫的瘀伤。

===Original===
It took me a lot of persuasion to get him to keep quiet. He wanted a public exposure of Lumley, a big trial, a general ferreting out of secret agents, the whole winding up with a speech in Parliament by himself on this last outrage of Capitalism. Gloomily he listened to my injunctions to silence. But he saw the reason of it and promised to hold his tongue out of loyalty to Tommy. I knew that Pitt-Heron’s secret was safe with him.

As I crossed Westminster Bridge on my way home the night express to the Continent rumbled over the river. I wondered if Lumley was on board or if he had taken one of the other ways of which he had spoken.

===Chinese===
我费了好一番口舌，才劝得他按捺不动。他要公开揭露拉姆利，要一场轰动的大审判，要把秘密爪牙一网打尽，最后还要由他本人在议会里就资本主义（Capitalism）这最近一桩暴行发表一场演说。他阴郁地听着我要他噤声的告诫。但他看清了其中的道理，并出于对汤米（Tommy）的忠诚，答应守口如瓶。我知道，皮特-赫伦的秘密交给他，是稳当的。

我归家途中走过威斯敏斯特桥（Westminster Bridge），开往大陆的夜班快车正从河面上隆隆驶过。我心里琢磨，不知拉姆利是否就在那列车上，还是走了他所说过的、别的哪条路。

## IX / IX

### Return of the Wild Geese / 野鹅归来

===Original===
I do not think I was surprised at the news I read in *The Times* next morning.

Mr. Andrew Lumley had died suddenly in the night of heart failure, and the newspapers woke up to the fact that we had been entertaining a great man unawares. There was an obituary in “leader” type of nearly two columns. He had been older than I thought—close on seventy—and *The Times* spoke of him as a man who might have done anything he pleased in public life, but had chosen to give to a small coterie of friends what was due to the country. I read of his wit and learning, his amazing connoisseurship, his social gifts, his personal charm. According to the writer, he was the finest type of cultivated amateur, a Beckford with more than a Beckford’s wealth and none of his folly. Large private charities were hinted at, and a hope was expressed that some part at least of his collections might come to the nation.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，我在《泰晤士报》（*The Times*）上读到那条消息，我想我并不感到意外。

安德鲁·拉姆利先生于夜间因心力衰竭猝然辞世，而各报这才如梦初醒，发觉我们竟在不知不觉中款待过一位伟人。报上有一篇近两栏长的「社论体」讣闻。他比我以为的还要年长——已近七十——而《泰晤士报》称他为这样一个人：本可在公共生活中随心所欲、成就任何事业，却偏要把本该奉献给国家的，给了寥寥一小圈子朋友。我读到他的机敏与博学、他那令人惊叹的鉴赏力、他的社交天赋和他的个人魅力。据这位作者所言，他是那种有教养的雅士中最完美的典范，是一位贝克福德（Beckford），财富更胜贝克福德，却毫无他的荒唐之处。报上暗示他做过大量不事声张的私人慈善，并表达了某种期望，盼他的收藏至少有一部分能归诸国家。

===Original===
The halfpenny papers said the same thing in their own way. One declared he reminded it of Atticus, another of Maecenas, another of Lord Houghton. There must have been a great run on biographical dictionaries in the various offices. Chapman’s own particular rag said that, although this kind of philanthropist was a dilettante and a back-number, yet Mr. Lumley was a good specimen of the class and had been a true friend to the poor. I thought Chapman would have a fit when he read this. After that he took in the *Morning Post*.

It was no business of mine to explode the myth. Indeed I couldn’t even if I had wanted to, for no one would have believed me unless I produced proofs, and these proofs were not to be made public. Besides I had an honest compunction. He had had, as he expressed it, a good run for his money, and I wanted the run to be properly rounded off.

===Chinese===
那些半便士的小报，则用它们自己的腔调说着同样的话。一家说他令其想起阿提库斯（Atticus），另一家说像梅塞纳斯（Maecenas），又一家说像霍顿勋爵（Lord Houghton）。各报馆里想必对传记词典掀起了一阵抢购。查普曼自己所青睐的那张小报写道：尽管这类慈善家不过是个附庸风雅的票友、一个过时人物，但拉姆利先生却是此辈中颇为出色的一位，并且一向是穷人的真朋友。我想查普曼读到这篇时，怕是要气得背过气去。打那以后，他改订了《晨邮报》（*Morning Post*）。

戳穿这个神话，并非我的分内之事。事实上，即便我想，我也做不到，因为除非我拿出证据，否则没人会相信我，而这些证据又是不容公开的。况且我还有一份实实在在的不忍。诚如他自己所言，他不虚此行，而我愿意让这一程好好收场。

===Original===
Three days later I went to the funeral. It was a wonderful occasion. Two eminent statesmen were among the pallbearers, Royalty was represented, and there were wreaths from learned societies and scores of notable people. It was a queer business to listen to that stately service which was never read over stranger dust. I was thinking all the time of the vast subterranean machine which he had controlled, and which now was so much old iron. I could dimly imagine what his death meant to the hosts who had worked blindly at his direction. He was a Napoleon who left no Marshals behind him. From the Powerhouse came no wreaths or newspaper tributes, but I knew that it had lost its power.…

===Chinese===
三天之后，我去参加了葬礼。那是一场极尽哀荣的盛会。扶柩者中有两位显赫的政治家，有王室代表出席，学会与数十位名流都献了花圈。听着一篇庄重的安魂仪文，诵读在一抔最不相称的尘土之上，实在是一桩荒诞的事。我自始至终想着他所操纵的那台庞大的地下机器，如今它已不过是一堆废铁。我隐约能想象，他的死，对那些盲目听命于他、为之奔走的大批爪牙意味着什么。他是一个没有留下任何元帅的拿破仑。动力室没有送来花圈，没有送来报章悼词，但我知道，它已失去了它的力量。……

===Original===
*De mortuis*, etc. My task was done, and it only remained to get Pitt-Heron home.

Of the three people in London besides myself who knew the story—Macgillivray, Chapman and Felix—the two last might be trusted to be silent, and Scotland Yard is not in the habit of publishing its information. Tommy, of course, must some time or other be told; it was his right; but I knew that Tommy would never breathe a word of it. I wanted Charles to believe that his secret died with Lumley, for otherwise I don’t think he would have ever come back to England.

The thing took some arranging, for we could not tell him directly about Lumley’s death without giving away the fact that we knew of the connection between the two. We had to approach it by a roundabout road. I got Felix to arrange to have the news telegraphed to and inserted by special order in a Russian paper which Charles could not avoid seeing.
